# Contributors

Soup is built by its community. Thank you to everyone who has contributed code,
tests, docs, and ideas. ❤️

This list is maintained by hand alongside the GitHub
[contributors graph](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/graphs/contributors).
Merged a PR and don't see yourself here? Open a PR adding your line — that counts too.

## Maintainer

- **Alpamys** ([@MakazhanAlpamys](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys)) — creator & lead maintainer

  - `soup data clean` — dataset repair in place of a hand-written script: control bytes stripped, whitespace normalised, empty and duplicate rows dropped, and a report of what changed. **The reason it merged without a fight is that the design was settled on [#766](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/766) before a line was written**, so review never turned into a redesign. Three decisions worth naming: it is **non-destructive by default** and needs `--force` to overwrite, because a cleaning tool that overwrites by default is one bad flag away from destroying someone's dataset; **unknown keys are preserved**, so a row carrying fields Soup does not model survives the round trip, where the easy version drops them and nobody notices until a training run is missing a column; and the CLI assertions go through an ANSI-strip helper, added when asked rather than argued about — the failure it avoids has turned this repo's CI red four separate times on Linux and macOS while passing on Windows. Verified here on the merged tree: 48 passed including #855's docs-completeness guard and #1057's repo-wide Click flag ratchet. Twelve commits are theirs; the maintainer's single commit on top pins the serialisation-equality check so a row reported as unchanged is byte-identical rather than merely re-serialising the same way ([#766](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/766), [#718](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/718))
  - `soup autopilot` asks for a missing `--model`, `--data` or `--goal` in a terminal, instead of a third setup command. The design was settled on the issue first, and the first version was parked as a draft until then. Prompted answers reuse the flags' validation, and scripts and CI still get the unchanged missing-option error with exit code 2. 8 new tests (5 fail on main), 11 of 11 guard mutations killed ([#1576](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1576), [#1577](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1577))

  - Cross-tokenizer speculative drafts: a small model that would make a fine draft was unusable unless it happened to share a vocabulary. Four rounds, and what each one actually produced was a **control that did not yet exist** — a `_FakeTarget` that genuinely implements the repetition penalty (without it the fix was indistinguishable from a no-op), a `_MAX_ALIGN_CHARS` test that is not itself a 300-second wall-clock guard, and for the skip path both the `(0,0)` fixture AND the control that an extendable prompt still counts, since `(0,0)` alone is satisfied by a function that always skips. Then a clean textual merge of `main` silently restored the original symptom — #409's contract landed underneath and the assisted arm ran twice, the second call discarding the first's result — and they fixed it plus pinned the wiring, which had passed 163 tests while being deletable, because every CLI-level patch was `lambda *a, **k` ([#417](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/417))

  - `training.seed` reached the SFT wrapper and nothing else — seventeen other task wrappers trained at HF's default 42 with no error, so replicates that differed only in the seed were the same run; the seed is now applied before the adapter is drawn, not only inside `Trainer` ([#381](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/381))
  - Under `use_fsdp2_compile`, every `checkpoint-*` kept `torch.compile`'s key prefix and resumed **silently** from a re-zeroed adapter — normalisation now runs as each checkpoint is written, ahead of anything that publishes it ([#380](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/380))
- **Amir Fathi** ([@AmirF194](https://github.com/AmirF194))
  - Repaired live flat-mode multipacking where the DataLoader ignored `training.batch_size` and capped each FFD bin at one sequence instead of the documented batch-sized token budget ([#562](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/562)).
  - Fixed transient non-finite metric handling by considering the newest logged value for each finite-state metric before artifact publication, with a regression mutation check ([#560](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/560)).
  - Honored `training.quantize_reward_model` in both PPO and reward-model training loaders, with explicit flag-off regression coverage ([#586](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/586)).
  - A streamed model's `named_parameters()` carried the wrapper's `.inner.` segment, so a name-keyed comparison against a resident model shared no names at all and a correctness gate reported `0/0` as a pass ([#384](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/384))
  - `training.stream_vram_override` — the layer-streaming pre-flight measured free VRAM with a device-level driver query, so it could not see a per-process cap and there was no way to make it simulate one ([#386](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/386))
  - The VRAM pre-flight never called its own calibration hook, so the guard against a stack whose loss path under-budgets by 12.5% sat inert with no caller ([#390](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/390))
  - `kl_control` re-wrote the same β on every hold step, so a non-acting run was not the no-op `log_only` claims to be; the mitigation log now carries `held` / `acted` / `released` as a field rather than as free text ([#414](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/414))
  - `training.use_cut_ce: true` was accepted and did nothing on every locally-produced checkpoint, because the architecture was matched against the model PATH rather than its config — the same defect v0.73.0 fixed for Liger. They found a second one on top of the reported bug: `phi-2` dispatched to the Phi-3 patcher, which patches `Phi3ForCausalLM` and therefore patched nothing, while `apply_cut_ce` returned True and the run printed "Cut Cross-Entropy enabled" — success reported for work not done. Told the accurate version was stronger than their own framing, they took it rather than defending the original ([#446](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/446))
  - `live_eval.load_model_and_tokenizer` had no quantization parameter, so every live-eval path loaded the base at full precision and an NF4-trained adapter was judged on a bf16 base it never saw. **The reason this is worth reading is what happened when it was blocked**: the parameter was threaded but no caller could reach it, and the CHANGELOG described five fixed commands — the exact "validated, documented, read by nothing" shape v0.73.3 is named for. Offered the choice of wiring it or splitting honestly, they split, and the entry now LEADS with "no live evaluation path sets it yet" and states the surviving user-visible defect in plain terms. They then closed two mutations that had survived, one of them the wire to `get_compute_dtype()` — the function v0.73.1 shipped for the T4 `including_emulation` trap, connected untested ([#461](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/461))
  - `build_optimizer_param_groups` skips the catch-all base group when every parameter matched an `lr_group` pattern, and that branch had no test. Their one test is the **sole guard on all three mutations I ran**, including the realistic `if base_bucket is not None:` regression — an empty list is not None, so an empty base group gets emitted. It also pins group ORDER, which nothing did before and which matters more than it looks: `optimizer.state_dict()["param_groups"]` is positional, so a silent reorder breaks optimizer-state resume. And it deliberately does NOT assert dict equality, so adding a key to the emitted groups will not redden it — the restraint that keeps a new test from becoming a guard that fires on correct code ([#469](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/469))
  - `downsample()`'s endpoint-pin branch was uncovered, and the gap was not the branch — it was that `test_long_capped` bounds the result with `<=`, so a regression in the **stride arithmetic** sails through it. Their test is the only thing that catches a `+ 1` there: 14 of 15 pass under that mutation. It asserts the exact row list plus an **identity** check on the endpoint, which is the right instrument for a branch guarded by `is not`. They also accepted that the assertion incidentally pins today's stride formula — which contradicts `downsample`'s own docstring — and let it be filed as [#473](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/473) rather than widening the PR ([#470](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/470))
  - Two GPTQ export bugs that each made the artifact unusable: a `quantize(tokenizer)` fallback that could never work, and a shard filename the standard transformers loader cannot find. **They got a real auto-gptq install running to prove the second** — same weights, old name gives the issue's exact `OSError`, new name loads clean — which nobody here had done. Scope was honest without being asked: `Refs #338`, not `Closes`, leaving the packaging half (no wheel on 3.12, transformers pinned back to 4.52.4) explicitly open. On review they closed three gaps including the one that mattered most — two of their tests passed only where auto-gptq was **absent**, i.e. failed on exactly the machine the feature exists for ([#475](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/475))
  - Spotted that #339/#471 fixed the frozen-base load dtype in the SFT wrapper only, filed **#491 as an explicit follow-on crediting that work**, and covered the other twelve trainers — checking the premise rather than assuming it, by confirming all twelve apply PEFT unconditionally with no full-FT branch. They used `torch_dtype=`, not the `dtype=` spelling that had already shipped broken elsewhere, and put the resolver in `utils/gpu.py` so there would be **one** implementation rather than two. **Review found a real numerics regression their own fix introduced**: peft upcasts LoRA adapters to fp32 but never `ModulesToSaveWrapper`, so the SEQ_CLS reward head — the one parameter whose output *is* the reward signal — would have trained in pure bf16. Their fix is scoped to `requires_grad`, so the frozen base stays bf16 and the PR does not undo its own VRAM saving; and the test pins **both** halves, carrying an explicit `assert base_dtype == torch.bfloat16` control commented as proving the fixture reproduces the precondition rather than passing because everything is fp32 already ([#492](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/492))
  - Closed two never-worked-once export paths in one day. TensorRT shelled out to a `tensorrt_llm.commands.convert_checkpoint` entry point upstream no longer ships, dying mid-subprocess instead of at the flag; their guard checks the entry point right after the lazy availability probe — **no eager import added** — and the error names what is missing, where conversion moved, and how to install it. The review mutation proved the test enforces the ordering that matters: neutering the raise dies on `run was called ZERO times`, i.e. abort before any subprocess. The MCP half blocks restart capacity off a persisted `launching` row committed before `Popen()`, extending #402's predicate and #424's shared liveness primitive rather than adding a third copy — with a test pinning that reconcile-on-read deliberately leaves `launching` rows alone ([#508](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/508), [#506](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/506))
  - Wired live-evaluation quantization and fallback dtype through every generator path, recognized indexed CUDA devices, and documented the remaining evidence/staleness follow-up honestly ([#570](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/570)).
  - Traced a dependency floor to the exact upstream commit rather than guessing at it: `accelerate` 0.27.0 is the first release whose `save_fsdp_model` / `load_fsdp_model` accept `adapter_only`, which transformers 5.16.1 feature-detects to keep a PEFT run's FSDP resume checkpoint to the adapter — so the old `>=0.25.0` floor let pip resolve a version where the detection returns `{}` and the full frozen base is written into every checkpoint again. **The part worth copying is what they declined to claim.** Told the fix did not cover the issue's third acceptance criterion, they did not add a mocked round trip to close the box: they explained that Soup imports `transformers.Trainer` unmodified and the save/load happens inside accelerate, so a `Trainer.train()` test would mostly exercise upstream, and offered to build a real single-process gloo FSDP group if I wanted it at GPU-CI cost. I told them not to, and #352 stays open on that one item by agreement rather than by omission. They also corrected the framing on request, so the changelog says the bump *prevents a resolvable regression* rather than implying it fixed the measured 37 GB write — which the `transformers>=5.16.1` floor had already closed ([#352](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/352), [#591](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/591))
  - Closed the half of [#623](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/623) I had left open after [#647](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/647), and **picked it up from the issue thread rather than waiting for me to file it**. The RAM tier's unpinned construction was unprotected on every CPU-only CI cell: before #647, mutating `RamSource`'s `pin_memory=self.pinned` to `True` left **169 pre-existing streaming tests green**, and after #647 it was caught only behind a CUDA gate, so nine of nine cells were blind to it. Both hops now die with no GPU — I re-ran them to confirm. **What makes it reach the deeper hop is that it asserts the ALLOCATION** (`source.get(0, "weight").is_pinned() is False`) rather than the recorded `source.pinned` attribute; an attribute assertion passes that mutation unchanged, which is precisely how the gap survived. Drives the real, unmocked `_build_source` against a genuine safetensors shard on disk, and corrects the file docstring its own change makes false. They also named the structural limit instead of implying full coverage: the mirror "never pin" direction cannot be tested without a device, so it stays CUDA-gated ([#623](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/623), [#653](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/653)).
  - Closed a validation leak that was **total, not partial** at realistic ratios. Under `data.interleave` with `over` or `probs`, oversampling duplicated rows *before* `val_split` cut the validation set, so the same row landed in both halves: measured through the real `load_dataset`, **1000 of 1000 distinct val rows also appeared in train** on `[10000, 1000] over 0.1`, and again on `probs`. The fix carves validation out per source, before padding, on the local and hub paths alike, and the two are pinned independently — breaking only the local gate fails 4 tests while the hub test stays green. **The part worth copying is what the second review round produced.** The test named after the issue was VACUOUS: `not (train & val)` is trivially true against an empty val set, and its companion assertion was a *total*, which is invariant under never-splitting — so it passed on a build where validation did not happen at all. One added `assert val_texts` and it now fails alone under that mutation. They also folded in three findings that were not defects but consequences: a genuinely empty source no longer gets blamed on `val_split`, the runtime warning branches per strategy, and the fact that train and val now come from **different mixtures** once padding runs (50.0% source A in train against 90.9% in val) is documented rather than left to be discovered by someone oversampling to rebalance. Six mutations, six killed; zero test lines removed. Scope stated rather than implied: the streaming path still leaks and is filed as [#702](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/702) ([#680](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/680), [#701](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/701))
  - Two pull requests that together close the cross-tokenizer ULD path, and they have to be read as a pair. **[#704](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/704)**: `wasserstein` and `topk_align` forwarded the student's own `input_ids` straight to the teacher, clamped into its vocab range — and clamping does not translate tokens between vocabularies, so a mismatched pair trained on logits the teacher computed for *different text* while reporting a finite, plausible loss. Silently wrong, not a crash, and the clamp did nothing at all when the teacher vocabulary was larger. **[#711](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/711)**: the ULD loss ignored the response-only label mask and the causal shift the CE term already applied, so under the default `train_on_responses_only` it optimised prompt tokens too — measured, per-position gradient L1 at the prompt position goes from `0.343096` to **exactly 0.0**. **The fixture design in #711 is the part to copy.** A causal-shift test proves nothing unless shifted and unshifted give different numbers, and most do not; `labels=[[-100, 5, 2]]` is discriminating by construction, so the off-by-one mutation (`labels[:, 1:]` -> `labels[:, :-1]`) dies where it would normally survive. They also verified the loss numerically against an independently recomputed W1 rather than asserting it merely changed, and got right an asymmetry that would have been invisible: in `uld_aligned_loss` the teacher logits are deliberately *not* shifted, because they live in teacher token space and are aggregated onto student positions by `align`. Nine mutations across the two, none surviving; zero test lines removed. On review they took a stale `docs/commands.md` line that still called both strategies cross-tokenizer and would have walked a reader into the new error ([#681](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/681), [#704](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/704), [#682](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/682), [#711](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/711))
  - Wired `training.gradient_checkpointing` into MLX's `TrainingArgs`, which had been dropping it for mlx-lm's `False` default while the written `adapter_config.json` recorded the user's intent — the artifact agreeing with a setting the run never used. **The review round is the story.** The first submission passed 7 tests and the mutation that restores the bug also passed 7, because the kwarg and the metadata write read the same variable and one assertion covered both. They rebuilt it so the two fail **disjoint** sets, and then added a mutation I had not asked for that closes a gap I had missed: misspelling the kwarg as `grad_ckpt=` now fails 7 tests, where `_FakeTrainingArgs` doing `__dict__.update(kwargs)` would have swallowed the typo silently. They also checked the field name against the real mlx-lm 0.31.3 sdist rather than against their own fake, which is the only way a stub-based test can be trusted about an upstream API ([#685](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/685), [#698](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/698))
  - Fixed the `gspo` GRPO variant centring its importance log-ratio over **padding positions**: a plain `log_ratio.mean(dim=0)` ran across the whole tensor, so the control variate — and therefore every real token's loss and gradient — moved with values belonging to no completion. Measured, not argued: varying only a masked position's log-ratio across -2.0 / -6.0 / +50.0 moved the loss `-0.33205155 / -0.53440636 / -0.21428600` before and left it fixed at `-0.28571433` after, with the gradient at that position going from `0.0800301731` to exactly `0.0`. **The property that made it safe to ship is the one they checked without being asked**: with an all-ones mask the loss is BITWISE identical to before and to `mask=None`, so no user whose batches never pad sees a change, and the other six variants are unchanged to the last digit. Three mutations, three killed, including dropping the `.clamp(min=1.0)` that stops an entirely-masked column dividing by zero. **What I would point others at is the scope honesty**: [#723](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/723) reports two defects, they fixed one, said so in the body before being asked, titled the PR to the half they did, and marked it `Refs` rather than `Fixes` — so the issue stayed open for the sequence-level objective instead of being silently closed under a partial fix ([#723](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/723), [#735](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/735)).
  - Closed a blind spot nobody had noticed by pinning `soup doctor`'s accelerate floor to the one `pyproject.toml` declares. The two were independent, and the drift direction that matters is doctor reporting the LOWER number: it would then certify an environment where `save_fsdp_model(adapter_only=...)` does not exist, which is [#352](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/352)'s defect — a LoRA run under FSDP writing the full frozen base into every checkpoint. **The gap was real, not theoretical**: with `doctor.py` reverted and this test absent the existing suite passes, because the neighbouring floor test never reads `doctor.py` and `_RUNTIME_FLOORS` covers transformers/trl/peft but not accelerate. **The part worth copying is that it pins AGREEMENT rather than a constant**, so it fails when either side moves alone and stays green when a maintainer legitimately raises both — which is exactly the failure this repo shipped in #496, where a guard fired on the correct future state and went red on the very migration its exception existed to allow ([#753](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/753))
  - `deploy_to_canary` activated the trained adapter at 100% traffic and then wrote `canary_verdict: "OK"` into the iteration record on the strength of that activation POST's bare 2xx; `canary_router.route()` and `BucketStats` are never called outside tests, so **nothing evaluated a canary before that verdict was recorded** — and `soup loop replay` reads the field back as evidence. **The restraint is the reason this is the right patch.** The tempting version wires `route()` / `evaluate_canary_verdict()` / `maybe_rollback()` into the serving path and makes the "OK" true; they declined, and said why: the only server primitive today is a full-adapter hot-swap rather than a traffic split, so making that verdict real is a design change and not a bug fix. Reporting `canary_verdict: None`, matching every other exit in the function, is the change that is provably correct without deciding anything about the design. They also changed their own `Fixes #815` to `Refs #815` on being shown it met one of four acceptance criteria — which is why that issue's remaining three (a real 5% traffic-split test, a MAJOR-stats rollback test, and the docs still describing the router as "deterministic... and sticky-on-rollback" as though it were live) are still visible instead of buried behind a green checkmark ([#815](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/815), [#982](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/982))
  - `training.use_flash_attn` and `training.use_liger` were validated, documented, accepted — and read only by `sft.py::_setup_transformers`, with `soup autopilot` making it worse than inert by auto-enabling both for DPO/GRPO/pretrain on Ampere or newer, emitting a config Soup's own validation would then refuse. **The detail that decides this change is that the allowlist is `{sft, tts}` and not `{sft}`**: `TTSTrainerWrapper` inherits that same setup method, so a scope-by-task-name reading of the issue lands on `sft` alone and silently breaks every TTS config setting either flag. They found it by following the inheritance rather than the issue text. The `soup migrate --from axolotl` half is the other one worth reading, because the obvious resolution is the wrong one: an axolotl config with `rl: dpo` and `flash_attention: true` migrated to `use_flash_attn=True` and was then **refused at load**, and the tempting fix is to make the loader lenient — instead they stopped migrating a flag the target task never reads, which keeps the new rejection strict while leaving migrate's output loadable. `generate_config`'s flat decisions path got the same gate rather than being deleted, since v0.32.0 callers still use it. And they left an acceptance criterion open on purpose and said so: the `reasoning_effort`/`train_on_eot` note is a different function with a different consumer, and whether the TTS formatting path calls it as sft's text path does was unverified, so `Refs` rather than `Fixes` — the second time in one day they made that call themselves ([#806](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/806), [#983](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/983))
  - A LoRA on `lm_head` or `embed_tokens` under layer streaming crashed at the first forward on an untied checkpoint, because the streamed large layer substituted its pooled buffer under the wrong parameter name. Fixed, and bit-exact against the resident model (`torch.equal` on logits, loss and every gradient) under both peft 0.20 and 0.21. **The judgement worth recording:** review showed that saving and resuming such an adapter was still broken, and they did not bolt a redesign onto a test-fix round. They landed the forward fix and refused the unsupported save path at load, naming the module, then said plainly what was still open. They had also reproduced the exact 0.0 difference independently before tightening the test to `torch.equal`, and a `1e-7` perturbation later confirmed that tightening is what catches the error ([#1012](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1012), [#1019](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1019))
  - A LoRA adapter wrapping a streamed `lm_head` or `embed_tokens` had only its literal `weight` key redirected to the canonical weight; every other key under the same prefix still pointed at a module that lives on `meta`, so **a save wrote them from nothing and a resume loaded them into nothing**. The redirect now covers every key under the prefix, which matches what the streamed module's state actually contains rather than adding a second special case beside the first. **The restraint is worth naming**: the optional both-spellings refusal was declined with a reason — it is a separate behaviour, not a wider version of this one — and moved to the issue, so the PR stayed reviewable. Verified here on the merged tree under peft 0.20.0 and 0.21.0: round-trip equality with `torch.equal` over 10/10 and 12/12 keys on both, including the regex, tied and dotted-name cases; reverting to the literal key fails those tests ([#1048](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1048), [#1055](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1055))
  - Two tests for a defect that is **already unreachable**, delivered within the hour of being asked for — and the reason they are worth having is the second one. `TestRealPageLockFailureIsDrainedBeforeTheProbeRuns` drives the real `_build_source(pin=True)` with only `RamSource` mocked to fail its pinned allocation, so #901's `recover_from_failed_page_lock` -> `drain_stale_cuda_error` runs for real before the VRAM probe. `TestPositiveControlUndrainedErrorReadsAsOom` is the control: **an undrained stale `AcceleratorError` classifies as `oom=True`**, which is the misread users reported — a page-lock failure surfacing as an out-of-memory with gigabytes free. A regression test for an unreachable defect is decoration unless something proves the harness would catch it, and that is what the control does. They reached this by investigating and concluding **not reproducible, with the reason** rather than shipping a speculative patch. Verified here: 2 passed under peft 0.20.0 and 0.21.0, 68 across the neighbouring #901/#1002 suites, and neutralising the drain at `layer_stream_runtime.py:2818` fails the test — pinned to the mechanism, not the classifier ([#1003](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1003), [#1072](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1072))
  - `training.gradient_checkpointing` was decided by the trainer rather than by the config on every 4-bit path: `prepare_model_for_kbit_training` was called without the resolved value, so `false` could not turn checkpointing off and nine wrappers could drop a `true`. Threaded into every k-bit site on the text path, thirteen wrappers from `sft` through the preference and RL families. **The part worth copying is what they did when the first revision's test was too weak**: asked for behavioural coverage, they did not add more kwarg assertions — they drove the real `_setup_transformers` for sft, dpo and grpo, so forcing `use_gradient_checkpointing=True` at the SFT text site or the GRPO site fails its own parametrisation, and the count of tests that fail without the fix went from 2 of 16 to 9 of 20. They also **declared the scope they did not take**: the vision and audio k-bit sites are named as a follow-up and [#797](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/797) stays open for them rather than being quietly closed. Two reds on this branch were mine to explain and not their code -- [#382](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/382)'s Windows illegal instruction, and the Lambda SIGINT test that [#1073](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1073) repaired ([#797](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/797), [#1045](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1045))
  - `accuracy` and `verifiable/math` scored 0.0 on common MATH answer spellings: `\boxed{90}` against a `90^\circ` gold, `\boxed{\text{Paris}}`, `\boxed{\frac12}` against `\frac{1}{2}`, and an `x = 7` prefix that only the completion side read. They taught the shared normaliser all four rewrites. Then, over three review rounds, they kept the variable's name so `x = 3` never pays `y = 3` (in both rewards, including `math`'s tolerance branch). They reworked the compact `\frac` rule after review showed their first version broke two MATH-500 golds (`\frac 34`, `\frac 59`), refused a gold with nothing after `x =`, and added a growth test for each rewrite. **Each push came with its own negative controls, and every one matched what the review measured.** All 16 mutations of the final fix fail its tests (262 of the 3025 fail on `main` without it). Across 9,792 GSM8K and MATH-500 golds, 70 MATH-500 readings change and none is refused ([#1346](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1346), [#1371](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1371))
  - `soup sweep --config sweep.yaml`, as the docs wrote it, could not work: `--param` was required, `--config` is the base `soup.yaml`, and `load_sweep_yaml` / `SweepSpec` were complete and tested with no caller anywhere in the CLI. They added a dedicated `--sweep-config` flag instead of overloading `--config`, made `--param` optional with clear errors for both-or-neither, and documented the precedence (file `strategy` and `seed` over `--strategy`, non-zero `n_runs` over `--max-runs`, `n_runs` only caps). **The second round is the one to read**: they applied every review finding exactly (a malformed YAML file no longer ends in a traceback, `for_terminal` on the error line keeps `params[lr]` and `sw[lr].yaml` intact, the fragment credit, `strip_ansi` on three raw assertions) and replaced a seed test that any constant satisfied with one comparing two seeds, so replacing the file's seed with a constant now fails a test. `Refs` rather than a closing keyword, because the issue has other items. 9 new tests, all fail on clean `main`; 12 mutations, 10 killed ([#1465](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1465), [#1475](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1475))

- **Ben Younes** ([@ousamabenyounes](https://github.com/ousamabenyounes))
  - `MitigationLogWriter` dropped every record in silence once its parent directory vanished mid-run — the controller kept acting while its evidence stopped growing ([#398](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/398))

  - The `soup ship` MCQ scorer read `\boxed {A}` as no-answer — LaTeX permits a space before the brace and models emit it, and the cue tier cannot rescue it ([#396](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/396))
  - `--noise-floor` shipped without a config surface, so it was the one `soup ship` gate-policy flag that could not be committed to `soup.yaml`; the bounds import from `ship_verdict` so the schema and the CLI validator cannot disagree ([#410](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/410))
  - A dead MCP watcher left its run at `running` in the tracker forever — reconciled on read, with a Windows liveness branch because `os.kill(pid, 0)` there sends a console Ctrl+C rather than checking existence ([#407](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/407))
  - The one-active-execution cap lived in process memory, so a restarted MCP server could double-book it ([#408](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/408))
  - The `soup ship` leg-1 noise floor was measured in `--task-mode metric` only, so in the judge modes a win smaller than the instrument's resolution still counted; it is now measured everywhere and **labelled**, so a decode-only floor is distinguishable from one carrying judge variance ([#419](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/419))
  - `detect_disk_kind` could not see through virtio, so a 1.5 GB/s cloud disk was classified HDD and refused the streaming tier — and when review found that the *fix* cited a rate from module state the cache never reset, producing `'hdd' (measured 2.00 GB/s, under the 1.0 GB/s NVMe floor)`, they removed the global rather than clearing it on the cache branch: the rate now travels in a frozen classification that is stripped on override, so the message cannot cite a verdict it did not produce ([#411](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/411))
  - `training.bnb_4bit_use_double_quant` was validated and then read by nothing — every 4-bit path hardcoded `True`, so setting it changed the config fingerprint and nothing else. Made `Optional[bool] = None` rather than `True`, because a plain default emits the key into `model_dump()` and breaks round-tripping for 21 of 173 shipped configs; and when review showed the first round's tests fired on *spelling* rather than behaviour — and its companion passed against `main`'s untouched file — both were deleted rather than patched ([#418](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/418))
  - `soup env check` now audits the live environment against the bounds Soup declares about itself, so `pip install vllm` quietly downgrading `transformers` past the `<5.0.0` cap is caught. The bound is read from package metadata rather than restated — and when review found the false-positive fix had, in closing it, made #368's own case unreachable, they narrowed enforcement to the ABI-relevant packages so both properties hold at once ([#421](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/421))
  - `soup bom emit` / `soup attest emit --attach-to-registry` — a published `soup card` now carries its ML-BOM and in-toto attestation. Told the signed path registered the statement but not its detached `.sig`, so the card linked an attestation the registry alone could not verify, they fixed it the way they had already solved the same multi-file problem for `bom --format both` ([#420](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/420))
  - `soup draft measure` and `soup draft distill` disagreed on what "shares a tokenizer" means, so a pair distill refuses could still be measured — and a completed acceptance measurement was then **discarded** when the assisted-throughput arm failed, because the report was only written after it. The persist-early half is the one worth naming: before this it was indistinguishable from writing at the end, and every pre-existing test passed against a version that only wrote from the `except` handlers. Four rounds, and each round they came back with the mutation matrix rather than an assertion that it was fixed ([#409](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/409))
  - Layer streaming had no config key to disable pinning, so the only known workaround for a box that cannot page-lock enough memory was to not use the feature. `training.stream_pin` is `Optional[bool]` so unset reproduces today's behaviour exactly, and `true` **announces and proceeds** on the disk tier and on CPU rather than refusing an otherwise-fine config. The round that mattered was the third: their assertion on the `require_pin` wiring *looked* discriminating and was not — on CPU the expression is `False` whatever the config holds, so a constant satisfied it identically. They did not paper over that; they built the CUDA-gated version that distinguishes them and skips honestly on CI ([#416](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/416))
  - Extended LISA to `task: pretrain`, and did the dtype half the way the tree now demands: pretrain's load goes through the shared full-FT-aware resolver (`resolve_base_load_dtype`) rather than a local copy, so pretrain+LISA loads fp32 master weights exactly like SFT+LISA — the semantic conflict with #492 resolved by having one implementation, not two — and both trainers share one `apply_lisa_setup()` so their LISA wiring cannot drift ([#476](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/476))
  - Bounded the layer-streaming RAM tier by a **physical-RAM ceiling**, closing the [#622](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/622) exit-137 OOM where a store that fit `MemAvailable` still saturated unevictable host memory — the reporter's second run had 27 GB of 30 free and died identically to the first, which no free-RAM story explains. **What makes it right is what they refused to add**: an `os.statvfs("/dev/shm")` check was proposed and declined with a reason, because the store is `torch.empty(..., pin_memory=True)` and that mount is not the allocation channel — so a check against it is wrong in both directions. The bound they shipped is mechanism-agnostic and therefore correct under either accounting, and a competing implementation converged on the same one independently. They also documented the 55% constant as a **chosen safety margin, not a measured bound**, with the comment naming the hole it stands in for — a container's `--shm-size` can still be smaller. Seven mutations, seven killed ([#622](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/622), [#644](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/644)).
  - `soup ship --evidence` and the MCP `ship_evidence` tool each carried their own reader for the same file, and they had **already diverged once in a way that flipped a verdict**. One shared decoder now serves both, with a parity harness that fails on a DECISION-level divergence rather than on a message string — breaking parity on the MCP side alone reports `{'failed_rule': 'regression'}` against `{'failed_rule': None}`, i.e. DON'T-SHIP against SHIP, which is precisely the historical bug the refactor exists to prevent. Zero tests deleted: all 116 deletions are the two duplicated readers themselves. **They also refused one of the maintainer's own review requests and were right to** — the suggested `McpToolError(f"invalid evidence ({type(exc).__name__})")` would have broken `test_v07302.py::TestTheMcpEvidenceReaderHonoursTheFloor` and regressed v0.73.2, and they said so with the test that disproved it. The suggestion was withdrawn. A follow-up commit tightened the redaction regex found in review of this PR; the decoder and the harness are theirs ([#758](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/758), [#768](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/768))
  - Closed the half of the Web UI body-size problem that #915 could not reach: `/api/chat/send` and `/api/data/inspect` read and parsed an unbounded request body before any handler check ran. **The cap fires BEFORE the parse, and that ordering is proven rather than asserted** — the same malformed JSON returns 422 at 64 bytes and 413 when oversized, and 422 is what body parsing produces, so 413 on an identical payload can only mean the cap ran first. I could not defeat it: an honest oversized `Content-Length`, a chunked request with no `Content-Length`, a lying `Content-Length: 5` in front of 3 MiB, a malformed header, and a raw ASGI request carrying no such header at all all return 413, with an exact boundary at 1048576 admitted / 1048577 refused. Four mutations each die, including one that kills only the streamed-byte check and correctly leaves the header fast path intact. **The judgement call is the best part**: `/api/data/inspect` caps rather than reordering, because its path is a body field and the acceptance criterion as literally written was unsatisfiable for that route — they wrote down why instead of implementing something that pretends otherwise. They also volunteered to fold a competing PR's mechanism into theirs when told it would merge second ([#939](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/939), [#948](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/948))

Every catalog-wide invariant passes that mutation; this is the defect class that shipped `glm-5` pointing at `THUDM` instead of `zai-org` ([#422](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/422))
  - Added the `deepseek-v4-flash-grpo` recipe, carrying the same literal-repo-id guard a second time — the consistently-wrong-id mutation stays green through every cross-field invariant and is caught only by that test ([#432](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/432))
  - `materialize_meta_adapters` returns a count, and on newer peft it returns `0` as a matter of course — so `0` stopped distinguishing "nothing to do" from the silent no-training case its own docstring warns about. Demoted the count to a diagnostic and moved the decision into a separate postcondition, so the caller can no longer choose to ignore it; the trigger needs a peft this repo does not pin, so the test stubs the capability and asserts the decision ([#435](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/435))

  - A direct `soup export --format awq` let AutoAWQ silently download its own default calibration set — roughly 214k rows — so an export the user believed was local was network-dependent and calibrated on whatever upstream happened to ship. An explicit local JSONL is now required, and missing, empty or malformed input is refused **before** AutoAWQ is imported and before the model is loaded, so the failure costs seconds instead of a model load. The substance is theirs, in three commits. Three remaining review items — a width-fragile test rewrite, a stale "Unlike `--format awq`" line in the GPTQ refusal that this change itself made false, and the fragment move after v0.74.0 was tagged mid-review — were finished by the maintainer after a publicly announced date passed, and the merge commit names exactly which commits were whose ([#338](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/338), [#592](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/592))

  - Layer streaming built its buffer-pool spec from layer 0 and refused any checkpoint whose layers disagreed, which excludes an MoE decoder outright. This project's rule is that an arch enters the streaming allowlist only after a **bit-exact gate against a resident run**, and they could not load the real 35B resident; rather than argue for an exception they built a CPU parity gate on a toy heterogeneous MoE decoder, `torch.equal` on streamed-vs-resident logits, no CUDA marker, so it runs on every CI cell like the nine dense families. I confirmed it discriminates by silently streaming expert weights from layer 0 — same shapes, no crash — and it fails on the numbers. **They also named their own alias table `_UNVALIDATED_STREAM_ARCH_ALIASES` and wrote the missing-resident-control caveat into the docs before anyone asked**, which is why this was a review about closing a gap rather than about finding a concealed one ([#426](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/426))

Their parser fix strips ANSI first and collapses whitespace second, and **the two steps are pinned independently** — removing only the strip fails 3 tests, removing only the collapse fails 24, reverting both fails 25, which a single blunt assertion would not have distinguished. The width sweep is `range(20, 201)` rather than hand-picked values, and 21 of those 181 widths genuinely discriminate the fix. 345 tests pass across the wider surface and 345 again under `FORCE_COLOR=1` — the Rich per-character ANSI trap that has reddened this repo's CI four times. Nine mutations, no survivors, zero mocks: the display, tracker, console, tee and the harness module itself are all real objects driven through the real `main()`. They also disclosed, unprompted, that the Apple Silicon measurements are unchanged and a hardware run with the bridge enabled is still outstanding ([#23](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/23), [#703](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/703))
  - Published a **failed** research gate as a first-class record, which is harder than publishing a passing one. Their QuEST W4A4 SFT slice for #674 missed its own predeclared bar — 0.114 nat [0.099, 0.130] against a <= 0.1 gate on both the gap and its upper bound — and the record leads with that, states one seed / one model / fake quantization / not parity / not an integration / not an efficiency claim, and keeps every failed direction in. It binds 23 SHA-256 hashes to the retained scripts, records which evaluation panel was spent, leaves the reserved 256 examples untouched for a single final confirmation, and labels the one provenance gap (an uncaptured driver version) instead of filling it in. Every number matches their issue report exactly ([#674](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/674), [#856](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/856))
  - The mixed W4/A4+A16 QuEST route - the one that survived after their own strict W4A4 arm FAILED its gate, with that failed record left in `benchmarks/` byte for byte rather than quietly replaced. Three rounds, and every finding was about what the code REACHES: a quest run was reaching the int8 QAT path (proved fixed with call-recording spies on a real 24-block Llama, plus the control that the probe still detects int8 when it happens), `nvfp4` and `activation_offloading` had become silent no-ops beside it (now refused at parse, with every field the v028 path consumes enumerated), and two CLI lines were unpinned, one of which crashed any `[train]`-without-`[qat]` install. **The provenance discipline is the reason this merged**: the sidecar stamp is scoped `topology_selection_only` with `artifact_training_quality: false`, validated by schema rather than by byte-equality, so a later correction to the published record does not invalidate artifacts already on disk while genuine calibration drift is still refused ([#674](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/674), [#1105](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1105))
  - Closed both follow-ups from the #1105 merge review. The QuEST dry-run assertion now goes through the shared `strip_ansi` helper, with colour forced on the console that actually renders the panel; the runner's environment arrives after Rich has fixed its colour system, which is why the first attempt through `FORCE_COLOR` produced no escapes at all. **The test now asserts that an escape reaches the output, so it cannot go green on a runner that renders no colour**, and a separate control pins that the helper joins a label an escape splits. The precision sentence in the docs was rewritten to hold on both reachable paths: BF16 by default, FP16 when `training.auto_mixed_precision` picks it, calibration always BF16 ([#674](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/674), [#1159](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1159))
  - QuEST artifacts wrote the local base's absolute path into both the sidecar and `config.json["soup_quest"]`, so every artifact disclosed a directory layout and resume depended on a path string. They replaced it with a content identity over the files Transformers actually loads, and **three review rounds each ended with every requested item closed, including the ones marked optional.** A real `soup train` found that a tool-use template edited on a relocated base was accepted, and they bound `additional_chat_templates/*.jinja` by name and content. A mutation showed renaming a template went unnoticed, and they pinned it. The Windows and POSIX sort orders disagreed, so they sorted by file name. A legacy tokenizer layout escaped the fingerprint, so they refused it the way `auto_map` is refused. Of 29 mutations, 28 fail the suite, and the survivor can only differ on Python 3.13+ ([#1162](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1162), [#1176](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1176))
  - The QuEST route accepted `training.auto_mixed_precision`, which can switch its fake-quant arithmetic to FP16 from nothing more than a substring of the base name, a precision the #674 gate never measured. **It is now refused at config load, naming both fields**, while a QuEST config without the key still loads. They took the first refusal the same evening, counted the shipped configs affected (none), and pinned the guard so that moving or widening it turns a test red; 6 of 6 mutations fail a test ([#1245](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1245), [#1252](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1252))
  - Took both optional points from the #1252 review about twenty minutes after they were raised. With more than one invalid QuEST setting, the task, backend or modality error is now reported before the auto mixed-precision refusal, and the Auto Mixed-Precision guide links to the QuEST evidence boundary. **The test pins the guard's position, not just its presence**: moving it back to the top fails 3 tests, and moving it only partway fails 2 or 1; 5 of 5 mutations fail a test ([#1252](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1252), [#1276](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1276))
  - Continuing from a local QuEST artifact quietly recalibrated its activation clips on the first 32 new rows. They made the warm start restore the stored calibration under the artifact loader's rule (the `config.json` declaration must equal the sidecar) and added two checks of their own: the base must not change while loading, and a non-local artifact is refused. They published the best post-fit checkpoint as a record that leads with the still-failing gate (ratio 1.038367, gap +0.0785 nat [+0.0715, +0.0860]), keeps nine negative or inconclusive follow-ups, and ships a verifier that reproduces its numbers exactly. All three review items were closed within half an hour. The new tests fail on `main` (6 of 6), and every non-equivalent mutation of the continuation branch tried over two review rounds now fails a test ([#674](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/674), [#1624](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1624))
- **Achuth Reddy Bangaru** ([@AchuthReddy-16](https://github.com/AchuthReddy-16))
  - `soup train --no-reexec` printed a launch command with the user's own flags dropped, so following it trained without `--fsdp` while still succeeding. Rather than patch the printed copy, they deleted it and derived the hint from the argv that actually launches the run — then, asked for a guard, wrote one whose exclusion set forces a *decision* for every new `soup train` flag instead of letting silence make it ([#415](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/415))
  - A tokenizer output that is dict-LIKE but not a registered `Mapping` was read as a sequence, so its KEYS became tokens and the error named `input_ids[0]='input_ids'` — pointing the reader at their data when the cause was the type check. They went past the reported instance to the second, quieter one: the same gate at `_apply_template_with_mask` does not raise, it silently drops `assistant_masks` and trains unmasked, so widening only the loud gate would have left the worse half in place.   - Eleven callback modules imported transformers at module scope because a lazy-LOOKING factory was called EAGERLY — `class X(_try_import_callback_base())` resolves the base in the class header — costing ~4.4 s per import and a 5x `soup --help` regression the moment a light command touched one. Measured after: 4.30s -> 0.03s, torch absent from `sys.modules`. **The instructive half is the defect their first attempt introduced**: deferring the classes while the builders still referenced bare globals, which PEP 562 `__getattr__` does not serve, so four of five builders raised `NameError` on a cold import — and CI reported ONE failure, because eleven of twelve vanish in a full-suite run when an earlier file caches the attribute into `globals()`. Told that, they fixed all five, removed the ten `# noqa: F821` that were silencing the diagnostic, and wrote the subprocess test that is the only thing which catches it: under a re-break, 37 in-process tests pass and only that one fails ([#455](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/455))
  - Gave `--baseline` artifacts a `scorer_revision` + `soup_version` stamp, so a snapshot taken on an older Soup is no longer diffed against a freshly-scored model on a different scale in silence — and deleted the v0.73.2 name-list mitigation rather than letting it grow a sibling constant per release, which is what #404 asked for. **Two blockers, and the first is a lesson about locks**: their revision lock did not lock anything, because the fingerprint generator was a stub under which **six of eight** bundled suites scored exactly 0.0 or 1.0 — pinned, so unable to move. Reverting the #346 `arguments` requirement, the very scorer change #404 cites as motivation, left the hash byte-identical and the lock green. They rebuilt the generator so every suite lands strictly between 0 and 1 (0 of 8 pinned now), and **turned my mutation into a permanent test** — `test_removing_arguments_requirement_moves_fingerprint`, plus one asserting the generator's own non-degeneracy so it cannot quietly regress. Second, nothing in `src/` actually wrote a stamped file — both helpers were test-only while the docs already claimed otherwise — so they added `soup eval gate --write-baseline`, and a stamped baseline now resolves **silently** while an unstamped one warns exactly once, which was the entire point ([#485](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/485))

Two reviews disagreed on whether the wrong spelling raises or is silently ignored, so I installed transformers 4.46.1 against a locally-built bf16 checkpoint to settle it: `TypeError` at `__init__`, not a quiet no-op. Their static AST guard runs in **all thirteen cells**, not only the new floor job, so it does not depend on the floor cell being reached — reintroducing `dtype=` at one site turns it red by name. They also refused to relabel 4.55.4 as "the floor" and documented why 4.36.0 genuinely cannot resolve (4.46.0 is yanked in full; trl 0.14.0 requires >=4.46.0), both of which I verified against PyPI rather than accepted ([#489](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/489))

  - Every non-quantized model loaded in float32 because `from_pretrained` got no dtype, so a bf16 checkpoint cost 2x memory.   - Took `data.interleave` the rest of the way to streaming + hub entries (#459), and closed the review's MEDIUM SSRF finding with a correct diagnosis rather than a patch where it hurt: `_looks_like_remote_uri` had been conflating "is this meant to be remote" with "is the scheme allowlisted" by delegating to `is_remote_uri`, so https/http — not covered by `_REMOTE_SCHEMES` — fell through to local classification and reached `hf_load` with zero validation on the streaming path; the sniff is now a plain `"://"` check while the allowlist stays enforced exactly where it already lived in `validate_remote_uri`, and `is_remote_uri` itself is untouched ([#468](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/468))

It took two rounds on one principle: v1 asserted an exact site count and so **reddened the build on a correct edit** (a second, correctly-numbered mention; any sixth site), and relaxing that then silently gave up detecting a site dropped from `DOC_SITES` — a property the test's own docstring claimed. They closed both at once by pinning the roster's path AND pattern, and went past the ask with a test for the roster logic itself. Shown a measurement that a ratchet there would redden the build on more than half of all merges — 11 of the last 20 commits touch `tests/` — **they took the subtractive fix instead**: delete the numbers so drift becomes impossible rather than merely detectable. Then, told their first shape test asserted "no digits" and would fire on this file's own house style (`(v0.X.Y)` tags sit on every sibling line), they narrowed it to a count shape. Verified both directions: a version tag with a bare digit keeps the suite green, a real count fails by name. Two rounds, both of which they resolved by making the guard *smaller* ([#467](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/467))
  - Closed a guard that existed, was cited in its own docstring, and could never fire: `_telemetry_endpoint_is_safe` promised that `https://10.0.0.1/` could not reach an internal network, and accepted it — the HTTPS precondition sat upstream of a scheme-conditional private-IP check, so the rejection was structurally unreachable. **They improved on the fix I proposed rather than implementing it.** My sketch would have replaced the delegation outright; they pointed out it would lose CRLF, null-byte, type and `0.0.0.0` sanitisation, and — the part that mattered — that `_is_private_or_link_local("localhost")` returns `False`, because `ipaddress.ip_address` raises on domain names. A guard that blocks `127.0.0.1` and waves through its own hostname is the half-fix I would have merged. They asked which direction I wanted before writing any of it. The result layers a strict predicate on top of the existing sanitisation, leaves `hubs.py` alone because a private HTTPS endpoint is a legitimate self-hosted hub, and covers bracketed IPv6, uppercase, trailing-dot and IPv4-mapped forms — the last unasked for. 25 tests, every one asserting against the real function with no mocks, and they discriminate in both directions: deleting the strict layer fails 19 named tests, dropping only the loopback half fails exactly the five `localhost` tests, and making the validator reject everything fails the six acceptance tests, so a validator that is too strict would not have merged either ([#593](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/593), [#598](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/598))

Rather than patching around that they moved termination to a LOCAL controller that holds the key and never ships it, so `user_data` now carries no credential at all, and the `finally` both terminates and **polls to confirm**, forcing a non-zero exit if termination itself fails — more than [#264](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/264) asked for, which said only terminate in a `finally`.
