Payload pinned hardware-only by a test that fails when a field is added; all four historical IPv4 bypass spellings still rejected; measured **no** startup cost on the default path (1.355s -> 1.114s on `soup --help`, noise in their favour); six mutations, six killed ([#318](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/318), [#529](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/529))
  - Two hardening PRs on the same evening, and both got better under review rather than merely passing it. **[#705](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/705)**: `lora.r: 0` is how the rest of Soup spells "no adapter, train the base", and on `task: embedding` it reached `peft.LoraConfig` and surfaced a raw `ValueError`. **The interesting part is what the first version got wrong and how completely they fixed it.** It implemented delegation twice — explicit properties *and* a catch-all `__getattr__` — so three separate mutations survived, and the `__getattr__` made `attach_empty_param_group_guard` (#336/#359) return **True** and stamp its marker on the OUTER wrapper while `Trainer.train()` calls `create_optimizer` on the inner one: an honest decline turned into a false success. They deleted it, and then wrote the **inverse** mutation test — re-adding `__getattr__` now fails — which defends against the pattern returning rather than describing today's state. They also closed a subtler one I raised: `lora.r: 0` made `is_full_finetune` true while the loader still used the frozen-base dtype helper, which returns `float16` on pre-Ampere CUDA — exactly the combination v0.74.0's #425/#429 reasoning had excluded by assuming full-FT bases load at fp32. **[#707](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/707)**: fifteen Web UI read endpoints returned run configurations and live training logs with no token, on a product that ships `soup ui --public` and a scannable QR code. Their SSE design was right first time and better than what I would have asked for — a single-use 30-second ticket over an authenticated POST, never a `?token=` in a URL that lands in proxy logs and browser history. **Zero test functions deleted across 58 removed test lines**: the four tests named `*_no_auth_required` were *inverted* in place rather than removed, so the file keeps a guard where a future refactor would otherwise find a hole — and that choice is measurable, since the drop-auth mutation now fails 6 tests where it failed 4. They also removed, on request, a `--no-auth` flag their first version had invented: there was no way to disable auth on `main`, so it would have made a hardening PR the first supported way to run without any. Nine mutations across the two, none surviving ([#700](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/700), [#705](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/705), [#687](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/687), [#707](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/707))
  - Third merge of the same evening, and the one whose first round taught the most. The Web UI started training with `stdout=PIPE` and only the SSE endpoint ever read it, so with no browser subscribed the pipe filled at ~8 KiB and the training subprocess blocked on its next write — against a minimal run emitting 14.7 KiB, i.e. the ordinary case. Their daemon-thread ring buffer is the right mechanism and shipped correctly first time. **What did not was the evidence.** Three tests, all green, all reasonable — and the mutation restoring the defect exactly (`_train_drain_thread.start()` to a no-op) left all three passing, with the suite's wall clock dropping 4.5x because nothing was waiting on a thread that no longer ran. Every test had started the drain in its own body or assigned the buffer by hand, so the helper was covered and the *wiring* was not. They extracted `_resolve_train_argv` so the real `POST /api/train/start` path could be driven, and kept the three original tests rather than replacing them — they were good tests, just not the one the PR needed ([#688](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/688), [#706](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/706))
  - Replaced the `grpo_variant: gspo` kernel with the published Group Sequence Policy Optimization objective (arXiv:2507.18071), and **the finding worth keeping is that the issue and the PR describe different defects**. [#723](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/723) named a masked-token leak through batch centering — but that half had already been closed by [#735](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/735), and checking `main` directly shows masked gradients already at zero. What #735 left behind is the one this fixes: column centering zeroes the **policy** gradient on any column with a single unmasked member, so 2 of 9 unmasked positions in an ordinary fixture were learning nothing. Nine of the sixteen new tests fail on unmodified `main`, which is what separates a rewrite from a restatement. They also caught a dead path of their own making between their two commits — the first added `delta` handling to the gspo kernel while the schema still rejected `grpo_delta` for every variant but `two_sided`, making it unreachable from any real `soup.yaml` — and fixed the schema rather than leaving code nobody could call. Verified at merge by mutation on the exact head: dropping the length-normalization divisor and reducing over all tokens instead of unmasked ones each fail four tests by name, against an 88-passing baseline. The three lines removed from the pre-existing `test_v0500_part_a.py` are schema-forced, not bent — the delta-rejection test is retargeted to a variant that must still reject, with a positive case added beside it. The changelog fragment states plainly that existing `gspo` runs will not reproduce prior numerics ([#723](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/723), [#744](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/744))
  - `soup edit diff` accepted a probe file and a **single** model, then diffed a model against itself and reported "0 changed" as though that were a measurement — the failure shape where the tool looks like it worked. Both models are now required. The second half is the one that is easy to skip: probe rows whose prompt carried a null byte were **silently dropped**, so a truncated run and a complete one were indistinguishable; they are now counted and reported. Verified: replacing the `skipped_null_byte += 1` increment with a no-op fails `test_edit_diff_probes_with_null_byte_exits_2`, so the counter is pinned rather than incidental. They also took the optional ANSI hardening unprompted ([#818](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/818), [#863](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/863))
  - Closed the gap that made `soup eval quant-check` unusable as a CI gate: it printed a MAJOR verdict and still exited 0, so a pipeline built on it passed on the exact regression it exists to catch. On [#809](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/809)'s own repro — two empty directories — `main` exits 0 behind a green `OK 0.000 0.000 +0.000` while their branch exits 1 and names `--before` as the side that failed. **Six of their seven new tests fail on `origin/main`**, which is the property that separates a regression test from a test written around behaviour that already existed. Two rounds, and the second is the one worth recording: told that two of my mutations had survived — the stub banner could be deleted and the table still read as a live measurement — they did not add an assertion on the string, they made the stub a *labelled* result the caller must opt into with `--allow-stub`, so the banner became load-bearing rather than decorative. They also corrected me: I went in expecting the load-failure exit code to be wrong and it was not, and their PR description had it right. ([#917](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/917))
  - Distillation over a large vocabulary retained a `B * S * V * 2` activation on the autograd tape for the whole sequence, when in SFT/chat distillation most of those tokens are prompt and padding and masked out anyway. The fix filters student and teacher logits to the actively supervised tokens (`labels != -100`, else the attention mask) **before** the divergence is evaluated, then chunks what remains with optional non-reentrant checkpointing, so peak activation memory is bounded by the chunk rather than the sequence. **What this PR is worth keeping as, though, is the clearest example in the repo that two green matrices say nothing about their merge**: both branches were green separately, and composing this head onto `main` after [#943](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/943) raised `NameError: _distill_chunk_size` — a name existing on neither branch alone, with git reporting no conflict. They asked the maintainer to push the two-commit repair onto their own branch and then reviewed it before the merge rather than letting it land unexamined. They also diagnosed the red `type-check` as runner acquisition rather than mypy, which checked out: the job's `steps` array is empty and it burned 1h14m without executing a step — and they were careful to call it a flake while asking for a re-run rather than asking for the check to be waived. The harness weakness underneath is filed as [#990](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/990) rather than papered over ([#722](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/722), [#743](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/743))
  - `kernel_auto_compose: true` was already refused by the schema, yet a production benchmarking helper and two validator branches still existed behind it, unreachable. Removed, with the field and its refusal kept so existing configs still load. Verified both ways: removing the refusal fails 11 tests, and re-adding a stub of the helper fails the removal test. When a frozen CI merge ref reported a false red, they rebased and left the diff untouched rather than "fixing" tests that were only red because of an old `main` ([#976](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/976), [#1006](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1006))
  - `data.chat_template` was ignored by the PPO trainer and silently accepted by four tasks that have no trainer reading it. PPO now applies it, and those tasks refuse it by name. Review found that two of the four refusals were untested, because the test's match string could be satisfied by an unrelated validator. Their fix went past the literal ask: they tightened the match and also changed the fixtures so every config really reaches this gate. After that, removing any one of the four refusals fails exactly one test. The new PPO fallback warning escapes the exception text before printing it ([#936](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/936), [#1021](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1021))
  - Sixteen trainer wrappers each hand-built the `SoupTrainerCallback` kwargs, and the ones that had drifted silently dropped `loss_spike_recovery` and `grad_accum_auto_tune` — **validated, documented, and reaching no callback outside SFT**. All sixteen now go through one shared `soup_callback_kwargs`, and `prm` / `mole_routing` / `unlearn`, which attach no callback at all, are refused at parse rather than accepting the keys. **What decided this was the second round's test, not the first round's fix.** The first head pinned the wiring with a source-text scan, so reverting `trainer/grpo.py` to a hand-built dict changed nothing any test could see; `TestTrainerWrapperBehaviouralCallbackWiring` drives `GRPOTrainerWrapper.train()` and `DPOTrainerWrapper.train()` with the trainer mocked, captures `add_callback`, and asserts the flags on the callback that actually arrives — after which that mutation dies. They also kept `eval_gate_config=` literally in all sixteen wrappers and passed `include_eval_gate=False`, rather than taking the tidier route that would have broken fifteen existing tests. Verified here on the merged tree: the 15 previously-failing tests pass, 317 across the callback and auto-tuning suites, 332 across recipes and config, zero deleted test lines. The remaining leak is recorded rather than hidden — behavioural coverage is 2 of 16, so reverting `trainer/kto.py` alone still passes ([#802](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/802), [#1023](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1023))
  - A teacher producing non-finite logits let a distillation run **train silently to completion** — nothing learned from those positions, nothing said so. It now warns once, naming the teacher and three causes. **Two design choices are why this merged rather than being sent back.** First, "warn once" is enforced by *disarming* the tracker, not by counting warnings: after the first warning it never syncs again, so a diagnostic for an already-unhealthy run cannot itself become a per-step device-to-host sync — and the test that actually holds that property re-forbids `.item()` for ten further steps. Second, the #720 `NameError` was fixed by **removing the closure**, not by making it resolvable: the tracker is passed into `_DistillTrainer.__init__` and read with `getattr`, so the harness's hand-built globals dict is out of the picture and that test file is untouched. The `distill_mode: sequence` carve-out is correct and was checked rather than assumed — sequence-level KD is plain cross-entropy over teacher-generated text with no teacher forward to inspect. Verified here on the merged tree: 13 passed on the #720 and #887 files, 277 across all nine distill suites, and mutations that make the warning fire every interval or break the device counter both fail ([#887](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/887), [#1026](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1026))
  - The draft registry lock, the registry read and the audit-log append all opened paths that could be symlinks, so on Windows a planted link redirected the write to a file of the attacker's choosing. `open_no_follow` now **refuses** rather than reports: a pre-open `lstat` rejects `S_ISLNK` and, on `nt`, `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT`; a post-open `fstat` compares `(st_ino, st_dev)` against the pre-open `lstat` to catch a swap **during** the open; the fd is closed on every failure path. **The post-open block had no coverage at all on the first head** — disabling it changed nothing any test could see — and it is now pinned by a mutation that fails two tests. Their `test_v07133.py` change is a strengthening, not a relaxation: a blanket `skipif(os.name == 'nt')` becomes a capability probe with the assertion unchanged, so the file reports one fewer skip than `main` and the Windows path is genuinely exercised. Verified here: 183 passed, **zero skipped**, including the repo-wide path-containment ratchet ([#820](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/820), [#1024](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1024))
  - Gate commands used **five different exit-code conventions**, so the same number meant different things depending on which command produced it: a missing spec file exited 2 (= MAJOR) in `eval checklist` while a real regression exited 1 (= typo) in `eval gate`. Ten commands now share one taxonomy behind a `GateCommand` class. **The scoping is what makes it safe, and it took three rounds to get there**: the first revision mutated `UsageError.exit_code` globally, which would have moved every command in the CLI; the shipped version hooks `make_context` on the gate commands only, so a bad flag on `eval gate` exits 3 while a bad flag on `recipes list` still exits 2 — and it is pinned from BOTH sides, since re-globalising the override fails exactly the three non-gate tests. I measured all ten commands through `CliRunner` and again as real subprocesses rather than reading the table. They also **declared what they did not do**: acceptance item 3 of the issue (a checklist with no evidence should not render a neutral OK) is not implemented, is stated in the PR and the fragment, and is now pinned by two tests so the next PR has to re-litigate it deliberately. An earlier round's constants had landed on three lines below a live `raise`, i.e. unreachable; an AST walk over all 23 changed files now reports zero ([#813](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/813), [#921](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/921))
  - `training.nvfp4` and `training.fp8_attention` were validated, documented, accepted on `task: sft` — and applied by nothing, because the shared helper ran in eleven trainers and not in the default one. **What made this mergeable was adding the test that makes the ruling falsifiable.** The previous round wired the call but no test pinned a *refusal*, so no mutation of the standing FP8 ruling was even possible; now wrapping SFT's delegation in a warn-and-continue fails a named test, and I simulated both directions of the ruling against the real `_apply_quantization_aware` with a fake torchao and with `sys.modules["torchao"] = None` — an explicitly requested FP8 on an unsupported card stops either way. They also rebased over [#1044](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1044) without undoing it (the diff against `utils/fp8.py` is empty, so the hardware gate still runs before the torchao probe) and **strengthened** a coverage assertion rather than satisfying it: `assert has_helper or has_inline_features` became an unconditional `assert "apply_v028_speed_memory" in src`, which fails on `main` ([#800](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/800), [#1022](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1022))
  - The ANSI-assertion guard covered `--help` output only, so a raw CLI-output assertion could still redden the Linux and macOS cells; it now reaches those assertions too ([#1068](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1068), [#1092](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1092))
  - `loss_watchdog`, `loss_spike_recovery` and `grad_accum_auto_tune` were accepted on `backend: mlx` and read by nothing; they are now refused and reported by name, with reasons that are true ([#1069](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1069), [#1088](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1088))
  - The fast-LoRA single-projection pin did not check that the NF4 path builds a graph or keeps the input dtype; it now asserts both, so the two mutations that had survived review fail on CPU ([#1163](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1163), [#1173](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1173))
  - `soup doctor --config` gave vision and audio configs an all-clear while `train_on_responses_only` and `mask_history` were unread there; the backend-support registry is now keyed by modality and reports both, with text output unchanged ([#1156](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1156), [#1169](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1169))
  - `soup mcp serve` wrote a child's output through whatever `.soup/mcp-runs` resolved to. So a directory symlink or junction at `.soup` or `.soup/mcp-runs`, or a hard link planted at the log path, sent training output wherever it pointed. Execution now refuses all three before spawn, with a message that names the reason and no path, and nothing is created at the link target. **Three rounds, and each one closed what the last one found.** A hard-link refusal first placed in the shared `open_no_follow` silently cost the audit log and the draft registry their records, so they scoped it to the run log. A directory check copied inline left the shared helper out of reach of every test, so they made it one helper in `utils/paths.py`. The pre-`mkdir` check let a refused execution hold the one-execution slot until restart, so they moved the call inside the slot-freeing `try` and kept the regression test. On Windows and Linux every refused layout frees the slot and a fresh plan runs, and 19 of 20 mutations fail tests ([#1158](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1158), [#1171](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1171))
  - `training.packing: true` silently trained SFT on the prompt: TRL packs only `input_ids`, so the `labels` mask was discarded and system and user tokens became targets (234 supervised positions where the unpacked run had 81). They carried the mask through `assistant_masks`, the column TRL's packing keeps and its collator always applies, derived once for train and eval, text and pre-tokenised data. The review round added the missing validation-split test and removed a second derivation that had left half of the fix untestable. New tests 6 passed and 5 fail on main; 5 of 6 mutations caught ([#1236](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1236), [#1304](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1304))
  - `task: distill` trained on rows whose assistant turn was truncated away at `data.max_length`: cross-entropy over zero targets was NaN, which the Trainer logged as 0.0 or a doubled loss, and a validation row made `eval_loss` NaN. Setup now refuses such a row by split and row number with SFT's own check and wording, on train and val and in every distill variant including sequence mode, and `compute_loss` returns a finite zero for a microbatch with no target. 15 new tests (14 fail on `main`); removing the guard fails 13 of them, removing the loss fallback fails 1, and skipping the guard for sequence, ULD or MiniLLM configs fails exactly those four cases ([#1242](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1242), [#1298](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1298))
  - `soup expect` passed datasets with PII or refusals in any field it did not know by name: ShareGPT conversations, DPO `chosen`/`rejected`, KTO `completion`, embedding pairs, multimodal parts and tool calls. They made extraction format-aware and scanned each field separately, so PII after a long field is no longer cut off by the 50 KB cap. **Three rounds, and the second turned the extraction table from documentation into a test**: the first version declared fields in a table that nothing read, so the table could be emptied with the suite green, and now every declared field of every format must catch a PII sample. New tests 102 passed and 62 fail on main; 17 of 17 targeted mutations caught ([#1231](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1231), [#1300](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1300))
  - `soup diagnose` tokenized with an ASCII-only regex, so an adapter answering in Greek, Cyrillic, Hindi, CJK or Thai read as MAJOR mode collapse, and one that had forgotten everything still read OK on the forgetting probe. They rebuilt the tokenizer on Unicode categories, with NFC, bigrams for unspaced scripts and digits, and kept English `token_f1` scores identical to main. **Three rounds, and the second was about cost**: NFC on untrusted text was quadratic in a run of combining marks (15 s for 100k characters), so they added a stream-safe cap and pinned it with a Tibetan case that only a category-based filter survives. New tests 54 passed and 32 fail on main; 11 of 11 targeted mutations caught ([#1233](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1233), [#1301](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1301))
  - Contrastive embedding training at one row per batch trained nothing. The in-batch similarity matrix is 1x1, so the loss and gradient are exactly 0, and the shipped template's `batch_size: auto` picked 1 on CPU, MPS and GPUs under ~45 GiB because BGE names were sized as 7B. They refused batch 1 at load and at setup (including the triplet-to-contrastive fallback, by resolving the loss before sizing the batch), floored auto at 2, dropped the last partial batch, guarded the forward, and taught the size estimate BGE / E5 / GTE / BERT-family names. 12 of 13 mutations fail tests ([#1234](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1234), [#1296](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1296))
  - A KL-free GRPO run could not be configured, because `grpo_beta` was validated `> 0`. That is the DAPO / Dr. GRPO setting, and it became unreachable once every variant applied the KL term. They relaxed the bound to `>= 0` without letting `false` or any spelling of infinity through, refused `grpo_beta: 0` next to the controllers that steer beta, and added a real-trainer test in which every variant computes the KL-free loss itself, bit for bit, with no fallback to trl's loss. Verified on trl 0.29.1: no reference forward runs at beta 0, and 10 of 11 mutations fail tests ([#1247](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1247), [#1295](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1295))
  - SFT's audio setup never reached the FP8 converter, and layer streaming and the unsloth backend accepted `quantization_aware: fp8`, `fp8_attention` and `nvfp4` without applying them. They wired the audio path to convert after the LoRA attach, refused the other two paths at config load on every task, and declared the unsloth rows in `soup doctor`'s table. It took five rounds: a roundtrip test that had to be inverted rather than left red; a refusal that briefly swept in `use_cut_ce` / `activation_offloading`; the "after LoRA" order pinned by a wrapper-identity test; rebases over #1169, #1154 and #1333; and three tests re-pointed once #1275 made `true` unloadable. They also credited the two pieces adapted from @Srinivasan8888's branch. On the merged tree: 1658 passed, 0 failed across 28 files; 11 of 12 mutations caught ([#1124](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1124), [#1155](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1155))
  - DeepSeek-V4-Pro had only an SFT recipe. They added `deepseek-v4-pro-dpo` and `deepseek-v4-pro-grpo` in one PR, as #850 asked, with every value's source stated. The model block comes from the Pro SFT sibling and the task blocks from the DPO and MoE-GRPO conventions. Both surfaces (`RecipeMeta.model` and YAML `base:`) are pinned by tests. Along the way they fixed the MoE counts in the docs. After #1132 they redid the catalog count with the sync and snapshot scripts, so the five sites and the fixture agree at 174. 2266 passed across 11 recipe files; 17 of 20 mutations caught (the other 3 equivalent), and 5 of 5 re-run after the rebase ([#850](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/850), [#1297](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1297))
  - `soup migrate` dropped the quantization key for unquantized sources and wrote nothing for full fine-tunes, so axolotl, LLaMA-Factory and Unsloth configs silently became 4-bit LoRA runs on load. They made all three migrators write quantization explicitly: HQQ/EETQ mapped, Unsloth's own precedence and notebook variables resolved, and a full fine-tune written as `lora.r: 0` or refused by task name. `soup migrate` now validates before writing. The review rounds added a receiver-qualified exception to the #748 guard instead of deleting its entry, a LoRA fallback for `llama-adapter`, HQQ 5/6-bit, and source-order variable resolution. Six mutations that survived gained tests, and a clash with #1254's import landed a one-line fix. 275 passed, 0 failed across 9 migrate files; 16 of 18 mutations caught ([#1215](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1215), [#1302](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1302))
  - Every DPO, IPO and `preference` run that set `dpo_beta_schedule` or `dpo_ref_regen_epochs` crashed at step 0. Both callbacks were plain classes, and transformers' `CallbackHandler` calls every event on every callback with no `hasattr` guard. They rebuilt both on the lazily resolved `TrainerCallback`, with the PEP 562 pattern of `lisa.py`, so the module still imports no torch. They also fixed the third bare callback the issue named. Their real CPU DPO `train()` tests assert the β trl uses after the first optimizer step, not just that the run finishes. `dpo_ref_regen_epochs` could never regenerate the reference: with LoRA there is no separate reference model, and `lora.r: 0` fails in peft. It is now refused at load in every case until [#1345](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1345) wires it. 10 of 11 mutations fail tests ([#1229](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1229), [#1294](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1294))
  - `soup train --resume` on `task: prm` and `task: moe_lora_routing` announced the checkpoint, then retrained from step 0 and rewrote it: both wrappers dropped `resume_from_checkpoint`, and MoLE's periodic checkpoints held no trained gate. They forwarded it in both, saved `mole_gate.pt` into every `checkpoint-N`, restored it on resume with the base and task adapters re-frozen, refused resume on `task: unlearn`, and added a scan that fails when any trainer wrapper accepts the checkpoint without passing it on. **Four rounds, and the last one made two fixes pin each other**: every copy of the gate now goes through one fp32 save shared with #1312, and a recording test fails if a checkpoint copy skips it. 6 new tests, all failing on `main`; 13 of 13 mutations fail a test; on the final `main` the PR still merges clean and 2446 tests across 57 related files pass ([#1220](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1220), [#1303](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1303))
  - Wrote the recipe size ratchet for #1161: `glm-4.6-sft` was labelled 9B on a 357B base and nothing compared a recipe's label with its base, so they recorded the Hub's parameter count per base (anonymously, with a reason when there is none) and made the test compare every label inside 0.70x to 1.15x. **The part worth copying is how the first version failed and what replaced it**: its kill test did arithmetic on hard-coded constants and its check `continue`d when a base had no count, so putting the GLM-4.6 label back left every test green; the final version names any recipe whose base has no count unless it is excluded with a reason, and the kill tests run the real check over `dataclasses.replace()` of the live catalog. Dropping the `continue` is also what surfaced the labels nobody had listed: the four Llama 4 Scout recipes (17B active against 109B total, hidden behind eighteen "gated" exclusions because `model_info` answers without a token), `deepseek-v3-7b-sft` (7B on a 685B base) and `voxtral-sft`. Four rounds, rebased over #1132, #1112 and #1113; the last one was the test's own "an exclusion must still be used" check going red on the `spark-tts` removal. 203 tests in the directly affected files and 4284 passed / 1 skipped across 39 recipe-touching files on the merged tree; 12 of 13 mutations caught (the survivor is the offline record script's failure branch); regenerating the fixture changes nothing ([#1161](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1161), [#1168](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1168))
  - `soup mcp serve` planned a run by copying the config to `.soup/mcp-runs/<run_id>/config.yaml` through whatever `.soup` and `.soup/mcp-runs` resolved to, so a directory symlink or junction at either level redirected the plan-time write, and on `main` even a junction to a directory outside the working directory got directories created in it before a bare `ValueError` surfaced. They took the follow-up their own #1171 had left, calling the same `refuse_linked_dirs` helper before the `mkdir` and refusing the plan with a path-free error. The six tests cover a symlink and a real junction at both levels, each directly and through the `train_start` handler (5 of 6 fail on `main`); mutations of the call fail 5 of 6, checking `.soup` only fails the 2 `mcp-runs` tests, and dropping the helper's reparse-point branch fails the 2 junction tests ([#1211](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1211), [#1492](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1492))
  - Fixed `soup train` for the sequence-classification family (#1219): the loader kept source columns only for `grpo`, so `classifier` / `reranker` lost `label` and died after the model loaded, and `cross_encoder` pair rows could not be loaded at all. **Five review rounds, and the lesson was in the scoping.** The first version taught `detect_format` the pair shape globally, which made GSM8K-shaped `{question, answer}` rows train GRPO silently on empty prompts; they moved the resolution to the one task that reads pairs (`data_config_for_task`) and gated `data.format: cross_encoder` at config load. A real-run test pins the second load site, so the dry run and the real run agree. 13 mutations killed, 6 failed / 4 passed on `main` ([#1219](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1219), [#1306](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1306))
  - GRPO's `accuracy` and `math` rewards scored `The answer is 42 because 6*7=42.` as a hedge (0.0), because the answer clause only ended at punctuation and kept the justification's numbers. They ended the clause at `because` / `since` exactly as the ruling specified (any case, whitespace on both sides, never `as`), and over three rounds turned every review point into a test: the first round's own regression (a leading `Because` emptying the clause) came back fixed with its own test class, and the last two unpinned boundaries (`as` and a connective at the end of a truncated completion) were pinned verbatim. 176 tests in the touched files (19 fail on `main`), 3251 across the reward suites; 14 of 15 boundary mutations killed, the 15th equivalent ([#1328](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1328), [#1501](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1501))
  - `distill_mode: sequence` skipped rows with no prompt turn silently, then named the wrong row in its refusals (a position in the rebuilt teacher list, not in the user's file), and a split with nothing left crashed in the DataLoader at `train()`. They carried each row's source position through the rebuild, report skipped rows at setup and refuse an empty split before training. **What made it mergeable**: when review showed a user's own `_source_index` column could renumber a refusal, they redesigned it so Soup writes the index and only sequence mode trusts it, and pinned both call sites. 10 tests (8 fail on `main`), 286 across the distillation suites; 19 of 20 mutations killed, the 20th equivalent ([#1344](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1344), [#1502](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1502))
  - `soup expect` passed every preference dataset at any threshold: `expect_chosen_preferred_over_rejected_by_judge` scored every row 1.0 when no judge was given, and the command never gave one. They made a missing judge a violation per row, added `--judge` and `args.judge` (validated when the suite loads, CLI wins), built the judge from the existing URL rules with both-order pairwise judging, made `advisory: true` an explicit opt-in that the output names, and stopped at the first unreachable judge with exit 1 instead of scoring it as a quality failure. Three rounds: the second closed all six review items, the third was a clean rebase onto a docs change that landed in between; 438 tests passed and 8 of 9 mutations on the final head were killed, including both that survived the first round ([#1433](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1433), [#1505](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1505))
  - `soup train --cloud lambda` held one idle SSH session for up to 25 hours with no keepalive, and read `ssh`'s exit 255 (connection lost) as a failed run, so a Wi-Fi drop or a NAT timeout terminated the instance and lost the job. They replaced it with short keepalive polls every 30 s that report cloud-init's own state, a 15-minute outage window with capped backoff that a good poll resets, and a `scp` retry window that starts at the first failure. **The value is in what review made testable**: the first version read a missing exit file as "still running" (a 25-hour paid keep-alive), and the fix for that lived in a remote shell string no test executed. The final tests run that exact command under `sh` with a fake `cloud-init`, and pin the clock reset with two ten-minute outages around one good poll. 8 of 9 mutations are caught; the survivor changes only the poll interval. The new tests fail 17 of 21 on `main` ([#1439](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1439), [#1506](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1506))
  - `training.warmup_ratio` was turned into absolute warmup steps from the raw row count in all 15 trainer wrappers, so GRPO's warmup shrank by `num_generations` (to 0 on small sets) and packed SFT and pretrain warmed up for longer than the whole run, never reaching their peak learning rate. They routed every wrapper through one helper that hands the ratio to transformers, which applies it to the real optimizer steps. **What made it mergeable was what they removed**: a second commit had patched `TrainingArguments.get_warmup_steps` process-wide to hide a rounding difference, and on review they deleted the patch. They then adopted tests that run all 15 wrappers instead of skipping 12, fixed the three seed tests the honest rounding exposed, and wrote the one-step change into the changelog. On the merged tree the issue's own reproduction reaches the configured peak with packing (3 of 6 warmup steps, where `main` used 20 of 6), GRPO resolves 20/40, 20/40 and 2/16 (`main`: 5, 2, 0), and 329 tests pass; every wiring mutation tried across two rounds is caught ([#1431](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1431), [#1507](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1507))
  - `soup diagnose` measured catastrophic forgetting on the first 12 rows of `--dataset`, the adapter's own training data, so an adapter that had lost everything else still scored `OK 1.000`. They added `--holdout` and a built-in general-knowledge prompt set, kept `--dataset` for the probes that need training rows, and validate the holdout path before any model loads. **The rounds were about the cases that measured nothing**: an unusable holdout and a probe that raised became `NOT_RUN` instead of a neutral OK, and when review showed that #1489's `--dataset` fallback loop overwrote a measured forgetting score, they fixed that too. 2809 tests pass on the merged tree, the issue's own mutation fails 5 tests, every other mutation across three rounds is caught, and the new tests fail 12 of 52 on `main` ([#1436](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1436), [#1508](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1508))
  - Wired `dpo_ref_regen_epochs` under LoRA, the half that their own #1294 had to refuse. trl keeps the reference as a frozen `ref` adapter and leaves `trainer.ref_model` as `None`, so the callback never copied anything. Now every Nth epoch it copies `.default.` into `.ref.` under `no_grad` with the `materialize_meta_adapter_copy` name mapping. It warns when there is no ref adapter or no source, and full fine-tuning is still refused by name. The tests run real CPU `train()` over all four DPO-family task/loss combinations, and a 999-sentinel test is the only one that catches a reversed copy. When review found two lines no test could see (the copied-count guard and the re-freeze), they pinned each with its own test in one push. 80 tests pass, 17 of them fail on `main`, and 6 of 6 mutations are killed ([#1345](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1345), [#1636](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1636))

- **AmixDigital** ([@Amix29](https://github.com/Amix29))
  - Gave `soup monitor` a real Apple Silicon path, where it had reported nothing but an NVIDIA-shaped blank since v0.44.1. The security work is the part worth naming: fixed absolute executable paths so `PATH` cannot influence what runs, `sudo -n` that never prompts and never reads a password, stderr pattern-matched for permission markers but never printed, and a parser that returns `[]` against a billion-laughs entity bomb, an XXE `file:///` reference, 5,000-deep nesting and embedded NUL bytes. **Three guards had no test holding them up and they closed all three in twenty minutes** — most importantly the call site that decides which implementation every Linux and Windows user gets, where `if True or detect_apple_silicon()` had left 126 tests passing. Their oversize-payload test had also been asserting on `b"x" * (CAP + 1)`, which is not a valid plist and returns `[]` whether the cap exists or not; they rebuilt it around a valid oversized document. The test file shrank by 18 lines and **every deletion was an upgrade** — membership assertions became adjacency assertions. Asked to confirm the live plist schema, they said plainly that the Mac is not theirs and they cannot authenticate `sudo`, rather than guessing — while confirming the two things they could check against the real binary ([#481](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/481))
  - Wrapped Aider's Polyglot benchmark as `soup eval aider`, and **verified the upstream contract before writing a line** — the `aider-chat` wheel carries no benchmark at all (235 entries, zero matching `benchmark`, an `args.py` with 135 `add_argument` calls and no `--polyglot`), so they wrapped the real source/image harness instead of a flag that does not exist, and said so in the issue first. The container contract held up to everything I threw at it: `--rm`, a `readonly` corpus mount, no `docker.sock`, no `--privileged`, `shell=False`, and credentials inherited **by name** so no secret value reaches argv. **The blocker was a test that could not fail** — `pytest.raises(match="symlink")` searches the whole message, the message embeds the path, and pytest's tmp_path is named after the test function, so removing both symlink guards still left 16 tests passing while a *different* guard tripped the assertion. They fixed it with the isolating case I could not have asked for more precisely: a symlink whose target stays **inside** the result root, which is the one case `is_under` containment cannot catch. Also closed three hardening gaps in the same push — non-finite counts escaping as bare `ValueError`, `--image=--privileged` landing in the image argv slot, and an exercises directory resolving outside cwd — and validated the new image check in **both** directions, since a validator that rejects `ghcr.io/org/img:tag` is worse than the bug ([#482](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/482))
  - Caught that layer streaming's core promise — the frozen base "lives in CPU RAM" — was **false on Apple Silicon**: from PyTorch 2.7 on, `torch.empty(device="cpu", pin_memory=True)` hands back an `mps:0` tensor whose `is_pinned()` is false, so a 256 MiB store raised `torch.mps.current_allocated_memory()` by exactly 256 MiB. Their CHANGELOG entry says out loud that the shipped `soup train` path already gated pinning on CUDA, so the fix is the lower-level boundary rather than a user-visible bug — declining a bigger claim than the evidence supports. **All three guards I flagged had nothing holding them up**, including the MPS gate itself, where the only test was `skipif`-gated and therefore skipped everywhere but a Mac: disabling the gate entirely left 15 tests passing. Their replacement is platform-independent and **fails on a Windows box**, which is the difference between coverage and green-because-skipped. They also rebuilt the test that could not discriminate the real bug — it simulated the misplaced allocation with `device="meta"`, so a guard narrowed to `== "meta"` would have let the actual `mps` tensor through — and folded in the `require_pin` announcement so pinning is never dropped in silence ([#480](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/480))
  - Made vision SFT actually work end to end by moving chat templating from dataset-prep to **collation** time — the rendered text had been carrying zero image tokens while an image was still handed to the processor, because Idefics3 does not preserve a legacy `<image>` string. Verified against three real processors. **Review then found two defects the fix itself introduced, both of the kind that produce a plausible loss curve rather than an error**: 1088 of 1141 label positions per row — 95.4% — were `<image>` placeholders being trained on, and `add_special_tokens=False` silently dropped BOS for LLaVA-1.5, one of the two architectures #302 names as must-not-regress. One push closed both, and the test for the first pins the trained-label **count**, not just positions — a positions-only assertion would have passed on the broken version. Their BOS fix derives the flag per architecture by comparing both tokenizer paths, rather than hardcoding the value that happened to suit SmolVLM ([#488](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/488))
  - Ended the CHANGELOG conflict tax — every merge had been conflicting every open PR, because entries all went to the top of the same section, so the conflict rate tracked how many PRs were open rather than whether they overlapped. **They reproduced all three candidate options from one base commit before writing any implementation, and the reproduction disproved the maintainer's own recommendation**: appending at the *end* of the section still conflicts, and the `merge=union` driver silently fuses two different corrections to one entry into a duplicated bullet with contradictory text — a loud conflict traded for a quiet corruption. They also caught that my acceptance criteria asked them to edit `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, which is gitignored and unreachable from any public PR.   - Made the transformers floor **derived** instead of restated: #489 had written `4.46.1` into three places and derived it from none, so raising the pin in `pyproject.toml` left the suite green while CI went on testing the old version and calling it "the floor". **Review found their guard firing on a correct future state** — raising the declared floor to equal the tested pin, the exact migration the exception exists to allow, produced two message-less reds. They also made offline mode refuse `--resume` / `--checkpoint` outright instead of accepting recovery flags it cannot honour, and — asked for it on this PR rather than their own — put `--revision` into the online `run_digest` so a changed local revision is rejected *before* the model loads, asserted as `load_calls == []` ([#557](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/557)).

Export now checkpoints each completed group durably and a resume replays nothing — and the resume is **bound to what produced it**, including the exact file *content* of a local model, so weights swapped in place at the same path are refused before sampling rather than silently mixed into one artifact. Offline validation streams through a `mkdtemp`-unique SQLite index instead of materialising both files as Python lists; no live path calls a whole-file materialiser any more. **The part that made this safe to merge is that it did not quietly undo the previous one**: neutering the rollback loop fails four tests, three of them belonging to [#557]'s own suite already on `main`, which is what proves a 267-line rewrite of `best_of_n_artifact.py` plus a new 647-line transaction kept the manifest-last and stale-DPO-inside-the-transaction guarantees. Asked for one disclosure and delivered it precisely: the export phase now seeds per prompt rather than once globally — required for resume determinism, consistent with what the online phase already did, and a change to the output of any existing `--export-candidates --seed N` run ([#559](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/559))
  - Defined the AutoDistill Milestone A artifact contract, and the part worth copying is the boundary they drew themselves: the PR body states that this adds no task or `SoupConfig` field, no CLI, no teacher or student loading, no capture or training loop, no adaptive allocator, no cross-tokenizer ULD and no benchmark integration, so [#580](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/580) stays open on the rest rather than being closed by a slice of it. **The blocker was a failure mode they removed instead of working around.** The first revision committed exact-byte `.jsonl` fixtures and asserted a 423-byte length and a SHA-256 over them; windows-latest 3.10 and 3.12 went red while macOS stayed green on the same commits, which is a checkout-layer signature rather than a logic error — `core.autocrlf` rewriting a committed newline underneath a byte-level commitment. Offered the choice, they deleted the fixtures and now build the payload in the test as canonical JSON plus one explicit LF, so there is nothing left for a newline conversion to touch. Re-ran five mutations on the rebased head rather than the reviewed one, since a rebase is a new tree: disabling the `student_rollout` branch, the top-k-union-forced equality, mass conservation, `..` traversal and the student-rollout replay ban each fail a distinctly named test, where the first had zero execution before this round and is the teacher-only/student-only boundary the whole design turns on. Zero deleted lines in the entire PR ([#580](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/580), [#613](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/613))
  - Added Qwen4-Exp layer streaming with SSD-backed PLE n-gram embeddings, over **three rounds, two of which were about the same finding**: the gates existed and nothing proved they were reached. Deleting both gate call sites gave `40 passed`, byte-identical to baseline — a suite fully green with the feature's fail-closed policies removed. The design they landed on is the right one and worth copying: gate **bodies** pinned by behaviour tests, gate **call sites** pinned by a single test that monkeypatches all four with recorders, drives the real `_setup_streaming_transformers()`, and asserts the ORDER they are reached in. Deleting each call site alone, two together, and all four together each fails it by name. **The HIGH was worse than a missing test**: the RAM refusal keyed on `stream_source` rather than `stream_ngram_source`, so on the DEFAULT `stream_source: auto` a 102 GB PLE table loaded into RAM with no refusal at all; verified fixed by execution at 100 GB free rather than by reading. They also drew boundaries themselves — `loop_stages`-style, the oQ RAM policy extracted into its own resolver, `config.json` folded into the cache fingerprint so a re-quantised base rebuilds instead of silently streaming stale weights, and a gate record that labels its own stopped production step **not validated** rather than omitting it. One gap I closed myself after merge: the planner half of that fingerprint was unreachable from their harness, because the stub for `resolve_model_weights` ignored the `before_materialize` callback the fingerprint is taken in ([#602](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/602), [#603](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/603))
  - Delivered AutoDistill Milestone B1 — same-tokenizer teacher-only capture — and **the second round is the one worth recording**. The first head shipped seven load-bearing guards that no test could distinguish from their absence: eight mutations survived the suite, including the teacher-only boundary named in the PR's own title. All seven now die to one distinctly named test each, calling the real function with real input. Two things make that coverage load-bearing rather than decorative. The title guard is probed by **widening** it — accepting `student_rollout` alongside `teacher_expert`, which is the regression someone would actually write, not a deletion nobody would — and it fails exactly one test. And every guard was also mutated to reject **everything**, which takes down genuine happy-path tests (9 failures on the `trajectory_kind` guard alone), so none of the seven is the one-sided assertion that passes a reject-all implementation. The hash-chain test is the strongest shape available here: it publishes for real, tampers `manifest.complete.json` on disk, and re-publishes. They also closed the MEDIUM finding from the earlier round — teacher weights now hash through bounded 8 MiB streaming reads, pinned by a test that makes `Path.read_bytes` raise. Zero deleted test lines against `main` and zero between heads; no CLI surface, no schema field, no new dependency, and `Refs #580` rather than `Fixes`, because #580 is a proposal and not an approved design ([#580](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/580), [#629](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/629)).
  - Made the Web UI discoverable from the front door, and the blocking item is the one worth recording: the section would have promoted a command that **exits 1 on a default install**, because `commands/ui.py` imports fastapi and uvicorn from the `[ui]` extra and the README mentioned that extra **nowhere** — `grep 'ui]'` against the pre-merge README returned zero hits, against an issue body that said it was mentioned in the installation text. They added the install-and-launch block and the address held up to checking rather than to reading: `7860` and `127.0.0.1` are both real defaults, and the docs anchor lands on `## Web UI` and not on `## Web UI Plugin Registry + Env Knobs` 84 lines below it. The screenshot is the part that is not a given — `soup ui` prints a bearer token at startup and `--public` embeds it in a scannable URL, so a casually-taken screenshot of this app is a plausible way to publish a credential; theirs carries no token, no bearer URL and no local path. They also held a compact scope through three rounds and waited out a hold on #706 that was not their doing ([#728](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/728))
  - `soup autopilot` wrote training gates into the config it generates that **nothing reads**, so anyone following its output believed a gate was active when it was not. The repair worth naming is not the wiring but what replaced the first draft: a permanent name-collision trapdoor — a list of field names allowed to look unwired — became a **receipt**, so the generator records what it emitted and the guard checks that against the real consumer set. A future gate that genuinely goes unwired is caught rather than grandfathered ([#799](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/799), [#927](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/927))
  - `soup data recipe --execute` selected a generation provider silently, so a run could fall through to the offline `llm_text` placeholder and **report success with empty generations**. The mode is now explicit, and unknown-provider / provider-plus-offline / provider-with-no-endpoint are each refused by name. They also caught that the documented example recipe was missing the `prompt` config the `llm_text` node requires — the published example would not have run. Honest about the half it does not close: a provider that is selected and then *fails* still yields a silent empty result ([#817](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/817), [#922](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/922))
  - `data.chat_template` was honoured by some trainers and silently overwritten by a fallback in others, so **the same config produced a different prompt depending on the task**. What makes this entry worth reading is what happened in review: two assertions on *source order* were deleted and replaced with behavioural GRPO and Online-DPO tests, because an assertion that a statement precedes another catches the refactor that moves it, not the fallback that overwrites the user's override. The `stream_layers` clause also gained its reason inline, which is what stops the next reader deleting it ([#804](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/804), [#919](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/919))
  - `soup eval gate` recomputed the baseline instead of reading the eval results already stored for that run, so the gate could disagree with the numbers on record for the same model. Reading the stored results is what makes it a comparison rather than a second measurement ([#812](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/812), [#914](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/914))
  - `lora.rank_pattern` and `lora.alpha_pattern` were validated, documented, and honoured by a minority of trainer paths — everywhere else the per-module override was accepted and silently dropped, so the same `soup.yaml` produced a different adapter depending on which task you ran. PEFT `LoraConfig` construction is now one lazy shared builder that all 20 transformers paths route through, and DPO, GRPO, distillation, ASR, layer streaming, unlearning and the vision/audio SFT paths honour both patterns for the first time. **The AST guard is the half that makes it stay fixed**: a new trainer constructing `LoraConfig` directly now fails, so the hole cannot reopen one wrapper at a time, which is how it opened. Verified on the rebased head rather than the pre-rebase one — a rebase is exactly what quietly breaks this class — and handing the builder a deep copy with both patterns `None` fails the reward_model case on `{'q_proj': 16} != {'q_proj': 4}`, naming the trainer ([#803](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/803), [#923](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/923))
  - `soup data toxicity` matched standalone action words, so ordinary text scored as toxic — and that same unmeasured heuristic gated Magpie's default quality filter, silently dropping generated rows. **What makes this one shippable is what was removed, not what was added**: the reproduction-fitted phrase list was deleted rather than defended, the heuristic was taken out of Magpie's default filter entirely, and the documented recall was allowed to *understate* the tool rather than flatter it. A heuristic that claims less than it delivers is usable; one that claims more is worse than none. Backward compatibility was kept deliberately — command name, default output filename, legacy JSON field and MCP key all unchanged, with honestly named `abuse_keyword` aliases added alongside and delegation verified at runtime to return equal values ([#821](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/821), [#925](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/925))
  - `training.kernel_auto_compose` was validated, documented, and **read by nothing** ([#801](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/801)). They took the refuse-at-parse option rather than implementing it, which is consistent with v0.75.0 already making an unknown key a hard error — a key that is *known and inert* is worse than one that is unrecognised, because the user has a written promise that it does something. Blast radius measured rather than assumed: 0 of 171 recipes, 0 templates, 0 examples. And they filed the now-unreachable helpers as a separate issue instead of deleting them inside this PR — a tracked cleanup rather than a quiet one. ([#955](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/955))
  - RoPE scaling was applied **after** the model was constructed, so the instantiated rotary module and the reloaded `config.json` both carried the default rather than the configured scaling ([#793](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/793)). Their four mutations all discriminate on *behaviour* rather than source text — the assertions read the instantiated rotary module and the reloaded file, not the kwargs — and the ordering is atomic: the nested-`rope_parameters` refusal raises before `max_position_embeddings` is written. **Deleting the four `yarn_*` kwargs re-arms the consumer ratchet by name**, which is what proves their 40 -> 36 pin is a retirement and not a weakening. ([#931](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/931))
  - Shipped feature descriptions had drifted from the code — schema `Field` descriptions promising follow-up releases that had already landed, and one claim (`rl_checkpoint` persisting scheduler and RNG state) that was simply false ([#823](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/823)). The guard they built is **version-aware and fires in both directions on a real file**: an expired promise fails naming the field, read off the live `model_fields` rather than the source text, while a genuinely future notice stays silent — the half that matters, because a guard that fires on correct code gets deleted. Six of their factual claims were spot-checked by execution and all six held. When a rebase made their *own* sentence about plugin discovery false, they dropped it rather than force-keeping it. ([#945](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/945))
  - The Web UI's three YAML-accepting endpoints had **no request-size cap** — a v0.75.0 known limitation, since the 100-finding cap bounds the unknown-key walk and not the body ([#897](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/897)). What makes this one worth reading is the *shape*: they could have kept their own middleware and added a cap to it, and instead deleted it — `app.py` went from **85 lines of new mechanism to 14 of table**, folding the three routes into the `_BODY_SIZE_LIMITS` map an earlier PR already owned, so every body-taking POST route now sits in one place instead of two that must be kept in sync. The cap runs before the body is read (zero `receive()` calls on the Content-Length path), and chunked, lying-Content-Length, trailing-slash, case and double-slash variants were all checked for a bypass. **The second revision is why it merged:** the three rows were pinned from above but not from below, and silently dropping the limit to the neighbouring route's 8 KiB passed **306 tests** — their new test sends a body over 8 KiB but under 1 MiB and requires it to reach the parser, so that mutation now fails all three parametrisations. They also caught a stale recipe count in their own changelog prose, which no ratchet covers. ([#915](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/915))
  - `soup serve --auto-quant` claimed to measure quantization candidates before the serving engine existed. The pick was timer noise: 60 identical runs handed vLLM gptq 31 times, fp8 12, None 12 and awq 5. **They removed the picker instead of papering over it**, and placed the refusal before any backend branch, so a future backend cannot slip past it. The flag was opt-in, so nobody who did not ask for it is affected, and the mutation count in their description matched the one measured in review exactly ([#816](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/816), [#1035](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1035))

  - `_DistillTrainer` weighted every microbatch equally across an accumulation window, so a window whose microbatches carried different numbers of trained tokens produced a gradient that was **not** the full-batch gradient — short sequences over-weighted, long ones under-weighted, and the loss curve looks normal either way. It now consumes Transformers' full-window `num_items_in_batch` and scales each microbatch-mean loss by trained-token share, which is the contract rather than an approximation of it, superseding #742's fixed-per-microbatch compensation. The mutation is decisive: substituting `loss / gradient_accumulation_steps` mismatches **95.8%** and **94.4%** of gradient elements. **They also took a note I had explicitly left as their call** — asserting `model_accepts_loss_kwargs is True` turns the flag mutation from 5 failures into 6 and makes the one test that previously *passed* under it fail by name on a real assertion instead of surfacing as an opaque harness `TypeError`, which is the difference between detecting a regression and explaining it ([#932](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/932), [#943](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/943))

  - Third-party plugin entry points were discovered **eagerly**, so merely having a plugin installed imported its module — before you had enabled it, and on paths as innocuous as `soup --help`. Discovery is now lazy, third-party plugins default to **disabled**, and an explicit choice persists atomically in `~/.soup/plugins.json`. **The laziness is the security property, not a speed optimisation**, and they verified it rather than asserting it: a real `fakeplug-1.2.3.dist-info`, an isolated `USERPROFILE` so the state file starts absent, the marker deleted before each row and a fresh process per case, showing an installed-but-not-enabled plugin is never imported while an enabled one is. They also checked the recursive-state-file fixture for **headroom** — depth 5000 still raises at recursion limits of 1000/1500/2000/3000, three times the default — which is the difference between a test that works and a test known to keep working ([#819](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/819), [#935](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/935))

  - `soup reward stress` told the operator, in its own docstring and its terminal notice, that every attack is scored against the real gold "so numeric/tool_call/**json_schema** verifiers get a valid `answer=`". For `json_schema` that was false: the reference was passed under `answer` where the verifier reads `schema`, so it never arrived, and `reference_accept` — the positive control — could not tell a robust verifier from one that rejects everything. **What lifts this above a keyword fix is that they corrected the claim in all four places it was repeated** rather than only at the call site, and reframed `reference_accept` honestly as a self-acceptance control that means something only when references are valid completions, which a schema is not. The short-return path now raises and names the metadata key instead of returning a quiet false "robust" ([#937](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/937), [#941](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/941))
  - `selective`, `medium` and `auto` all ran **full** gradient checkpointing: three documented tiers, one behaviour, three names. They are now measurably distinct, and this was verified by counting module-forward invocations through a real forward+backward rather than by reading the plan dict — `full` 16 total forwards, `medium` 12 (whole blocks 0 and 2), `selective` 12 (attention only, MLP never), `false` 8. **The correctness check is the half that mattered**: gradients are bit-identical to no-checkpointing for every tier (`max|Δgrad| = 0.000e+00` across all 39 parameters) with `use_cache` both ways, so the `DynamicCache` double-append hazard the selective path invites does not materialise. They also replaced a source-grep test with two behavioural ones when asked — a test that greps for a call site cannot tell you the call did anything. One user-visible consequence is deliberate and now documented: a 4-bit `selective` run peaks **higher** than before, having stopped inheriting peft's full-block checkpointing for free ([#796](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/796), [#1036](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1036))
  - Wired PiSSA, OLoRA, LoftQ and VeRA into every trainer. All four were accepted by the schema and never reached the adapter; VeRA even built a plain LoRA. All 20 adapter construction sites now go through one builder. **Each strategy is proven by what it does, not by a config field**: through the real SFT setup on a tiny CPU Llama, pissa, olora and loftq each change the base weight, and VeRA trains 56 parameters against plain LoRA's 896. The conflict with #1043 ended in a bare `@model_validator` line on both sides, where a naive resolution silently drops a validator. Their resolution kept it, and they pinned the #1043 ordering (distill + 4bit + LoftQ) with a test rather than leaving it to validator order. They also retired five #748 allowlist entries in the same PR. Thirteen mutations, thirteen kills ([#794](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/794), [#1037](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1037))
  - `soup adapters audit` reported on nine settings and not on the learning rate, although MLX already recorded `peak_lr` — so a run whose optimizer used a different peak than the config asked for audited clean. **The judgement worth crediting is which number they compared.** The record carries two candidates, and the obvious one (`learning_rate`) is a straight echo of `cfg.training.lr` that says nothing about the optimizer; they used `peak_lr` from the recorded `OptimizerPlan` instead, and pinned the choice with a test that sets `learning_rate=9e-4` beside `peak_lr=5e-4` solely so the wrong choice fails — reading the echo instead fails 10 tests. They also wrote down why warmup does not make this a false positive (`training.lr` names the schedule target, so a warmup+cosine run reads `ok`) rather than leaving the next reader to wonder, and made a malformed or non-finite recorded peak read DIVERGED, consistent with the module's existing rule that a nonsense record must not read as agreement. 4 mutations, 4 killed; 148 passed; 10 of the new tests fail on `main`. On record: the M4 Max run behind the claim is not committed as a fixture, so it is corroborated from source rather than reproducible by a reviewer without Apple Silicon ([#942](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/942), [#1109](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1109))
  - Eight documented `soup train` flags did not exist, so four copy-pasteable examples exited 2 with `No such option`. Every one is rewritten to the real `training.*` field **and the allowlist that grandfathered them is deleted**, which is the half that makes it stay fixed: the guard now scores 8 failed against `main`'s docs where `main`'s own guard scored 4 passed. The care is in the replacements — each one loads through the real `load_config_from_string`, and every field they pointed at has a consumer in `src/` outside the schema, so the fix does not trade a nonexistent flag for a declared-but-unread field, which is this project's most common defect shape. They also kept the one flag of the nine that is live (`--echo-trap-tokenizer-aware`) instead of sweeping it with the rest. One test was deleted and the deletion is net-strengthening: it existed only to police the allowlist and would have asserted `not set()` once the allowlist was gone; 4 tests became 19, and the three survivors lost their escape hatch ([#997](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/997), [#1110](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1110))
  - Retired the stale `auto_quant` picker claims: the module still described itself as a live picker and restated a removal version that nothing read, while `soup serve --auto-quant` has refused with exit 2 since #816. **The round that mattered was about a promise that could never come due** - the first draft tied the removal to `DEPRECATED_VALUE_REJECTION_VERSION`, a constant a test forces to advance every minor, so the deadline would have read "next minor" forever; they moved it to a tracked issue instead. The duplicate test they delete is safe and they showed why rather than asserting it: what replaces it pins exit code 2, the refusal text and the ABSENCE of the picker's output across four backends. Confirmed by mutation - `Exit(2)` -> `Exit(1)` fails 5, and removing the refusal entirely fails the same 5 ([#1063](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1063))
  - `mini_tool_call` sat at 1.000 on a capable model once v0.73.2 repaired its scorer, and a suite pinned at the ceiling detects a regression as poorly as one pinned at zero. It is now a 40-row selection-and-abstention suite, 24 tool calls and 16 rows whose correct answer is exactly `NO_TOOL`, scoring 0.850 on Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct against 0.050 on SmolLM2-135M-Instruct. **The scale was chosen by measurement, and the record keeps the option they turned down**: a 32-row candidate at 0.969, still too close to the ceiling. The scorer revision and fingerprint move together, so a baseline taken before the change warns instead of being compared across scales, and the exact-abstention contract is pinned against `startswith`, case-folding and stripped backticks ([#405](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/405), [#1111](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1111))
  - Made `soup adapters sign --backend sigstore` live: keyless signing through sigstore-python 4.x over the adapter's Merkle root, coded against the real 4.5.0 API rather than the issue's old sketch. **Verification trusts only an out-of-band certificate identity and OIDC issuer, never the identity the bundle carries, and refuses an identity policy on any non-Sigstore record** (the same shape the v0.75.1 `--public-key` fix closed). Browser OIDC is opt-in, so headless runners fail instead of hanging. The Sigstore stack lives in its own `[sigstore]` extra, so air-gapped ed25519 users pull nothing new. Every failure (identity mismatch, sigstore's own errors, a missing extra, bundle text carrying terminal escapes) reaches `adapters verify` as a finding with `--strict` exit 3. The last push also checks the record path before any signer runs, which stops `ed25519 --generate-key` from leaving a private key behind ([#185](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/185), [#1114](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1114))
  - Made Llasa's XCodec2 live encoding real and replaced Sesame CSM's false install hint with a config-load refusal that says why it cannot be trained; pinned torchaudio in the floor CI job, where pip had paired torch 2.6.0 with an incompatible torchaudio and killed the job before its test ran ([#265](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/265), [#1112](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1112))
  - Made `soup attest emit --sign sigstore` live, where it had printed "infra-blocked" and silently written an UNSIGNED attestation with exit 0. **Every local check runs before the first Fulcio/Rekor request, because a Sigstore signature is a permanent public Rekor entry carrying the signer's identity**: `--output`, paths, `--key` and case variants of `--sign` are all refused first. Verification needs an out-of-band identity and issuer, and a verifier outage now exits 1 instead of being reported as tampering. 19 of 20 mutations of the sign and verify paths were killed at the final head ([#179](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/179), [#1115](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1115))
  - Published the STEP 13 CPU fixture-window probe (`benchmarks/harness/fixture_window_cpu.py`), which answers both questions the scratchpad lost: where the CI fixture sits relative to the fused-kernel window, and whether bitsandbytes' CPU inference packing causes the recorded rounding-scale difference, one `Linear4bit` at a time against Soup's shipped variant 2. The default run fails closed on every axis (control exact on every row, packing present, every packed row different, every difference inside 2%), and each row carries a same-run emulated-bf16 reference, so the envelope is justified by the harness's own output (4.17x the worst emulated row). Its conditional test surfaced the first real AVX512-BF16 run, on a GitHub runner: 12 of 12 rows packed with the native kernel, 0.375-0.572% against variant 2. Final review: 30 tests (63 with the README reader), all 30 failing on main; the three mutations that had left the emulated reference unpinned each fail a test ([#379](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/379), [#1144](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1144))
  - Built the fused Fast-LoRA SwiGLU MLP block: gate/up/down LoRA around SiLU with a hand-written backward that keeps `X`, `G` and `U` and recomputes `silu(G)` and `M`. At the Llama-3.1-8B shape it saves **117,702,656 bytes** of activations against plain peft, exactly the two `[N, f]` tensors the issue predicted plus one `[N, r]`. **The round worth recording is the numerics one**: review found the bf16 backward failing the tracker's own 2x-vs-float64 rule on one NF4 seed (4.93x), from a six-op bf16 SiLU-derivative chain and a cast-before-add on the LoRA term. The fix came back within the hour: fp32 opmath for the derivative, one shared promote-add-cast helper that the single-projection and QKV kernels now use too, and a CPU test for each half, so CI sees what only a card could before. Along the way they moved the peft-delegation guards into one shared `_projection_state` instead of a third drifting copy. On the final head the CPU suite is 126 passed / 6 skipped, the file fails 88 tests on `main`, and reverting the shared add, the down-projection add, or saving one extra `[N, f]` tensor fails 6, 5 and 1 tests ([#837](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/837), [#1107](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1107))
  - Built the shared-X Fast-LoRA Q/K/V kernel: one `X @ A_cat^T` GEMM for the three LoRA-A projections, one `dA` GEMM and one accumulated `dX`, with the base GEMMs left separate so packed NF4 never needs re-laying-out. **The design call worth recording is the hook**: instead of forking HF attention forwards per architecture, the patched `q_proj` computes Q, K and V together and hands K and V to the next two calls through a short-lived cache keyed by the identity of the input tensor, delegating to peft for any other tensor. When review showed the suite could not tell the kernel from its absence, they pinned the fused path on all three outputs, so a cache that is silently not consumed now fails 56 tests. Over six review rounds they also closed a bf16 cast-before-add, a crash on cross-attention whose key width differs (reproduced on a real TrOCR module), a mixed int8 sibling, and ownership with the single-projection patcher in both orders, each with a test, and kept every restack patch-identical. On the final head: 210 passed / 10 skipped on CPU, the three NF4 rows pass on an RTX 5070, and 23 of 24 mutations fail a test ([#838](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/838), [#1108](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1108))
  - Streamed NF4 on CUDA always dequantised and ran `F.linear` while resident NF4 ran bitsandbytes' fused GEMM, so on sm_120 the two disagreed (4.88e-4 fp16 / 3.9e-3 bf16). They routed streamed NF4 through bitsandbytes' own dispatch, read at call time, and ran the fused arm through a checkpoint-visible autograd Function with a slot-ownership check, which fails toward the exact path if bitsandbytes' private names move. They also folded #841's per-mapping view cache into the same PR. On the RTX 5070: 19/19 `gpu` tests pass with `[nf4]` at exactly 0.0, the three ruling mutations are each caught (7, 1 and 3 failures), and the #776 numbers return when the source is taken from main. The second round found the defect that mattered most: `_serve_sglang` loaded the tokenizer with `trust_remote_code` defaulting to `False` while `create_sglang_runtime` hardcodes `True` and the startup panel promises exactly that — so on custom-code models, the ones where a chat template matters most, the load failed, `tokenizer` stayed `None`, and **the fix silently did nothing**. Offered both repairs, they took the narrower one and said the wider change to the SGLang trust contract belongs in its own PR. They also extended `test_no_second_hand_rolled_prompt_remains_in_the_serve_backends` to scan `sglang.py`, which had covered `serve.py` and `vllm.py` only — leaving the one file the whole PR exists because of unprotected against re-introduction. They found it **as a red test rather than by reading**. The guard that decided the merge is that the model id is pinned on **two independent surfaces** — mutating `RecipeMeta.model` alone and mutating the YAML `base:` alone each fail a named test — which is exactly what a competing PR for the same issue lacked, and they had it without being asked.
