The finding was arithmetic rather than opinion: two rows sharing a tokenizer over identical data must share a trained-token total, and at the published figures the 0.5B row implied 98.5 tokens per iteration against the 7B row's 46.5. Ground truth came from the captured mlx-lm fixtures in their **own sibling PR** — ~45 — so 240 tok/s was a late instantaneous reading mixed into an otherwise whole-run table, and the honest figure is 108.1. **What lifts it above a correction is that they carried the consequences into the parts that made the record look worse**: the unexplained 7B-slower-than-8B result was left standing at 31.5 against 32.6, because a metric fix that also dissolved an unexplained oddity would be suspicious rather than reassuring; and the warm-cache caveat, which had compared instantaneous against instantaneous and called the spread ~2x, was restated on one metric as 3.5x — larger than they had claimed. The harness now computes throughput from mlx-lm's own cumulative counter rather than recomputing tokenisation, which would drift silently the first time the chat template changed upstream, and prints nothing when the counter is absent instead of inventing a figure. The record is the working one: a failed rung with the mechanism traced, a wrong prediction left in, and three self-reported corrections — one of which, that a bare 401 from the Hub proves nothing because an invented repo returns the same, is directly load-bearing for [#661](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/661) ([#23](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/23), [#663](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/663))

Driving the real CLI with a recording display returns `start(48)`, per-report updates and one `stop`. **Two decisions carry the PR.** `speed` maps `iterations_per_second`, not `tokens_per_second` — feeding the latter into a field labelled `it/s` is a ~44x overstatement — and the docstring that listed the wrong key, *inside the PR whose whole reason for existing is that mistake*, now names the one actually read. And `grad_norm` is **absent** rather than 0.0, because a plausible zero on one backend beside a real value on another is worse than an obviously missing field. Review caught that their own test for that permitted `in (None, 0.0)`, i.e. accepted exactly what its docstring called worse; an injected 0.0 survived, and the strengthened assertion kills it. **Best of all, they wrote a test, it failed, and they corrected the test rather than the code**: it had asserted the Web UI should be fed with no display attached, which the transformers path does not do either — two backends disagreeing about when `/api/train/stream` goes quiet is worse than both being quiet together. They chose the stronger of the two available designs: the routes are **removed** on a non-loopback bind rather than auth-gated, so there is no route left to get the wiring wrong on, and the test that decides it asserts **404 with a valid Bearer token**. **Five mutations, five killed, including both over-broad shapes** — docs always on, and docs always off; the second matters as much as the first, since a test proving only the public case would accept a patch that also breaks local development. **What I would point others at is the weakness they volunteered.** They reported, unprompted, that three of their own mutations were killed only by an attribute assertion, because FastAPI gates every docs route behind `openapi_url` first and a behavioural probe alone cannot distinguish them — then converted that into a real test with a positive control pinning the upstream contract the whole design leans on, instead of leaving it as a footnote that made the suite look stronger than it was. They also enumerated all 25 routes on a public bind so the remaining surface is stated rather than assumed, and corrected a security paragraph that had been stale since [#707](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/707) ([#731](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/731), [#732](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/732)).
  - Gave validation loss the three sinks it had never reached, and **the design choice that makes it trustworthy is the one that refuses to be convenient**. `SoupTrainerCallback` read `logs["loss"]` and never `logs["eval_loss"]`, so a run with `val_split` set computed a number that reached no panel, no database column and no API consumer. The naive fix -- hold the last value and write it every step -- fabricates data: nine database rows for two measurements, indistinguishable from real ones afterwards. They split it deliberately, so the panel is sticky (it must not blink out between evaluations) while the tracker and the SSE wire receive a value only when one was measured, and `metrics.val_loss` is nullable so a train-only row stores NULL rather than 0.0. **The finding they most wanted on the record was against their own work**: they had verified the sink at the dataclass boundary, one layer above where the value actually died in `_ALLOWED_KEYS`, so the first round's "all three sinks" claim was false as shipped -- they said so plainly rather than quietly repairing it. The last round closed exactly that class: a field can no longer be added to the dataclass and to `_ALLOWED_KEYS` while being absent from the `to_payload` tuple, and their new test is the single failure under that mutation. Ten mutations at merge, ten killed; zero deleted test lines; the Rich-rendered assertions verified under `FORCE_COLOR=1` so they cannot break the coloured CI cells ([#23](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/23), [#713](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/713))
  - Built the weekly guard that resolves every recipe model id against the Hub, and **the constraints are better than the feature**. 165 recipes carry two independently editable surfaces each, and nothing checked that either names a repository that still exists. The guard runs on `schedule` and `workflow_dispatch` only, asks for `contents: read` and nothing else, references no secrets, and the script *refuses to start* if a Hub token is in scope -- an authenticated run would resolve gated repos the ordinary user cannot reach, which is the opposite of the question being asked. A rate limit prints "could not check" and returns 0; only a real not-found fails the job, so an unrelated docs PR can never go red because an upstream repo moved. **The subtle half is pinned twice**: `GatedRepoError` subclasses `RepositoryNotFoundError`, so catching the parent first silently reclassifies every gated repo as missing -- they inject both classes as parameters *and* assert the subclass relationship against the installed library, so if upstream un-nests them the suite says so instead of the ordering becoming quietly pointless. The budget is measured, not assumed: 118 unique ids from 330 surfaces, 64% of calls saved by caching, 29.8 s, zero limits hit. Nine of ten mutations killed by name; the tenth is currently equivalent and was recorded rather than hidden. No network in the default suite, verified by making `socket.connect` raise ([#677](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/677), [#715](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/715))
  - Gave MLX SFT the response-only mask it had been documenting and not doing — `data.train_on_responses_only` defaults to `true` and reached **nothing** on that path, so every MLX run trained on system and user turns while the adapter metadata recorded `mask_prompt: false` regardless. **The measurement is why they did not simply set upstream's flag**: `ChatDataset` masks one prefix ending before the last message, so on multi-turn chat it supervises only the final assistant turn — 772 supervised tokens before, **71** with upstream's flag, 146 with a correct mask, over 16 two-turn conversations on their own M1. Taking the flag would have looked like a fix and trained on a tenth of the intended tokens. Three review rounds, and the correction that mattered was not code: they had been mutating `mlx_masking.py`, the part they designed, and never `mlx_sft.py`, where the feature is *connected* — so the mutation restoring the original defect verbatim left the suite identical at `21 passed, 7 skipped`. They stated that diagnosis back in their own words, killed all four survivors **on a runner with no MLX**, and generalised it to their other open PR without being told twice. They also volunteered that one of their own claims was false once I gave line numbers, and named the single shipped recipe whose behaviour changes rather than describing it in the abstract ([#683](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/683), [#733](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/733))
  - Wired the four MLX optimizer settings that the schema validated, documented and nothing read — `warmup_ratio`, `scheduler`, `weight_decay`, `optimizer` were all dropped by a `build_optimizer` that constructed `AdamW(learning_rate=lr)`, so a config asking for cosine decay with warmup trained flat and said nothing. **Four review rounds, and the last one is the one worth recording**: the test that would have caught the final survivor already existed and asserted exactly the right property, but evaluated the schedule through a helper opening with `pytest.importorskip("mlx.core")`, so it was skipped on every runner this project has — while the builder-level control meant to cover for it passed `warmup_ratio=0.0`, which returns before the MLX import, making its assertion true for every possible implementation of the branch it was named after. They closed it by parametrising **both** spellings that reach the branch and asserting **both** halves (no cosine curve AND exactly one linear_schedule, the second being what catches a fall-through specifically), then **renamed** the misleading control rather than deleting it, with a docstring recording why it cannot discriminate. Nine mutations, nine killed, all on an interpreter with no mlx. They also pinned the benchmark harness against their own change before being asked ([#686](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/686), [#734](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/734))
  - Built the MLX validation-loss producer, and **corrected the review that was blocking it**. mlx-lm calls `on_val_loss_report` on every evaluation and the base-class body is `pass`, so an MLX run with a validation split computed a number and discarded it. Two blockers, both closed by measurement rather than by argument: a test named for the SSE wire passed **no display**, and `on_train_loss_report` returns at its `display is None` guard before both sinks, so training steps emitted nothing and its assertion held for every implementation — the same shape they had already caught and documented on the tracker sibling, which had not been carried across; and the val hook wrote a row with `loss`/`lr`/`speed` at their `0.0` defaults, fabricating over half the `loss` series. **My proposed remedy for the second was wrong** — I said the transformers path folds the value into one call and creates no extra row; it does create a row, and what it actually does is *carry* the last measured training values. They measured the merged backend, found the review's premise false, and shipped the faithful mirror instead of what they were asked for. Eight mutations, eight killed ([#23](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/23), [#739](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/739))
  - Found and closed the third MLX setting in one week that the schema validated, the docs described, and no code read: `training.max_grad_norm`. Fifteen transformers trainers forward it, one site each; MLX forwarded it nowhere **and did not warn**, since `_check_unsupported` did not list it either — so an MLX run silently trained with no gradient clipping while every other backend clipped. Their wrapper hands the **clipped** gradients to the inner optimizer and delegates attribute access so it stays transparent to mlx-lm. **The two mutations worth recording both leave a correct-looking `clip_grad_norm` call in place** and change only what reaches the optimizer — applying the raw gradients and discarding the clip result, and clipping after the update rather than before — so a test asserting merely that the clipper was called would pass on both; theirs asserts the clipper's *return value* is what the optimizer receives, and dies on each. Six mutations, six killed on a runner with no MLX. They were also honest about the boundary rather than letting a green tick imply more: no CI test asserts a clipped gradient magnitude, the arithmetic is their own M1 measurement, and what covers the real path is the macos `mlx-smoke` job driving the actual CLI through `mx.compile` ([#749](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/749), [#750](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/750))
  - Reported and fixed, twenty-five minutes apart, the crash that ended every clean `soup eval auto` run: a typer command called as a plain Python function keeps `OptionInfo` objects as its unpassed defaults, and `OptionInfo` defines neither `__bool__` nor `__len__`, so it is **always truthy** — which is why the `--output` block fired on every direct call and died in `os.fspath`, past the results table and past the tracker write, in a `TypeError` the surrounding handler does not catch. **The two-line fix is not why this PR is here**: it ships a repo-wide AST guard that resolves each callee to its declaration, including through `from X import name`, and compares the call's keywords against whatever that command declares *at the time the suite runs* — neither of today's two parameters appears in it, and adding a brand-new option to the command a release from now fails five tests naming the caller, the callee and the new parameter. A second PR arrived against the same issue with a **byte-identical** production change, and what separated them is that theirs could be silently disabled — a leak finder returning nothing left its suite green, while this one loses two tests. They also ran the vacuity sweep on themselves unprompted and reported the number that invited the question, and declined to characterise the competing PR's results on the grounds that they were not theirs to report ([#752](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/752), [#754](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/754))
  - Built the guard that fails the suite when a config field is declared in `schema.py` and read by nothing outside it — the "validated, documented, accepted, and read by nothing" defect two consecutive releases have been named for. **What makes it more than a grep is that reads are collected structurally**: Load-context attribute access, Load-context string subscripts, and the name argument of `getattr`/`get`/`pop`/`setdefault`/`hasattr`, with writes and bare constants never collected, so a field appearing only in an error string or a docstring cannot launder itself into looking wired — and `@property` reads fold in only when the property is itself called. I verified it against the real schema rather than its own fixtures: an unwired field added to `TrainingConfig` turns it red naming that field, and a module that reads it turns it green again. The 39-entry allowlist is pinned exactly so it fails when it grows *and* when it goes stale, and no entry may hand-wave its reason. They also **measured the hole rather than claiming there was none** — an unwired `training.top_k` slips through where `training.verbose` does not, because that attribute name occurs elsewhere in the tree — and filed [#761](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/761) for the four settings the guard turned out to have no consumer at all ([#748](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/748), [#751](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/751))
  - Turned a review request for a docstring note into an executable pin, and gave the reason that justifies it: a leak table in a docstring goes stale silently, which is the exact failure mode the guard exists to prevent. The guard's name space is global, so an unwired field whose attribute name occurs elsewhere in the tree reads as consumed — I reproduced their table by injecting real unwired fields into the actual `TrainingConfig` and running the real guard, with a deliberately unique control name to prove the guard fires at all: `verbose` caught, `top_k`/`top_p`/`temperature`/`dtype` leaked, attribution exact down to `probability_policy.top_k` in the CLI-unwired `autodistill/` subsystem. **They pinned the harder direction**, that `verbose` must stay catchable, which guards against silent WEAKENING — a module that starts reading a `.verbose` attribute would otherwise leave the guard green while quietly seeing less. My first mutation survived because their assertion is hard to falsify rather than weak: renaming two of `top_k`'s ~14 consumers is not enough, and rewriting all of them across 19 files fires with a message telling the reader to update the table rather than edit the assertion ([#748](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/748), [#770](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/770))
  - Gave `soup doctor` a `--config` leg that answers, before a run, which of the settings your config actually writes are read by nothing on the backend you chose — the question the "validated, documented, read by nothing" pattern had been answering one field at a time in #683, #686, #745 and #749. **The part worth copying is the argument for declaring rather than inferring, which they made with measurements instead of assertion**: reachability over the import graph detected none of five independently-known MLX gaps, reading the trainer module alone invented gaps for fields that live in helpers, and `--dry-run` exits before a trainer exists. The declared table is kept honest by a guard in both directions, and that guard **shrank their own table from twelve entries to seven** once #734 and #750 wired five of them — reported openly rather than quietly. Only fields the user wrote are listed (`model_fields_set`, not ~275 defaults), and every other task/backend pair reports nothing rather than guessing. Chasing a review note about `COLUMNS=35` turned up a real product defect — at that width the report named no setting at all — fixed with folding rather than a test tweak. Verified before merge: 111 passed across the affected suites, ANSI-safe under `FORCE_COLOR=1` and at `COLUMNS=35`, and four mutations killed, including `check_config` returning nothing (6 failed) and dropping the `model_fields_set` filter ([#755](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/755), [#756](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/756))
  - Closed the MCP half of the `--format` guard, and the part worth copying is that they pinned the **agreement between the two surfaces** rather than an exit code. `validate_and_stats` computes `check_format = bool(expected_format and expected_format in VALID_FORMATS)`, so an unrecognised format silently turns the check *off* and an omitted one never turns it on — both answer "every row valid" for a reason that has nothing to do with the data, and after [#869](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/869) guarded the CLI the same file got two different verdicts depending on which surface asked. They took the allowlist from `VALID_FORMATS` itself rather than writing a second tuple — a hand-written copy is how the two surfaces drifted apart to begin with — and returned the resolved format in the report, which is what makes the schema's "omit to auto-detect" observable rather than merely true. The near-miss parametrize (`""`, `"  "`, `"ALPACA"`, `"alpaca "`) is the half a normalising implementation would have slipped through. Scope held: `tool_data_doctor`'s neighbouring blank-string reading was **noted in a comment and filed as** [#889](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/889) rather than swept into the same PR ([#878](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/878), [#882](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/882))
  - Five tests passed with colour off and failed with colour forced (`TERM=dumb` 148 passed; `FORCE_COLOR=1` 5 failed, 143 passed). **The most valuable thing in the PR is that they contradicted the issue body — which they had written themselves**: it prescribed the narrow-assertion repair for all of them, and that was right for none, so anyone following it to the letter would have landed a still-red test and blamed themselves. Three mechanisms wearing one symptom: a fixture built its Rich `Console` with `force_terminal` unset, so Rich read `FORCE_COLOR` from the shell and colourised into the buffer, making `"\x1b" not in out` unsatisfiable by construction; a banner check that was not in the ESC class at all (stripped, the line is exactly `--- Run 1/1: sweep_1 ---`); and two `soup doctor` NCCL checks matching raw output that colour splits. Pinning the console **keeps** the broad assertion rather than narrowing it to hand-picked escapes, which is the stronger question once the only possible ESC is the payload's — and they supplied the reason the narrow fix could not have sufficed alone, which the maintainer's ruling had not accounted for: the same test asserts `"[bold red on white]" in out`, and Rich's highlighter splits that literal across four SGR runs. They also strengthened a test nobody asked them to strengthen, so a sanitiser that swallowed the control byte entirely would no longer pass. On the three-day delay they were plainly factual — they claimed it on 09-12 and the scope grew under it — which is how anyone reading the thread knows it was scope rather than silence ([#886](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/886), [#984](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/984))
  - The #748 consumer guard counted a read inside a function that nothing calls as consumption, so `training.warmup_auto` passed on one line in `autopilot.generate_config`, which nothing in `src/` reaches. **The half worth copying is how they got the number right**: their first prototype flagged six fields, not the issue's four, and they published the two false positives and why they were false rather than quietly tuning them away. The guard now drops reads in unreferenced functions, the same reasoning the file already applied to `@property` resolvers, and pins the four real escapes by name. Verified as a ratchet in both directions: removing the gate fails two tests that name the four fields, an injected dead reader fails the exact-four pin, and referencing it turns the allowlist entry obsolete by name. When a frozen CI merge ref reported a false red, they rebased onto the right base themselves and changed nothing else ([#807](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/807), [#906](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/906))
  - `soup edit diff` given both `--before-model` and `--after-model` but no `--probes` printed an empty table and "Total probes: 0; 0 changed" and exited 0, so a comparison of nothing read as "no change". It is now refused before any model loads, with a message naming `--probes` and no diff file written. All three tests fail on that exact symptom when the guard is disabled. They also retracted a mutation row of their own that could never have fired. When a frozen CI merge ref showed a false red, they rebased onto the right base without being asked twice, and `git range-diff` shows both commits unchanged ([#880](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/880), [#985](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/985))
  - `soup rewind`: an SFT run records the dataset rows in every micro-batch with their per-row loss, and the command names the rows behind a loss spike, ranked by their share of the step's summed token loss. On an 8 GB M1 it put a poisoned row at 100% of step 58's loss. **Where the recorder cannot attribute rows, it switches itself off with one warning instead of guessing**: under packing, on resumed runs, and when distributed. Review found the spike search quadratic, and they fixed it with a counting test rather than a timing assert: 942 s became 0.197 s on a 50,000-step log, with the same 10 spikes found. They also moved the MLX tests into a CI job that actually runs them (23 passed on macOS), with the mutation evidence written into the step so nobody deletes it as redundant ([#1000](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1000), [#1018](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1018))
  - The #748 guard compared the tree it scans and the tree it imports by path spelling. On macOS, a lowercase `__file__` for the same checkout made it report a mismatch that was not there. It now compares by identity. They re-measured the issue's trigger first and corrected it: the spelling arrives through `__file__`, not the working directory. They also wrote the false-alarm test with a symlink, so it means the same thing on Linux CI; a case-only test would have passed on Windows for the wrong reason. They deleted stale evidence rather than re-quoting it, and held the PR until the change it depended on had merged ([#988](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/988), [#1029](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1029))
  - The FP8-attention and NVFP4 gate tests asked the real hardware and torchao instead of patching the gate. On a Blackwell card they failed, and on older machines they passed without being able to tell whether the gates existed. That was measured on this repo's RTX 5070: the old tests survived deleting the Hopper and the Blackwell gate. The tests now patch the gate, give the same result with and without CUDA or torchao, and each gate has a test that fails when it is removed. They caught their own first mutation surviving and traced it to the conversion's own torchao import ([#834](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/834), [#1041](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1041))
  - `soup data preprocess` wrote a cache that `format: pre_tokenized` then refused as a hash mismatch, and a list-valued `train` could crash it. Both sides now derive the key through one shared helper. The stored format is used only as a hash input, so a tampered cache is still refused. Verified end to end with no mocks: preprocess followed by `soup train` exits 1 on `main` and trains on the cached rows with the fix. They filed the issue themselves, measured the root cause on both sides, and found an interleave gap that no one had reported. Making the flow work is also what exposed the next defect: the cache carries no labels ([#1038](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1038), [#1040](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1040))
  - `data.remove_unused_columns` defaulted to `True` while every trainer that sets it passed `False`, and nothing read the field. They measured before choosing a shape, through the real config loader rather than the model, and corrected two facts in the maintainer's ruling against the code. **Then the ruling changed under them mid-review**, because `soup autopilot` turns out to write the whole model into `soup.yaml`, so configs Soup itself generated carry the value that was about to be refused. They reproduced that with real dumps and found 16 affected files where the review had reported 8, and rebuilt the fix as a deprecation: the value loads with a warning and is ignored now, and is refused in the next release. The mechanism is one shared module with the deadline written once and a test that turns red when that release arrives, reused byte-identically by their neighbouring PR ([#759](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/759), [#1027](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1027))
  - Eight tasks never apply quantization, and on every one of them `training.quantization` was validated, documented and then **read by nothing** — this project's most common defect shape, found eight times at once. The value now resolves to `none` there and an explicitly requested one is refused by name. **The transition was implemented as ruled rather than argued with**: a value Soup itself wrote into a config warns and resolves instead of failing the load, so all 48 shipped dumps keep loading with one warning each, and refusal waits for the next release. **The ordering test is what keeps this fixed** — a resolver that sits in the right place today and is moved later by an unrelated PR reopens the hole with every test still green; on the earlier head that misplacement passed 97 tests, and now it fails exactly one. Verified here on the merged tree: 121 passed across the PR's tests and the two documentation guards, 783 passed / 8 skipped across the config blast radius ([#795](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/795), [#1043](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1043))
  - `soup draft measure` reported throughput and left the operator to work out the rest. It now reports the **acceptance rate at which speculative decoding stops paying** and the draft length that maximises speedup. The arithmetic was hand-verified here rather than read off the tests: with `c = 39.28/65.90`, the k=5 break-even bisects to 0.832, `S(1,5) = 1.507`, `S(0.81293,5) = 0.955`, and at a=0.813 the speedups 1.136 / 1.129 / 1.080 decrease so best k = 1 — each matching what the command prints. **The ANSI half is the part that took two rounds and was worth it**: they diagnosed Rich's `Console.size` returning `self._width - self.legacy_windows` on a legacy Windows console, then pinned the module console outright, so 64 tests pass at `COLUMNS` unset, 79, 80 and under `FORCE_COLOR=1` — a failure class that has turned this repo's CI red four times. #1053 landed in the same file while this was open and the escaping survives: every new row interpolates floats, ints and five literal constants ([#843](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/843), [#1042](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1042))
  - `soup adapters audit` compares what a finished adapter **actually is** against the `soup.yaml` that produced it, so a run whose settings never reached the trainer is detectable afterwards instead of only by reading trainer source — the counter-measure to this project's most common defect. **The mutation that decided it** had survived a previous round: `_lora_params` returning an empty mapping instead of `None` for a malformed `lora` block, i.e. the code distinguished absent from malformed and nothing could tell. It now fails 7 tests. The previous form let a test lose its marker while keeping the import, and that mutation now fails. A `GUARDED_TESTS` map covers all twelve real-train tests, including #1059's eight, and a drift test keeps it in sync with the call-site list. They re-read the CI logs themselves, corrected their own rationale at the decorator where the next reader will see it, and reported that their own first `sed` mutation had been a no-op ([#382](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/382), [#1062](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1062))

They reproduced the maintainer's torchao-present/absent measurement before changing anything, and added one test per path that fails when the order is swapped back, plus a control proving a missing dependency alone still degrades rather than refuses. **The 19 tests the reorder broke were each explained rather than loosened**: five MagicMock configs had been enabling `fp8_attention` by accident, because a MagicMock attribute is truthy. Five trainers now wire `moe_lora`, the flags no trainer reads are refused per task, and an attach peft cannot perform is stopped before it is attempted. **Three things are worth copying.** They reported a deviation from my ruling BEFORE opening the PR — I had ruled "refuse at config load when `moe_lora: true` and `lora.dropout != 0`", they measured a second architecture, found the refusal had to move to attach time, and said so rather than implementing the instruction they knew was wrong; I amended the ruling. When I named `templates/moe.yaml` as a 32nd shipped MoE config missing its dropout pin, they found a **second copy of the same template inlined in `config/schema.py`** through the repo's own template-equality test, and widened the guard to sweep `templates/**` and `examples/**` with an anti-vacuity control. And they replaced a shape-guessing predicate with a call to peft's own `convert_peft_config_for_transformers`, which is how Mixtral and MiniMax stopped being refused. 18 mutations, 0 survivors, each of the five wirings killed by a test naming its own trainer; 35 of the new file's 68 tests fail on `main` ([#798](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/798), [#1074](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1074))
  - `training.nvfp4` and three of four torchao export schemes looked up class names torchao does not export — and the reason the old tests passed is the part worth reading: they stubbed a fake `torchao.quantization.NVFP4Config`, **a name torchao has never exported**, so the suite agreed with the shipped code about a class that does not exist. Every name now resolves through one helper that imports the **root package first** and then walks a declared candidate list. That order is load-bearing and it is what the first revision got wrong: `import_module("torchao.prototype…")` returns a cached submodule even when the root is gone, so the resolver answered from a warm cache and the friendly missing-torchao path could never fire. Their regression test is built to make that visible — it caches the submodules deliberately and pops the root key, the exact bug shape — and I could not find an arrangement (alone, after the file, after a polluter, reversed order) that broke it. 4 mutations, 4 killed, measured with **torchao 0.18.0 really installed**, since the 12 tests that matter skip without it; they also added an advisory `torchao-contract` CI job that installs the extra and asserts `import torchao`, so it cannot silently skip. They also took the MLX half as a real defect rather than an edge case - `backend: mlx` supervises every assistant turn, so it now SAYS it ignores the field and `soup doctor --config` prints the row instead of an affirmative all-clear ([#761](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/761), [#1101](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1101))
  - `target_modules: auto` had no mapping for the MoE architectures Soup ships, so peft was asked to guess and answered with an error naming nothing. Two blocking rounds, both closed by measurement: the first draft's refusal fired BEFORE the `moe_lora` override, which they proved fixed by replaying a real `Qwen2MoeConfig` through the trainers' own call order (main attaches 14 modules, the fix attaches the same 14); and the `minimax_m3_vl` pattern, which had only ever been matched against hand-written literals, is now checked against the real model's `named_modules()`. **The ratchet rebuild is what will keep paying** - 117 recipe bases recorded from raw `config.json`, 20 MoE across 11 model_types against the previous hand-kept 17/9, and it FIRES: a synthetic flagless MoE recipe now fails it where the old one stayed green ([#1070](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1070), [#1102](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1102))
  - `soup bench` measured inference only, so "did this change make training faster" had no reproducible answer and no contract saying what such a number must report. `soup bench train` reports median and p95 step time, supervised tokens/s counted from `labels != -100` rather than from the padded batch, and allocated and reserved memory SEPARATELY - and it refuses rather than guesses: no steps left after the warm-up, zero trainable parameters, an unreadable `nvidia-smi`, and a parameters-unchanged check so it cannot report a benchmark of a run that did not train. **The review finding worth repeating is how they fixed it**: an earlier head watched for ANY `nvidia-smi` spawn and went red on a real card because accelerate spawns one itself; instead of loosening the watch until it stopped complaining they scoped it to Soup's own code, and the scoping is proven to discriminate - the same spawn injected inside `bench/train_run.py` fails the control while the byte-identical one from outside `soup_cli` does not. 14 mutations, 0 survivors ([#836](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/836), [#1146](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1146))
  - `training.moe_lora` loaded on `ipo`, `bco`, `reward_model`, `ppo` and `embedding` and none of those trainers read it — the #748 silently-ignored class, so a config asking for expert adapters trained attention-only without a word. **They found a sixth before opening**: flipping the flag on a shipped config for every task turned up `online_dpo`, which the issue had not named. Each trainer now resolves expert targets at the site where it already resolved its LoRA targets, and each of the six wirings is killed by exactly one test whose parametrize id names that trainer (8/8 mutations). The control was written to be correct in either merge order with #1102, and it is: making `_settle_unmapped` never raise on the merged tree reddens five of #1102's own tests, so the fail-closed guard is intact underneath ([#1099](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1099), [#1148](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1148))
  - The preprocess cache and the live path disagreed on the leading BOS for chat-template presets whose template already renders one: the tokenizer's post-processor added a second, so cached rows trained on a sequence the live path never produced. The cache now strips exactly the BOS the post-processor added — measured per tokenizer by a probe — and never the template's own, and truncation runs before the strip so a truncated row keeps the EOS reserved for it. **The review round is the instructive part**: they declined a reviewer-suggested `truncation=False` + slice with a reason, and executing it proved them right (it drops the post-processor EOS and fails 3 tests). 7 of 8 mutations killed, real-tokenizer tables on TinyLlama, Llama-3.2-1B and Qwen2.5, and the cache-key schema bumped to v5 so no stale cache is reused ([#876](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/876), [#1096](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1096))
  - Closed the two non-blocking nits from the #1101 and #1102 reviews. `docs/data.md` now says where `data.mask_history` is actually applied — text SFT on the transformers and unsloth backends, and `task: distill` — and that `task: sft` with `modality: vision` or `audio` accepts it without applying it (#1156); the #1102 fragment names all 10 `MOE_TEXT_LORA_TARGETS` entries instead of 8. **They checked the whole task × backend × modality grid before rewording**, and that is what showed the clause I had suggested would have been false for `distill`, which never reads `modality` ([#1167](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1167))
  - `load_dataset` dropped every row its format converter rejected with a bare `continue`, so `soup train` fitted fewer rows than the file held, exited 0 and said nothing. The drop stays, because it is the documented contract, but every load now prints how many rows it lost, the first one's index and the converter's own reason, and for a local file the `soup data validate` command that lists them all. **They reported the drop rather than refusing on it, and said why**: turning it into a stop would change a documented contract, so they left that call to a ruling instead of making it inside a fix. The loader's line and `soup data validate` agree on the same file, the before/after comes from the real CLI, and the one mutant that survived their first run (naming the last dropped row instead of the first) now fails a test; 8 of 8 mutations killed ([#1181](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1181), [#1183](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1183))
  - `soup data preprocess` skipped any row it could not tokenize with a bare `continue` and exited 0, while live `soup train` stops on the same rows. So a `pre_tokenized` run could train on 3 of 5 rows under a `soup.yaml` whose live run refuses, with nothing naming the two that went. The command now refuses what live training refuses, and **the way that was established is the part worth copying**: the same rows through both paths, case by case. When live pretraining turned out to stop on a text row the tokenizer rejects, they removed the pretrain carve-out and inverted the test that had pinned it, rather than keep a documented exception. The refusal numbers the row as training does, quotes the start of the row, and is terminal-safe down to the role, which they capped, `repr`'d and routed through `for_terminal`. A clean dataset caches the same bytes as before, and 19 of 21 mutations fail tests in both colour modes ([#1180](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1180), [#1182](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1182))
  - `training.moe_lora` was accepted and read by nothing on eight tasks; it is now wired into distill, unlearn and the classifier family, and refused at config load wherever no adapter is built (asr, moe_lora_routing, prm, backend: unsloth, SFT vision/audio), with all 19 mutation cases killed ([#1151](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1151), [#1179](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1179))
  - A missing torchao now stops an explicitly requested FP8 run (card checked first) instead of falling back silently, and `soup train` refuses before the model loads; running the real CLI also turned up Rich eating the `[qat]` extra from the install hint, fixed in the same PR ([#835](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/835), [#1154](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1154))
  - Added `soup recipes verify`, the check #1116 argued nothing ran: does a shipped config actually reach an adapter? It fetches only `config.json`, builds the architecture on the meta device with the class that config's trainer loads, and runs Soup's own target resolution, the `moe_lora` rescue and the real peft attach with the config's own LoRA settings. So the verdict is the trainer's, not a re-implementation of it. A gated or remote-code repo is reported as unverified rather than broken, and exit codes follow the gate/verdict contract. The design choice worth copying is attaching instead of grepping: before any code, their live run found three MoE-by-architecture recipes (`gpt-oss-20b-sft`, `gpt-oss-120b-sft`, `minimax-m2-sft`) that #1102's flag-based sweep could not see. Six review rounds, each point closed with a measurement: a skeleton that keeps one layer of every kind (26 catalogue rows gained adapted modules, 0 verdicts changed), a broken library that exits 1 instead of turning into a verdict, and a `--json` stdout that stays a document even when the config loader warns. On the final head merged with `main`, 124 tests pass and 7 of 7 source mutations fail a test ([#1116](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1116), [#1117](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1117))
  - `training.lr_groups`, `training.early_stop_patience` and `training.citation_recall_threshold` were documented, validated and read by nothing. They now get #808's warn-then-refuse through #1106's `STAGED_FIELDS` and its single deadline constant, and the docs and schema descriptions no longer promise a per-module learning rate or a recall gate that never existed. **The move is the part worth copying**: `early_stop_patience` left `soup train`'s undated note instead of being listed twice, which is the double diagnostic #1106's review had to untangle, and a test pins both that it left and that its sibling flag stayed. When review showed that staging the fields blinded the #755 drift guard, they fixed the guard and put its original stand-in back instead of keeping the substitute that had hidden the gap. 10 of 10 mutations fail a test, and 1,103 tests pass across 23 related files ([#761](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/761), [#1337](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1337))
  - An explicitly requested FP8 that failed during conversion trained on anyway. `apply_fp8_training` returned `False` and discarded torchao's error, so `quantization_aware: fp8` printed a yellow line and trained in bf16. The `fp8_attention` refusals ("PARTIALLY converted", "refusing the silent no-op") were caught and printed as yellow lines while training went on over a half-converted model or a no-op. All three now stop the run at setup and quote the cause. The uncalled `validate_fp8_config` is deleted, with a table mapping every behaviour its tests pinned to a live-path test that still holds it. **The discipline worth copying is what they left out**: NVFP4's identical degrade, and the OOM matcher that swallows the wrapped message, were both flagged as out of scope rather than folded in. Review ran the real torchao 0.18 and found its own conversion raising `AssertionError` and `TypeError`, which escaped the first catch unwrapped. They widened it to every exception and added the ready test in one push, replying twenty-five minutes after the review with three numbers that match my own re-measurement exactly. On the final head merged with `main`: 458 tests pass in the FP8 core set and 1,923 in the 28-file FP8 radius, 11 of the PR's tests fail on `main`, and 14 of 14 source mutations fail a test ([#1152](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1152), [#1333](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1333))
  - The `moe_lora` + `backend: unsloth` refusal gave one reason on every task, and it was false on the ten tasks with no unsloth setup; it also fired before the refusals that hold on every backend, so `asr`, `prm`, `moe_lora_routing` and SFT vision/audio were sent to `transformers` only to be refused again. They measured the task list from the wrapper classes rather than trusting the issue's seven, split the reason by a ratchet-checked `UNSLOTH_SETUP_TASKS`, and reordered the checks so each of those configs gets one refusal on every backend. A 936-config grid shows nothing newly refused (305 load on both sides); 135 tests pass on the merged head, 35 of the 72 new ones fail on `main`, and 12 of 12 source mutations are killed ([#1264](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1264), [#1323](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1323))
  - `backend: unsloth` loaded on seven tasks whose trainers have no unsloth setup (`reward_model`, `prm`, the classifier family, `unlearn`, `moe_lora_routing`). The run trained on plain transformers under an "unsloth (fast mode)" banner, and `soup doctor --config` called the config clean. They refused it at load with one check keyed on #1323's ratchet-checked `UNSLOTH_SETUP_TASKS`. It sits last, so the refusals that hold on every backend still come first, and the fragment states the breaking change together with its one-line fix. **Review's catch was about order**: on six tasks `unsloth_bnb_4bit` got in ahead of the refusal with "got quantization='none'", a value the user never wrote, because the #795 resolver had already rewritten it. The fix went in within about half an hour, and so did a second class closing a gap in the review's own test. Each time they re-ran the review's key mutation before replying. They also took the optional structural test that forces any later every-backend check above theirs. When #1313 then landed in the same spot, they rebased with that check first and theirs last, exactly as asked: rebuilding the resolution from the approved head gave their file byte for byte. On the final head, merged with `main`, 341 tests pass across the file, #1264, `test_v0530` and #748, and 282 across the file, #1223's three and #795's. 23 of the file's 64 tests fail on `main` with every control passing, all 202 shipped configs load, and 23 of 23 source mutations fail a test ([#1357](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1357), [#1372](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1372))
  - Added the CI ratchet that #1116's second half asked for. `scripts/check_recipe_attach.py` runs `soup recipes verify --json` over every shipped recipe, template and example and fails only on a change: a new config that cannot attach, a pinned one that now attaches, or a pinned one that vanishes from verify's output, against 20 exceptions each pinned with a reason. As ruled, it runs anonymously on pull requests and with `HF_TOKEN` only on push to `main`, is never a required check, and reports what it could not cover by reason (50 gated, 12 remote code, 7 unreadable), so a token-less green is not read as full coverage. **The rule worth copying is `not_reported`**: they added it test-first after their own review showed that a pinned config which stopped parsing would silently leave the list while the run stayed green, and it is what names `minimax-m3-dpo` once #1398 removes it. Review found that this rule had made the new-failure tests red for the wrong reason, and that the push trigger predated #1374's shared group for `main`; both fixes and every optional point landed in one push, with mutation counts that matched the review's exactly. On the final head merged with `main`, 18 tests pass, 13 of 14 source mutations fail a test and the 14th is caught by the live run, and actionlint is clean ([#1116](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1116), [#1388](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1388))
  - `soup expect` silently ran an expectation on its defaults when the suite put `max_tokens` beside `name` (the `docs/compliance.md` example itself) or misspelled an argument, so a 5000-token row passed a 512-token gate with exit 0; they declared each expectation's arguments in one table that also drives the allowlist and the dispatcher, refused stray keys and unknown arguments at load with a pointed hint and a `difflib` suggestion (exit 3, before the data is read), validated bounds and threshold at parse time, moved the docs example to `args:`, and wrote a test that runs the YAML block read out of `docs/compliance.md` on a 5000-token row. 21 new tests (14 fail on the previous tree), every mutation of the fix plus the docs revert killed ([#1428](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1428), [#1461](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1461))
  - A dead LLM judge was scored as a tie: `compare_pair` mapped every exception to "no winner", so `soup ship --task-mode pairwise` reported a measured 0.5 win-rate and DON'T SHIP with the judge down, `soup eval judge` aborted on the first transport error and lost the rows already scored, and nothing retried. They put every judge request behind one bounded retry helper that raises `JudgeUnavailableError` naming the judge, kept the Online DPO adapter on its own unranked-pair path with a test, and made the gate log say "could not be scored" instead of "regressed". **What is worth copying is how they took review**: three holes in their own design (a `Retry-After: nan` that reached `time.sleep`, the judge URL's userinfo and `?api_key=` printed into errors and logs, a `DecodingError` that still aborted a run) were fixed in one push, each with the test that fails without it and a mutation that turns it red; the four mutations that had survived round one were all pinned with absolute bounds rather than bounds derived from the constants they bound. 45 new tests (40 fail on `main`); 17 of 19 re-review mutations caught, the two survivors being an unpinned fallback and an unreachable branch; 470 related tests passed ([#1447](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1447), [#1484](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1484))
  - Resolved #1145's four recipes that could not load, following the three rulings on the issue: removed `mistral-large-3-sft`/`-dpo` (the repo has no `config.json`) and `minimax-m3-dpo` (DPO cannot build the `minimax_m3_vl` wrapper), and moved `minimax-m3-sft` onto the vision path. **The finding worth copying is the consequence nobody asked about**: the vision path loads `AutoProcessor`, MiniMax-M3's processor is custom code, so the recipe now needs `--trust-remote-code`, and they wrote that into the description and pinned it in a test, then checked a second review comment (the vision collator supervises every token) by running `soup doctor --config` both ways. The rebase chased moving targets (#1297, then #1388 and #1113), each time from `main`'s side with both fixtures regenerated by their scripts; the last round closed the attach-ratchet coupling and pinned the docs paragraph whose mutation had survived. 2446 tests over 20 files on the merged tree, 9 of 9 mutations caught, live attach ratchet exit 0 ([#1145](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1145), [#1398](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1398))
  - Closed the top-level half of `soup expect`'s silent-drop family: `parse_suite_spec` read only `expectations`, so `fail_fast: true` (or a `name:` label, or a typo like `expectation:`) beside it ran and exited 0. They filed it from the #1461 review, waited for #1461 to merge as agreed, and opened the PR rebased on it: six lines in `src/`, the key text echoed through #1461's `repr()` helper, `soup expect` exiting 3 before the data file is opened. They found that one existing test fixture (`name: test_all_formats_suite`) was itself an instance of the bug and removed that single line, leaving the test's rows and exit-2 assertion untouched. When review found one docs sentence missing and one surviving mutation (an allowlist widened to `name`), they closed both in one push with `src/` byte-identical and a 4-case test that kills it, and said plainly that bounding the key list would be a separate follow-up rather than folding it in. Verified on the merged tree: 270 passed; the check removed, the `repr()` removed, naming only the first key, and four allowlist widenings are each caught (8 / 2 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 failures). ([#1483](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1483), [#1499](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1499))
  - Finished the LLaMA-Factory and Unsloth halves of `soup migrate`'s preference mapping. `pref_loss: ipo` had become plain DPO, `hinge` / `kto_pair` loaded as DPO, and four TRL trainer classes migrated as SFT, all without a warning. Each value now maps to its Soup task or stops by name, `OnlineDPOTrainer` gets a placeholder judge that loads, and `SFTConfig` hyperparameters are read. **The fourth round is the one worth copying.** Adding `SFTConfig` exposed that the notebook walk was breadth-first, so an inline SFT warm-up beat a later GRPO config, and four public Unsloth GRPO notebooks turned into SFT recipes with a 40x learning rate. They switched to a source-order walk. Unasked, they also made the config resolver handle the positional `CPOTrainer(model, args)` form and refuse a rebound config name instead of reading a stale one. 278 tests pass on the merged tree, the new tests fail 42 of 67 on `main`, and all 9 mutations run on the last two rounds' code are caught ([#1214](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1214), [#1560](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1560))
  - Removed the Web UI plugin registry that nothing rendered: `soup_cli.ui.plugins` had been importable since v0.44.0, but `create_app` never read it and a registered tab never appeared. The module, its docs section and its 11 dead tests went, exactly as the ruling listed, with `soup plugins` and `ui_env` untouched. They added a guard that keeps it gone; in review they reproduced the stale ignored `__pycache__` failure themselves before applying the suggested replacement, and they extended the route pin to every decorated Web UI path plus the static mount. 709 passed / 2 skipped across 19 files; 9 of 9 mutations caught ([#1465](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1465), [#1608](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1608))
  - Made `soup migrate --from unsloth` read real Unsloth notebooks: IPython magics were parsed as Python, so every official notebook was refused as one `SyntaxError`. Their per-cell transform treats a `!` / `%` line as a magic only at statement level. That keeps `%` / `!=` continuation lines as Python, and a magic that opens a suite becomes a `pass`, so the block stays valid. It keeps the bodies of `%%capture`-style cells, treats a backslash-continued install as one magic, and makes a real syntax error name its cell and line. All 82 official GRPO notebooks migrate (0 on `main`). On the final head 129 migrate tests pass, 217 pass on the merged tree, and 8 of 8 mutations are caught ([#1579](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1579), [#1583](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1583))

They found the fused parameters by shape, carried them on the resolved targets into `build_lora_config` so every MoE-wired trainer adapts the experts without a call-site change, widened the expert scan to return the fused block, and made the quant refuse it by name. Measured per family before and after; on the merged tree 4155 passed, 26 skipped across 72 related files, and removing the parameter merge fails 15 tests ([#1421](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1421), [#1590](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1590))

The `--invocation` of `soup attest emit` now reaches the SLSA statement, and Annex XI / XII write `not measured` instead of zeros. The CycloneDX 1.6 ML-BOM gets an RFC 4122 serial number and canonical SPDX ids, expressions or names. The SPDX 2.3 document gets the right dependency direction and a defined `LicenseRef-` for every license it uses. The receipt fills `driver_version` on a CUDA host. Each artifact is tested against its own specification: a vendored CycloneDX 1.6 schema, a real ed25519-signed statement and the real Annex XI writer. It took five rounds, adding verbatim stdout, a small SPDX expression parser, per-operand `LicenseRef-` entries and a mixed-case operator row, each with its own mutation. On the final head: 278 passed, 0 failed across 8 files, before and after a trial merge with main; 48 of 59 new tests fail on main; in the last two review rounds 23 of 26 mutations caught, one harmless, and both survivors closed by a row added for each ([#1446](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1446), [#1569](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1569))
