- **Tal** ([@taljeon](https://github.com/taljeon))
  - First contribution, and it closed a gap in a guard that was **one day old**: the `dtype=` scanner from #489 matched only class names starting with `AutoModel`, so the same load-breaking kwarg on a CONCRETE class — `WhisperForConditionalGeneration.from_pretrained` in the ASR training path — was invisible to it. They took the **suffix rule** rather than a name allowlist, which is the harder and correct choice: an allowlist silently stops covering a class added later. Verified with a negative control rather than by inspection — `dtype="auto"` on the real Whisper site fails by name on their branch and **survives on `main`**, so the gap was real. Two things I check on every widening they got right unprompted: the existing `AutoModel*` coverage was not traded away for the new coverage, and the scanner still ignores `FastLanguageModel`, `PeftModel`, `torch.zeros` and wrapper kwargs — a guard that fires on correct code is one people delete. All six deleted lines are renames or a reworded message; the file goes 11 tests to 12 ([#497](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/497))
  - Soup's own two shipped preference examples tripped Soup's own linter: `soup data lint` reported MINOR length bias on `dpo_sample.jsonl` and `preference_sample.jsonl`, so anyone following the examples learned the shape of a dataset the tool flags. **The easy fix was padding, and they did not take it.** Every `rejected` completion is rewritten to be similarly detailed and still clearly worse — specific factual or methodological errors, not brevity — which is exactly the anti-padding property #929 made an acceptance criterion; `chosen` is byte-identical in all 13 rows. Sampling one: *"plants absorb sunlight through their roots... the reaction occurs in the cell wall"* — confident, detailed, wrong. The regression test is tighter than I expected: reverting **one row in eight** moves the mean from 66.9 to 60.2, a 9.3% gap below MAJOR but above the MINOR floor, and it still fails. They also chose to drive `run_lint` as a library function rather than the CLI, which is faster and sidesteps the ANSI-assertion trap entirely, with the verdict-to-exit-code mapping already covered elsewhere ([#929](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/929), [#940](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/940))
  - A sweep arm whose loss went to NaN or infinity sorted to the **top** of the summary and could be selected as the best run. A divergence is the single outcome a hyperparameter search must never recommend, and it was being recommended — silently, because the row looked like any other. Ordering is now explicit (finite losses numerically first, non-finite completed arms after, failed rows still visible at the end), a non-finite arm is labelled `diverged` and cannot carry the best-run marker, and it counts as explicitly *worse* than the early-stop threshold so early stop can no longer be triggered or suppressed by a number that is not one. **Two things raise this above the fix itself.** The colour assertions route through the shared `tests/conftest.py:strip_ansi` rather than a private copy — the difference between this and two other PRs I had to flag the same day — and they were shown to discriminate rather than assumed to: under `FORCE_COLOR=1` the fixed head passes 5 while the pre-fix tree fails on `'skipping 3 remaining run(s)' in '┌────...'`. And four non-blocking notes from an earlier round were left alone once the head stopped touching `src/`, rather than swept into an unrelated diff ([#944](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/944), [#946](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/946))
  - A provider call that failed during recipe execution was swallowed, so the run reported success having produced nothing from that node ([#930](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/930)) — **a failure that reports as success is worse than a crash**, because nothing prompts anyone to look. All five review items closed, three of them better than asked for. ([#969](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/969))
  - `soup train` reported a loss delta for runs too short to have one, computing it against a baseline that did not exist yet ([#899](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/899)) — a fabricated number presented in the same format as a real measurement, which is precisely what teaches people to trust the display. They also took a **nine-file conflict resolution** and it was checked hunk by hunk against both sides rather than by confirming the merge was clean. ([#908](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/908))
  - A step that never logged a gradient norm and a step that measured one of exactly 0.0 were INDISTINGUISHABLE: the callback carried the last value forward, the tracker defaulted the column to 0.0, the panel hid anything not `> 0`, and the chart mapped a missing value with `|| 0`. They made an absent norm stay absent at all six sinks - and the measurement is what makes the PR: on `main` the never-measured step and the measured-zero step are BYTE-IDENTICAL in the run database and on the wire, while a real 0.0 was the one value the panel refused to show. **Eight mutations, eight kills, one per behaviour**, including both wrong directions of the deliberate display-versus-record split. A win they did not claim: `mlx_sft.py` and `ppo.py` already omit the norm, so both inherit the fix from the tracker default ([#1147](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1147), [#1149](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1149))
  - `soup eval checklist` and `soup eval behavior` emitted a neutral OK and exited 0 when given no `--evidence` - a green tick that means "not evaluated" and looks identical to a pass. Both now exit 3, and the message names what to supply. **What makes it a good fix is that it still discriminates**: measured through the real CLI, no evidence -> 3, passing evidence -> 0, failing evidence -> 2, and a JSON list, malformed JSON or a missing file each exit 3 naming the real problem. The two tests that pinned the old behaviour were renamed and TIGHTENED rather than deleted, each gaining both an exit-code and a message assertion ([#1123](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1123), [#1129](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1129))
  - The last three unguarded regex sinks, and the `diagnose/format.py` one is the sharpest: its guard WAS the denial of service, because it matched the untrusted pattern against `"a" * 128` before deciding anything. Measured on main with a ten-character input, `matches_regex('short text', '(a+)+b')` never returns; twelve pattern/sink combinations hang past a ten-second timeout there and refuse in under 0.06 s here, each naming its own field. They also proved the check DISCRIMINATES - `(?:q|k)+_proj`, `force.*attraction` and `\d+` all still match - and flipped the source-grep that asserted the probe's presence into one asserting its absence, so it cannot come back quietly ([#1137](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1137), [#1142](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1142))
  - Pinned the async-disk tier's page-lock recovery behaviourally: a real `AsyncDiskSource` built from a minimal on-disk shard, only its pinned staging allocation failing, driven through the real drain into the real VRAM probe — so replacing the drain with `pass` fails on `peak.oom is False`, the exact misreport of #1003. It complements a recorder-based test that proves the call and its order; this one proves the stale error is actually gone ([#1095](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1095), [#1098](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1098))
  - Published the STEP 14 control-versus-repair gate (`benchmarks/harness/variant2_gate.py`) that the H100 record could only describe. Review then found its wiring counter had been blind since #1010: `StreamedDecoderLayer.named_modules()` hides the wrapper carrying `n_dequant_forward`, so `wiring_distinct` could never be true. They now walk registered children, refuse to measure a repaired arm with nothing rewired, and pin both the whole-run verdict and `_run_arm` on CPU, with 14 of 17 mutations killed ([#379](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/379), [#1085](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1085))
  - The regex PII baseline behind `soup expect`, `soup data pii` and `soup data score` was **quadratic** on long `[\w.+-]` runs with no `@`: the email pattern restarted at every word boundary, so one 50 KB row of `1-1-1-...` took 14-20 s, paid again for every such row. They made a match start only where a run starts, keeping the rule that the local part needs a word character, and **pinned both sides of the trade**. An 8N-versus-N ratio test covers the speed: 59-66x on `main`, worst 11.8 of 300 repeats against a limit of 32 with the fix. A seeded differential against the old pattern, with the two known snippet differences named, covers the detections. Row flags are identical by construction, and in review 7.2 million exhaustive strings plus 100,000 random rows showed 0 disagreements. 7 of 7 mutations of the fix fail their tests ([#1329](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1329), [#1360](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1360))
  - Closed #1309, a setting that was accepted and then ignored: `training.gdpo_variant` loaded on `task: dpo` and `task: preference` and its description promised to replace the trainer's preference loss, but on trl >= 0.29 the `DPOTrainer.dpo_loss` hook it relied on is gone, so every variant trained exactly the default loss and nothing said so. They took the owner's "refuse" ruling and kept to it: a `mode="before"` field validator that refuses every non-null value, so `bogus`, `false`, `""` and `[]` get the same message as the real variants and the task and backend gates cannot mask it. **What makes it a good change**: every test the earlier EBFT refusal (#1279) had handed to this fix was inverted rather than deleted — loads-as-valid became refused, the two gate-unchanged tests became "refusal precedes the gate", the silent `False` from the legacy hook became a `ValueError` — and the refusal text names the nearest real alternative while saying it is not identical. 452 tests in the four touched files (44 fail on a clean `main`); 12 mutations of the validator, its message and the `live_wired` flags, 12 killed ([#1309](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1309), [#1515](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1515))
  - Took the `soup data score` slice of [#1448](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1448), an option that was validated, announced and then read by nothing: `--benchmarks` and `--threshold` were accepted and decontamination was always called with no texts, so the scorecard printed `Decontaminated 0` for any input. They added `--benchmark-file` and wired the supplied texts and the threshold to the real 8-gram kernel, refuse labels alone by name, and made the "not run" case say so instead of showing a zero. **What makes it worth keeping**: asked what the `-b` labels do once a file is given, they did not argue it — they put the answer in the help text, the docs, a printed note and a test — and every refusal (labels alone, malformed or non-object JSONL, a truncated file, an empty or unusable corpus) comes before a scorecard prints, so a bad corpus can no longer read as a clean result. 34 tests (22 fail on a clean `main`); 16 mutations of the wiring, the strict loader and the core validation, 16 killed ([#1448](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1448), [#1518](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1518))

- **k4its1t** ([@k4its1t](https://github.com/k4its1t))
  - First contribution, picking up where #497 stopped one day earlier. The dtype scanner's predicate already matched anything starting with `AutoModel`, so every `AutoModelFor*` load was covered and the surviving hole was **concrete, non-Auto** classes: `Qwen2VLForImageTextToText.from_pretrained(..., dtype=)` or `BertForMaskedLM.from_config(..., dtype=)` were invisible and would have shipped a kwarg that TypeErrors below the declared transformers floor. Four suffixes, a focused regression test each, both load methods. **Their own mutation claim was the coarse one -- removing all four suffixes makes four tests fail -- and removing them one at a time is stronger than they claimed**: each single removal produces 1 failed, 15 passed, so every named test is the sole guard for its own suffix rather than the four reddening together. The two things I check on any widening they got right: the four existing false-positive controls survive untouched (a guard that fires on correct code is one people delete), and all six deleted lines are a rename plus reworded docstrings, not a test. The rename `_is_transformers_model_load` -> `_is_dtype_guarded_model_load` describes the contract being enforced instead of implying every matched class comes straight from transformers. **Scope was not oversold, which is why it is worth writing down**: none of the four suffixes appears in `src/` today and the scanner reports zero live hits, so this is prospective -- Whisper loads as `WhisperForConditionalGeneration` and #497 already covered it. Timely prospective work, though: concrete vision classes are exactly what #488, #501 and #502 are moving through right now ([#500](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/500))

- **lesterppo** ([@lesterppo](https://github.com/lesterppo))
  - Reported #425 from a real Colab crash and fixed it themselves: on pre-Ampere cards (T4/P100/V100/GTX 16xx — the entire free Colab/Kaggle tier) fp16 training crashed at step 0 because peft creates LoRA adapters in the base checkpoint's bf16 dtype while the fp16 GradScaler needs fp32 gradients. The design decisions are the ones worth keeping: cast only trainable `*lora_*` params so full-FT paths (`lora.r: 0`, Spectrum, LISA) do not double trainable memory after the VRAM pre-flight has passed; call the shared helper from every trainer `train()` site rather than patching sft alone (#328's shape); and write the source-scanning test that makes a nineteenth call site a new row instead of a silent gap. They also opened #433 out of the review — the meta/non-meta peft finding someone else merged. Two weeks of silence after an accepted review, so the three mechanical items landed per the dated plan under a clearly-marked maintainer commit while everything that matters stayed theirs ([#429](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/429))

- **Pernav Jain** ([@here-2007](https://github.com/here-2007))
  - Replaced hard-coded eval-gate judge normalization with active-rubric min-max scaling, validation, and regression coverage ([#578](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/578))
  - Routed GRPO objective variants through TRL per-token log-probability computation with runtime-contract regression coverage ([#585](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/585))
  - Closed the abbreviated-IPv4 SSRF bypass one layer out from the `localhost` hole #598 had just fixed — `ipaddress.ip_address()` raises on `127.1`, `2130706433`, `0x7f000001` and `0177.0.0.1`, so the guard returned `False` and treated them as public hostnames. **They answered the question the issue could not**: I had filed it saying I could not tell from a Windows box whether glibc actually resolves those forms, and declined to claim a live bypass. They measured it — glibc 2.39 resolves every one, `2852039166` reaching `169.254.169.254`, while Windows returns `gaierror` for all of them. Using `socket.inet_aton` rather than a hand-rolled parser is the load-bearing choice: across 26 exotic forms the guard and the resolver diverge only *together*, because both track the platform's own parser. They also found and fixed a second bypass nobody had reported (`--slack-url https://2852039166/` reaching cloud metadata through `webhooks.py`) and closed the trailing-dot FQDN form raised as non-blocking. Review found three changelog defects — a direct `CHANGELOG.md` edit that would have published #577 **twice** and added a duplicate `### Fixed` heading, and a fragment filed under **another contributor's** PR number — and all three were gone in one push ([#600](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/600), [#604](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/604))
  - Closed the hostname-indirection bypass in the telemetry SSRF guard: a host that was not a literal IP passed regardless of what it resolved to, so `10.0.0.1.nip.io` (real DNS -> `10.0.0.1`) and `localtest.me` (-> `127.0.0.1`) were both accepted while the literal `10.0.0.1` was refused. **They pushed back on the design before building it, and were right to.** Handed a sketch that resolved every custom endpoint, they pointed out that `socket.getaddrinfo` takes no timeout in Python and that `send_telemetry` calls `httpx.post` synchronously, so an unbounded resolver call would block air-gapped runs — and proposed a tiered design where a trusted allowlist short-circuits before any lookup. Verified by measurement rather than by reading: with `getaddrinfo` patched to raise, the default endpoint still validates with **zero** DNS calls. They also took the one correction handed back — **no `lru_cache`**, because caching a *permissive* verdict for the life of a process means a rebinding attacker need only win once — and they documented the DNS layer as defence in depth rather than as SSRF prevention, which it cannot be across two separate lookups. Each of the three tiers is killed by a named test, and the one place that margin is a single test wide is stated in the merge commit rather than glossed. The self-hosted-PostHog test was **adapted, not deleted** — mocked to a public IP, still asserting `True`, so the self-hoster path stays covered ([#599](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/599), [#624](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/624))
  - Filed #1543 with both causes already diagnosed, each of which checks out against the hub release notes and `refuse_if_data_parallel`, and took it themselves: the proof notebook's install cell pulled `huggingface_hub` 1.32, whose shared blob store soup-cli 0.75.0 refuses (#1509), and the notebook left two CUDA devices visible, so layer streaming refused to start under `nn.DataParallel`. They capped the hub below 1.32 with a comment that says when to drop it and set one visible device before torch is imported. **In the second round they took the guarded `setdefault`, so a caller who chose their own card keeps it, and added the fragment and the two tests the first review asked for.** Only cell 3 changes (outputs identical in all 19 cells); the two new tests are 2 failed / 7 passed on `main`, and 4 of 4 targeted mutations are caught ([#1543](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1543), [#1545](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1545))

- **Samarth Mukhija** ([@Samearth17](https://github.com/Samearth17))
  - First contribution, and it answers the awkward half of #379: the repo could not reproduce its own benchmark records, because most of those harnesses only ever existed in a scratchpad on a machine that is gone. They published the upstream bitsandbytes #2034 NF4 reproducer as a standalone, download-free script and left the methodology and the existing table row untouched — a reproducibility slice that reproduces, rather than a rewrite. **Review blocked on the one thing that matters for a harness: it exited `0` whether or not the defect was present**, so it could not distinguish the mechanism it claims to demonstrate from a machine where nothing is wrong. They fixed it in both directions — an explicit `--bypass-pool` negative control, *and* an ordinary invocation that now requires the NF4 mismatch, so a removed `pool.acquire()` fails instead of passing quietly. Verified on real CUDA at merge: normal run reproduces on 6/8 layers with the bf16 control clean on 8/8; cutting `pool.acquire()` exits 1 on the merged head and exited **0** on the pre-fix commit. They also said plainly that they could not run it themselves — no GPU — instead of implying they had ([#589](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/589))
  - Published the layer-streaming bit-exactness harness, which [#379](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/379) had wanted since it was filed: the ~20 harnesses behind the v0.72.x gate records lived only in a scratchpad on a machine that is gone, so the published numbers could be read but not re-measured. **The control they added unprompted is what makes the tool worth trusting** — `--skip-lora-sync` forces the comparison to fail, and on real CUDA it reports `max_abs_diff=3.687500e+00` and exits 1 against a clean run's `0.0` and exit 0. For an instrument whose entire output is *these two agree to 0.0*, being able to say *no* is the whole question, and `make_non_vacuous_lora` closes the other axis so "gradients exact" cannot mean "both sides are zero". Two rounds, both on the same defect class: as submitted the harness could not complete a single run, because `copy_lora` keyed on raw `named_parameters()`, which is not canonical on a streamed model — every wrapped layer carries an extra `.inner.` segment — and the first repair reached for a suffix fallback that also cannot work, since `.inner.` sits in the MIDDLE of the name. **A maintainer suggestion was withdrawn in their favour**: the per-parameter raise they wrote catches a *partial* mismatch and names the tensor, where the `assert_canonical_parameters_intersect` they were asked for only catches an empty intersection ([#379](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/379), [#597](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/597))
  - Ended the five-release blind spot from [#596](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/596): `test_sft_smoke` and `test_dpo_smoke` carry a module-wide `pytest.mark.smoke` against an `addopts` of `-m 'not smoke'`, so they ran in **neither** the default suite nor CI — which is how one of them stayed red for five releases. The new `pytorch-smoke` job runs them for real (the CI log names both in its warnings, so it is a genuine model load and real training steps, not a collection that happened to succeed), and both halves of `-o addopts= -m smoke` are load-bearing: without the first, the job would fail on `--cov-fail-under=77` rather than on the tests. **The revision worth recording is the second one.** The first named `tests/test_smoke_train.py`, which left `TestOnlineDpoTrainLogsReward`'s two smoke-marked tests running in none of the three places — deselected by default, out of scope for `mlx-smoke`, outside the job's path — measured as `-m smoke tests/` collecting 5 against the job's 3. That is #596's own sentence surviving inside the job written to end it. They dropped the path argument, so the job now covers the **marker** and the next contributor who marks something `smoke` gets it run without knowing this job exists. 2m01s, of which 13s is the tests, in parallel with 32-minute Windows cells ([#596](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/596), [#601](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/601)).
  - `measure_gemm_tflops` hard-coded `torch.bfloat16`, so the streaming pre-flight benchmarked a dtype pre-Ampere cards never use and under-forecast throughput on exactly the hardware the free tier runs on. **The trap is why this looked correct for so long**: `torch.cuda.is_bf16_supported()` returns True on a T4 through emulation, so the obvious check agrees with the wrong answer — the probe now resolves through `resolve_stream_dtype`, the same function the streaming path itself uses. Review took two rounds and the second is the interesting one: the first version's panel assertion did not exist, so a mutation deleting the dtype from the user-visible string survived; their new parametrized panel test kills it, **and kills it on a CPU-only run**, so all nine CI cells can catch it rather than only a GPU box. They drove the pre-Ampere direction end to end without a pre-Ampere card by faking exactly one thing — `is_bf16_supported(including_emulation=False)` returning False, which is a T4's truth — and showed the real `soup train` printing `measured on this card now using float16`. The docs line now matches the emitted string byte for byte. Four removed test lines, all audited in-place edits, one of them a strengthening ([#617](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/617), [#648](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/648))
  - Closed the dependency floor that v0.74.0 shipped knowing it was wrong. That release declared `torch>=2.5.0` alongside `trl>=0.29`, and the two cannot both be satisfied: at torch 2.5.1 `torch.distributed.fsdp.FSDPModule` does not exist, trl 0.29 fails to import, and DPO, KTO, GRPO and BCO are all dead. pip cannot see it because **trl declares no torch dependency at all**, and CI never saw it because `>=2.5.0` always resolved to the newest torch. The release deliberately shipped evidence instead of a bump, because 2.5.1 was measured to fail and 2.6 was not measured to work — **and answering that objection is what this PR is.** Their first version raised the numbers and made the pinned-stack CI job install torch 2.6.0; review found that green proved *installation* and not *compatibility*, since not one of the five test files that job ran imported trl. They added a step that asserts `FSDPModule` exists and then actually does `import trl` / `from trl import DPOTrainer, KTOTrainer`, which is the failure this issue is about. The guard was then shown to discriminate rather than assumed to: run against this box, still on the old floor, the assert fires and the import fails — so it rejects exactly the version it exists to reject, while the job is green on the version being adopted. #651's hardest criterion, a test catching the "two numbers in two files nobody compares" drift, is met in **both** directions: reverting either the pyproject floor or the CI pin alone fails a test. Eight removed test lines, all audited, none weakened ([#651](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/651), [#717](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/717))
  - The NF4 bit-exactness test compared a streamed model against a resident reference taking a **different bitsandbytes kernel** — production streams through `install_dequant_forward` (`dequantize_4bit + F.linear`) while the reference used the fused `MatMul4Bit` — so a failure measured a kernel-path difference rather than a streaming defect. **What makes this one stand out is what they did NOT do**: the assertions stay at exact equality, no tolerance, no `approx`, no skip, no xfail. Two failing arms turning green is exactly the shape a weakening takes, and this is the opposite of one — the reference moves onto the same compute path, which strictly narrows what the test measures. It also handed over the root cause of [#776](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/776): isolated with streaming removed from the experiment entirely, two resident NF4 models with byte-identical weights differ by 6.10e-04 in float16 and 4.39e-03 in bfloat16 purely on which bitsandbytes 4-bit path they take, so streaming was never the variable. The `layer_stream_runtime.py` edit was confirmed docstring-only by comparing ASTs with docstrings stripped, so no card's behaviour changes ([#776](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/776), [#857](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/857))
  - `soup doctor` parsed the CUDA version out of `nvidia-smi` with a pattern that did not match modern driver headers, so a current NVIDIA driver read as *no version* and the wheel advice fell back to `cu121` — **confidently wrong rather than absent** ([#827](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/827)). Measured on this box (RTX 5070 Laptop, driver 616.92, header `CUDA UMD Version: 13.4`): `main` gives `driver=None, tag=cu121`; theirs gives `driver=(13,4), tag=cu132`. The half that makes it *safe* is the second one — an unrecognised driver now returns `None` instead of guessing, and both call sites guard on the **tag**, so no advice is printed rather than wrong advice. Their mutation is the best kind: dropping `if tag else None` emits `whl/None` **from the real wheel table**, which is what proves the test drives production code instead of a stub of itself — the defect I had blocked an earlier revision for. ([#883](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/883))
  - Published the measurement harness behind the layer-streaming NF4 cost record, which until now could not be reproduced by anyone - including by us. **The property that earned the merge is that it REFUSES rather than guesses**: on a model too small to exhibit the mechanism it exits 1 saying the control never reproduced a gradient mismatch, instead of printing a cost for something it did not observe. Two rounds, and the second turned `--self-test` from a check that declared PASS on ANY exception into a real negative acceptance test - it now catches the PRODUCTION assertion in `layer_stream_runtime.py` being neutered while the harness itself is untouched, which is the stronger of the two directions. The unverifiable half is stated in the record rather than omitted: the ~20 GiB figure is the original record's, because the timing path needs a 32B checkpoint and more VRAM than the review box has ([#379](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/379), [#1121](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1121))

Review on real hardware: sm_120 output matched `nvidia-smi` row by row, six mutations killed, 59 passed on the merged tree ([#829](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/829), [#947](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/947))
  - Published the STEP 2b GRADDIFF measurement, the two-line result that started the 32B investigation (gradients match after one backward, yet the curves do not reproduce themselves). The bar for a reconstruction is that it can say no, and the review rounds were about exactly that: the success condition demanded a resident curve the record had printed as non-identical, the success branch could not be reached at any commit because every tree that can import the harness already carries the #331 repair, a NaN streamed curve counted as the defect, and eight mutations of the verdict left `--self-test` green. **What they did with it**: imported the historical control from `mechanism_cost` and restored it in a `finally`, made NaN an invalid measurement with its own exit code, stated the departures from the record (fresh adapter and optimizer per curve; the record never explains its C = False) as differences rather than causes, and took the maintainer's guard tests in as their own file — all seven mutations that still survived the second review are now killed. 33 CPU tests drive the real `main()` and `run_measurement()`; 19 of 26 mutations of the harness fail them and the 7 survivors are non-verdict guards that are named, not hidden. They said plainly that the CUDA path needs a 32B checkpoint they did not have ([#379](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/379), [#1170](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1170))
  - Brought the "Reproducing" section of `gate-h100-validation.md` in line with the harnesses that are now published: it said no script was in the repo and linked none, so a reader following the record was told there was nothing to run. **They re-synced it the same day `graddiff.py` merged and made their own note stale**, which is the discipline that keeps a dated correction true. Each of the three reconstructions is labelled as one, the unpublished rows still point at #379, and every other cell is left as the record had it. Checked by a 19-check script (19/19, fails on `main`), 11 mutations of the section (11 killed) and 104 resolving links ([#1348](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1348), [#1364](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1364))
  - `data.format: pre_tokenized` loaded with `data.add_new_tokens`, `data.new_special_tokens`, `data.prompt_strategy` or a non-text `modality`, although `soup data preprocess` never applies any of them, so the run trained on cached ids that silently lacked the transformation. They refused each combination at config load (three `DataConfig` checks plus a `SoupConfig` validator placed after the quantization resolver), updated the `NOT_PREPROCESS_KEY_FIELDS` reasons and the docs, and after review rewrote the one refusal that stated the opposite reason and pinned it in the test. Re-run by the maintainer: the 6 new cases fail on `main`, 15 of 16 mutations killed, 1452 related tests pass ([#1273](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1273), [#1363](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1363))
  - Published the STEP 9 mechanism reproducer behind the layer-streaming NF4 record, the five-arm harness that shows the defect is aliasing of the recycled buffers and that neither the packed weights nor the quantisation state alone is enough. It took six review rounds: as first submitted it crashed on its first pooled acquire; the repair then overflowed, so its "exact" column was `inf == inf` arithmetic that happened to land on the record's counts; the clone baseline was compared against a copy of itself and could not fail; a non-finite run under `--bypass-pool` exited 1, the same code as "negative control caught"; and five verdict rules were reached by no test. **The part worth keeping is the last two rounds**: they gave the overflow its own exception and exit code 3, made the full clone match a resident run that bypasses the pools (so a clone that is 1.5x off or drifts between repetitions now exits 1), and then wrote five per-arm tests that each drive the real `main()` to the rule they name. 17 CPU tests; 14 of 17 mutations of the harness fail them, and the 3 survivors are named rather than hidden — one verdict message that a neighbouring rule makes redundant for the exit code, one display-only count and one equivalent mutant. They said plainly that they had no CUDA device to run it on ([#379](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/379), [#1119](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1119))
  - `soup infer` was documented as batch inference but generated one prompt at a time. They added an opt-in `--batch-size` that renders each prompt through the shared template path, left-pads, generates once per batch and writes rows back in input order, with token counts that stop at the first EOS or pad, so short rows are not inflated, plus a refusal of `--cuda-graphs` above batch 1. **The review rounds were about proving order, and they got there**: their prompt-dependent pairing test, where the fake model answers from each row's own content with mixed lengths and early stops, kills the length-sort and reversed-batch mutations that position-only tests could not see. 79 tests in the two infer files (21 fail on `main`), 14 of 14 behavioural mutations killed ([#765](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/765), [#1083](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1083))
  - Published the STEP 2b determinism reconstruction (`benchmarks/harness/determinism.py`): forward twice, backward rep 1 vs rep 3 and a trained 5-step curve twice, streamed NF4 against a resident NF4 reference, with the record's asymmetry as the verdict. Over four rounds it went from a validator of hand-written JSON (where a `null` read as the defect) to an in-process measurement. **The fix that mattered** was ordering: the historical control has to be installed before the streamed model is built, or the harness measures the repaired path. It now goes through `build_streamed_arm(..., "control", ...)` and restores the control in a `finally`, and a `main()` test pins both, so swapping in the clone arm or dropping the restore fails it. Six of its 20 CPU tests drive the real measurement path. Not run on CUDA ([#379](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/379), [#1286](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1286))

- **chadetov** ([@chadetov](https://github.com/chadetov))
  - First contribution, and it is the kind that is easy to file as a one-line fixture tweak and much harder to *diagnose*: `test_sft_smoke` had been red since v0.36.0, and they traced why nobody knew. `pyproject`'s `addopts` carry `-m 'not smoke'`, and the only smoke job in CI is `mlx-smoke`, which selects `-k mlx_sft_smoke` on an install that asserts the PyTorch stack is **absent** — so `test_sft_smoke` and `test_dpo_smoke` run in neither the default suite nor CI, which is how a fixture drifted out of sync with a hard error added five releases earlier. **The scope discipline is what got it merged unchanged**: the MLX fixture carries the same `format: chatml` line and they left it alone, because it reaches the MLX trainer rather than `sft_format.py` and is the one smoke test CI actually runs and passes — verified, not assumed. They also declined to wire the PyTorch smoke tests into CI in the same PR and said why (model downloads, runner minutes), leaving the call to the maintainer instead of taking it ([#594](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/594)). Separately, and on the same day, they measured `utils/log_level.py`'s branch coverage on #273 and filed [#595](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/595) — four code sites quoting a probe-denominated VRAM ratio under a "real peak" label, plus a schema field description still carrying a justification the gate record had explicitly withdrawn

- **UmranPros** ([@umran666](https://github.com/umran666))
  - Caught that four code sites quoted a **probe-denominated** VRAM ratio under the label "the real peak" — `0.830x` is the formula against the *probe*, while against the real training run the same shape is `0.934x`. The corroboration is what makes it a finding rather than a guess: the quoted series turns over between seq 3072 and 4096, which the gate record identifies as the **probe's** crossover, not the training path's. They also found the `stream_vram_probe` field description still carrying a justification the gate record had explicitly **withdrawn** — that the probe under-measures preference losses "badly enough to make the gate unsafe", when it measures +13.5% HIGH — contradicting the validator 1,900 lines below it in the same file. **Asked to keep both series with their denominators named rather than swap one number for another, they did, and explained the gap** (the probe runs 12.5-14.3% above the real step, which is what makes it safe as a gate) — dropping the probe series would have left no record in the code that it over-reads. When review found their revision had kept an unsourced determinism claim while vaguening it, they went and found the source (#395) rather than deleting the sentence or leaving it ([#595](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/595), [#605](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/605))
  - Pinned both reward-hack ladder thresholds with **n-minus-1 controls**, which is the difference between a test that discriminates and one that merely goes green — an assertion that the ladder "fires eventually" passes for an off-by-one, and both of mine died on their new controls. They also reconciled the two ladders' documentation with what the code does (`_run_bang_bang` has no rollback rung at all; rollback lives only in `_run_pid`, and the schema enforces that by rejecting `reward_hack_rollback` for every other mitigation), each claim checkable against a named line rather than asserted. **The scope discipline is the part worth copying**: `Part of #371`, never `Fixes`, with item 1 explicitly deferred to GPU hardware they do not have and item 3 credited to another contributor's PR — on an issue where a test-only change could easily have been dressed up as closing it. Source diff is +22/-2 and comments only, which I verified by comparing ASTs with docstrings stripped rather than by reading ([#371](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/371), [#611](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/611))
  - Added the `kimi-k2.6-grpo` recipe, and **pinned the model id on two independent surfaces** — mutating `RecipeMeta.model` alone and mutating the YAML `base:` alone each fail a named test, so an edit to one that forgets the other cannot pass. That is precisely the guard a competing PR for the same issue lacked, and it was here before anyone asked. When the red Windows cell turned out to be [#382](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/382) rather than their code, they diagnosed it correctly from the faulthandler signature instead of retrying the job. On learning the issue was already covered by an older PR they had not been able to see — the issue carried no claim comment — they offered to defer immediately and said they would search before claiming in future, which is the habit the whole collision cost a day to establish. The rebase this needed was caused by merge ordering rather than by anything in the work: two recipe PRs bump the same catalog counter and append a test class at the same point, so whichever landed second was always going to conflict ([#281](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/281), [#614](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/614))
  - Took `utils/log_level.py` to 100% branch coverage, and **the PR is more correct than the issue it closes**. [#273](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/273) described the uncovered branch `74->73` as "an existing handler whose `_soup_log_tier` MATCHES the requested tier", with the repro "call `setup_logging` twice with the same tier" — that is a different edge, `75->73`, and following it would not have closed the branch. Line 74 is `if getattr(existing, "_soup_log_tier", None) is not None:` and the missing case is a FOREIGN handler, one Soup did not install. They read the source rather than the ticket. Non-vacuity demonstrated rather than asserted: under the load-bearing mutation the 15 pre-existing tests ALL pass, so the two new tests are the only guard on that branch. The four deleted lines are an in-place strengthening of `test_idempotent` and it earns its place — restoring the old version on top of a mutation that destroys and rebuilds the handler every call gives a fully green suite, because the old assertion compared handler COUNTS, which that mutation preserves ([#273](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/273), [#631](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/631))
  - Raised the `[train]` torch floor to the one that actually binds, and **took the review's duller design over their own clever one**. Their first version read the floor from installed metadata so only one copy could exist; the shipped version keeps a literal in `doctor.py` pinned by a test against `pyproject.toml`. The reason is the reusable part: dist-info records what was *installed*, not what the repo *declares*, and those diverge exactly when it matters — measured on a box whose install predated the raise, `soup doctor` printed `>=2.0.0` beside a declared `2.5.0`, marked the row **OK**, and emitted `pip install -U 'torch>=2.0.0'`, advising a user to install *below* the real floor. Two guards also did not survive review as written and they fixed both: the drift test compared declared-against-**installed** transformers, so reverting pyproject to `2.3.0` passed on any box below 5.16.1 while the file's own comment claimed that mutation failed by name; and the guard's stated home, the `transformers-floor` job, never collected it — it ran only in the ordinary matrix, and only by coincidence of 5.16.1 being the newest release on PyPI, so 5.16.2 shipping would have made it dormant with no repo change and no signal. That one is verifiable in a log rather than argued: the job goes from `36 passed` to `39`. Reverting the pyproject floor, changing the `DEPS` literal, and reintroducing a second declaration through a module constant are each killed by two named tests — the last of those survived two earlier rounds ([#636](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/636), [#641](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/641)).
  - Made `soup sweep --dry-run` validate instead of returning before the config loads, and **then reproduced a claim I had publicly said did not reproduce** — which is the part worth recording. Their first version asserted that every real sweep on Python 3.10 died with `issubclass() arg 1 must be a class`; I could not make that happen and blocked on it. They came back with the discriminating variable, and it was never the Python patch level: **pydantic 2.10.6's `ModelMetaclass` does not define `__subclasscheck__` and inherits ABCMeta's, which raises, while 2.13.4 defines a tolerant one**, so the PEP-585 alias never reaches `abc.__subclasscheck__` on a current stack. Confirmed on my own box — the quirk `isinstance(dict[str,float], type) is True` is live there, and `issubclass` returns `False` rather than raising. Both observations were the same fact. They also characterised the trigger rather than leaving it abstract: `training.preference_loss_weights: Optional[dict[str, float]]`, reached through a full `model_dump()`, i.e. *every* config. The guard that replaced the environment-dependent test is the model answer — an ABCMeta stand-in monkeypatched over `pydantic.BaseModel` recreates the pre-override metaclass, so removing the filter dies on any 3.10 box whatever pydantic is installed, verified on the masked stack where every earlier test had passed with the filter gone. Twelve tests became fourteen, all through the real CLI with a clean-config control; zero deleted test lines ([#642](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/642), [#645](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/645)).
  - Settled a disputed bug by measuring it, and **the answer was that my own status note was wrong** — which is the harder thing to deliver than a fix. [#623](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/623) said `stream_pin: false` was accepted and ignored; they showed the field **did not exist at the v0.73.3 tag** the reporter was running (`grep -c stream_pin` -> 0, against 18 for `stream_layers` as a control), so pydantic's `extra="ignore"` dropped the key, the default pinned path ran, and the reported OOM is what a *correctly pinned* run looks like. The wiring is honoured end to end on `main`. **The PR is still worth merging, and for a reason that is the point of it**: nothing guarded the chain below the layer every existing test stops at. Mutating the deepest hop so the flag is *recorded* and then ignored at allocation (`pin_memory=self.pinned` -> `True`) leaves **169 pre-existing streaming tests green**; this file is the only thing that catches it — because it asserts `is_pinned()` on the real allocation rather than the recorded attribute, which is what an attribute assertion would have missed. Tests only, no source change, correctly no changelog fragment, and a non-empty-store guard so the CUDA test cannot pass while measuring nothing ([#623](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/623), [#647](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/647)).
  - `benchmarks/harness/issue361_nf4_throughput.py` pins the v0.72.2 8B NF4 protocol — batch 1, S=512, 50 steps after 10 warm-up, `PagedAdamW8bit`, a same-session GEMM ceiling — so a post-#331 reading is comparable to the record rather than being a differently-shaped run. **The PR ships no number, deliberately, and is worth more for it.** The original figures were measured on an RTX 3050 Laptop 4 GB; the dev box is now an RTX 5070 Blackwell with twice the VRAM, so a run there would be a change in the INSTRUMENT rather than evidence about repaired code. They built the protocol and left the measurement for the card that can answer the question — which is the distinction the whole `benchmarks/` folder exists to protect. All eight review items closed, seven mutations and seven kills, `Refs #361` confirmed correct via GraphQL rather than assumed. **The conflict resolution is the other half**: rebased across eleven merges, their row inserted in `benchmarks/README.md` without dropping a word of the neighbouring `stream_probe.py` row, and `README.tr.md` re-stamped last as guard #852 requires ([#361](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/361), [#1014](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1014))
  - Measured the post-#331 8B NF4 row on their own RTX 3050 Laptop 4 GB: 208.6 tok/s at 1935 MHz in a 2.40 GB peak. They **published the unflattering reading instead of the headline**: the raw 1.74x over the pre-repair 119.6 tok/s is a 2x clock difference, per unit clock the repaired code is about 14% slower (68% -> 59% of the same-session ceiling), and the record attributes that shortfall to none of the three changes it lists. Four review rounds tightened the provenance. The run date was recovered from the output file's timestamps, and the committed JSON was tied to that file byte for byte. The two laptops behind the two rows are told apart, and an unverifiable console line was removed rather than defended ([#361](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/361), [#1103](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1103))
  - `soup train --find-lr` answered every failure of the live sweep, a missing base model id, a YAML syntax error in the config, a model or dataset that could not be loaded, or no `torch`, with a curve shaped by the LR schedule alone, wrote a `recommended_lr` from it and exited 0, so two different nonexistent ids reported the identical number. They deleted the fabrication rather than hiding it behind a flag: a new `LrSweepUnavailableError` beside `SweepTooShortError` makes every such case a refusal that names the cause, exits 1 and writes no report. The control test, a sweep that can run still writing its 8-point report, is what stops the refusal from swallowing a real measurement, and they disclosed the side effect that an install without `torch` now exits 1 before being asked. **The review round is the part worth copying**: five items came back, and each was closed with the mutation run to prove it. Removing the loader message, removing the escaped print and deleting the `SweepTooShortError` re-raise each survived the first round and each now fails a test; 10 of 10 mutations fail at least one test, and the new file fails 3 on `main`. The bug was found by @mohdUwaish59 while reproducing #1189 ([#1203](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1203), [#1334](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1334))
  - Answered #1440, the defect that Soup's own trace stream never yielded a preference pair: `parse_soup_serve` read the signal only from a nested `feedback.rating` that no producer writes, while `soup ingest` writes a top-level `signal`, and no parser ever set `edited_output`, so the round trip ended in a green `Wrote 0`. **What earned the merge is the scope and the clamp**: they fixed only the consumer half and said in the issue which half was left (thumbs into the `soup serve` trace log), changed `Closes` to `Refs` so the issue stays open, and clamped the top-level `signal` to a closed vocabulary, so the new "Signals present" line can only ever print constants — hostile `signal` values (lists, dicts, ANSI and OSC escapes, a 100,000-character string) read as `none`, verified by execution. Their 16 tests fail 11 on the parent commit; against 19 mutations 15 are killed, including the issue's own (read only `feedback`: 10 failed). Review found four surviving mutations (the no-signal branch of the message, `none` leaking into the present-signals list, precedence on a conflicting `feedback.rating`, a whitespace-only `edited_output`) and they closed all four by adding tests, **re-running each mutation against their own file rather than quoting mine**, and also gated the `soup ingest` hint to the one format that reads a top-level `signal` and corrected the docs to say who writes which signal. 414 passed across 9 related files on the merged tree ([#1440](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1440), [#1498](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1498))
  - `task: ppo` loaded `reward_hack_detector`, `reward_hack_mitigation` and `echo_trap_enabled` and then ran them blind: the trl PPO trainer takes no `reward_funcs`, so the shared signal buffer was never written, and because the buffer object existed the detector's `on_log` fallback returned early too. **They chose the refusal over a half-wired feed on exactly that second observation** (a controller that cannot fire is worse than a config that stops), refused the precise set `rl_callbacks_need_buffer` uses before `datasets` or any model loads, and left `reward_fn`-on-PPO and the real buffer feed out of the PR rather than widen it. **The round worth recording is the second one**: their first tests drove the helper and never `setup()`, so deleting the one call line left 12 of 12 green; they added setup-level tests with stubbed loaders and a flagless control, a drift test between the refusal and `rl_callbacks_need_buffer`, and moved the `datasets` import below the refusal so the flag is reported first. 21 tests; 11 mutations (call removed, condition inverted, each flag dropped, refusal after each load, never/always raising, no flag names, a fourth buffer flag) all killed, where the first round's call-removal survived ([#1441](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1441), [#1514](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1514))
  - Found that `training.preference_loss_weights` never blended anything: no trl 0.29 trainer puts log-probs on the batch, so the wrapper fell back to `primary_loss * primary_weight` and `{dpo: 0.7, simpo: 0.3}` trained DPO alone at 0.7 while printing a blend. They measured it on a real trainer (`Loss: 0.4852` = 0.7 x 0.693147), deleted the fallback and its dead helper so the run refuses naming the terms, and said in the issue exactly what the PR did not do instead of letting `Closes` over-claim, then changed it to `Refs` when review asked. Review closed with a docs rewrite (the page had claimed a true weighted sum), a whole-string assertion that kills the mutation that had survived (`missing = sorted(snapshot)`), and a real-trainer test that fails on `main` 2 of 2 and with the fallback restored 2 of 2. Verified on the merged tree: 183 passed; fallback restored / over-broad missing list / empty computed list / partial blend accepted / wrong exception type each caught. Partial progress: the real blend is still open on the issue ([#1425](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1425), [#1513](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1513))
  - `task: simpo` trained every LoRA tensor to NaN behind a logged loss of `0.0` when a row's `chosen` outran `data.max_length`, because trl's CPO truncation empties the shorter answer and SimPO's length-normalised log-probability becomes 0/0. They read the slice out of the installed trl source, pinned it with a canary, and refused the run before the first step instead of repairing anyone's dataset. **The review found their first predicate refusing pairs that train fine, and what they did with it is the part worth recording:** they had re-applied trl's truncation formula to rows trl had already truncated (a 64/14 pair, measured on real trl at loss 3.995 with no NaN, was being refused), and instead of loosening a threshold they rewrote the check to the one question that survives truncation (does a side have no trainable label left), moved every fixture to the post-truncation shape, and wrote down why their own fixtures had hidden it. The wiring is pinned twice, by real-trl `setup()` tests on a tiny CPU model and by a source pin for machines where trl cannot import a CPO config; 16 of 19 new tests fail on `main`, 9 of 9 mutations killed ([#1208](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1208), [#1500](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1500))
  - `preference_loss_weights` could not blend on trl 0.29 (every blend stopped at step 0 after #1513). They captured the per-sequence log-probs trl's CPO/ORPO trainers already compute in `concatenated_forward` and restored the response length, so the sum-based kernels round-trip to trl's averages. They also moved each term's hyperparameters from the primary trainer to the config. A `simpo` + `orpo` blend now trains, independently of YAML key order, and the setup line says the terms are margin-only. Verified on a real CPOTrainer/ORPOTrainer; 220 tests across six related files; 7 of 9 mutations killed ([#1425](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1425), [#1556](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1556))

- **Aravind S** ([@ARAVIND281](https://github.com/ARAVIND281))
  - Measured the layer-streaming VRAM fit on a **second GPU and software stack** — an A10G 23 GB on Ubuntu with torch 2.13 / transformers 5.16.1 / trl 0.29.1, against the RTX 3050 laptop on Windows and torch 2.5.1 that every previous figure came from. All three findings are constraints on an open question rather than extra data points beside it: the v0.73.1 long-sequence under-prediction **does not reproduce** (flat 1.1621-1.1694 across seq 2048-6144 where the RTX series reads 0.787x at 6144), the logits loss term measures **12.000000 exactly** and invariant across vocabulary and torch version — so the shipped 14's `+2` retained copy, which #327 has never identified, demonstrably does not happen here — and the SDPA hypothesis #395 names as leading is **not supported**, with the math backend included as a positive control that IS expensive enough (3.27 GB at seq 6144) to prove the measurement could have found it. **They retracted their own headline figure mid-review** — a back-solved 11.83 B/element — and then enforced the retraction in CI rather than in prose: writing 11.83 back into the constant fails a named test. The discarded first sweep is kept in the record because its failure is silent: `data.max_length` truncates rather than pads, so short rows run at their own length while the prediction keeps climbing, producing a clean-looking over-prediction that is entirely an artifact and would mislead the next person identically ([#395](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/395), [#610](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/610))
  - Closed the coverage gap in #370, whose point is that the gap did not merely miss a bug — it produced a **wrong localisation**. During the #328 diagnosis "only `[dpo]` and `[kto]` failed" read as a sharp finding about the reference forward; it was wrong, because the class was parametrized over those two only. ORPO and SimPO are genuinely reference-free and fail identically. **The acceptance criterion they satisfied is the one most PRs skip**: the test had to turn red when the fix is reverted, verified by reverting rather than assumed — and they published both revert forms with results. Reproduced at merge on a *different* stack than theirs (RTX 3050 / torch 2.5.1 / trl 0.19.1 against their A10G / torch 2.13 / trl 0.29.1): baseline 8 passed, dropping `and not stream_layers` gives 8 failed with the issue's exact `Tensor on device cuda:0 is not on the expected device meta!`, on all four losses — which makes the #328 failure **not** torch-2.13-specific, stronger than the PR claimed. They also split it into its own class rather than adding arms to the VRAM test, reasoning that hanging regression cover off a memory assertion means a later refactor deletes it silently ([#370](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/370), [#609](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/609))

Asked for the one gap both PRs shared — neither tested the `serve.py` call site, so deleting the tokenizer wiring left every test green while production fell back to the legacy prompt — they added `TestServeMiiCallSite`, which drives the real CLI with the pipeline factory and uvicorn patched and asserts on the kwargs `build_mii_app` receives, **and verified it against the mutation rather than assuming**.
