- **Siddhardha Nanda** ([@SID-6921](https://github.com/SID-6921))
  - First contribution, and it found a live SSRF bypass while doing the tidy-up it was filed as. `utils/tracing.py`'s `_is_private_ip` predated the #604 fix and was never folded into it, so `validate_otlp_endpoint` accepted `https://2852039166:4317` and `https://0xa9fea9fe:4317` — both `169.254.169.254`, the cloud-metadata address — while refusing the dotted-quad form. **The blocking finding from round one is fixed and I verified it by mutation into real files rather than by reading**: six duplicates reintroduced, six caught, including the leading-underscore spelling that defeated the previous guard and two placed outside `utils/`, which the whole-tree walk now reaches; on the unmodified tree it reports zero offenders, because a guard that fires on correct code is one people delete. Zero deleted test lines. **Two claims in the PR did not survive measurement and I corrected them myself rather than opening a third round**: the consolidation is not "strictly more restrictive" — 468 differential probes show the reserved/multicast clauses change no caller's outcome, since `hf.py` and `hubs.py` raise on both branches of their `if` — and `loop_stages` is marginally more permissive, though every newly-accepted spelling canonicalises to an address it already allowed. Being wrong in a PR body is ordinary; the fix belongs in the changelog, which ships verbatim to users, not in another round-trip ([#616](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/616), [#625](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/625))
  - Implemented the recipe-config snapshot guard, and the number that justifies it is the one they measured rather than argued: changing ONE schema default (`dpo_beta` 0.1 -> 0.2) turns **149 named recipe tests red** on the branch and **nothing** red on `main`. That is [#621](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/621)'s exposure 2 — a one-line schema edit silently retuning recipes nobody touched — caught exactly. **What makes it trustworthy is what it deliberately does NOT catch**: deleting a recipe line that duplicates a default still kills nothing, because the issue excluded that direction, so the guard did not quietly answer a question it was told to leave alone. Five review follow-ups landed in one push and none was done to the letter while missing the point — the one worth copying is the non-vacuity controls, now exercising the diff on synthetic dicts so they stay GREEN while the real tests go red, which is what makes "did the guard break, or did the data move?" answerable from a CI log. They also pre-empted this repo's `core.autocrlf` fixture trap in the docstring before review raised it, and closed the one item review left open — regeneration writing CRLF on Windows — by adding `newline="\n"` on top of the `.gitattributes` belt. Verified byte-exact: `git status --porcelain` empty, 0 CRLF. Zero deleted test lines. Analysis, measurement and the option list are @Srinivasan8888's in [#621](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/621) — they offered to implement whichever option I picked and waited on an answer I did not give for two days, which is how the work ended up split ([#621](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/621), [#637](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/637))
  - Delta-encoded the snapshot fixture they had built one release earlier — 1,571,967 bytes down to 66,560, a 23.6x reduction — and **the encoding is provably lossless rather than merely adequate**: reconstructing every recipe as baseline + delta reproduces the old fixture with **zero mismatches across all 162**, and the key union is 296 either way. So the drop from 149 identical failures to 1 is a change in reporting shape, not in what is detected; the two failing sets are perfectly disjoint and their union is the whole catalog. **The half worth copying is the honesty**: the fragment states the worst case rather than the favourable one — for a near-universally pinned field (`epochs` 3 -> 1) it reddens 158 named recipes, a wall of red on a change that alters no recipe's behaviour — which they volunteered without being asked. When review caught that an earlier revision explained 158 as "158 of 162 declare it explicitly" (all 162 declare it; the real rule is 126 pinning the old default plus 32 pinning the new), they re-measured the distribution independently and it matched mine exactly. Regeneration byte-exact, the Windows CRLF trap pre-empted, no assertion removed, and the property that must NOT fire still does not — deleting a recipe line that merely duplicates a default kills nothing, because #621 excluded that direction ([#638](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/638), [#640](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/640)).
  - Made `--resume` work on the MLX backend, which had been silently restarting from scratch: the resolver only knew the transformers `checkpoint-*` shape, so mlx-lm's flat `NNNNNNN_adapters.safetensors` snapshots were invisible. **The instructive part is the second round.** Their first version copied the transformers `base / experiment_name` nesting into the MLX path — but `mlx_sft.py` writes *flat* — so any config with an `experiment_name` still produced the reporter's exact symptom. The fix is structural rather than conditional (`_resolve_mlx_checkpoint` takes no `experiment_name` at all), and the writer/resolver coupling is now pinned by a test that runs `train()` for real and resolves what it just wrote — **coupled in both directions**, so mutating either side fails it. Three `inspect.getsource` assertions became tests that call the code, and the MLX suite *executes* rather than skips by faking `mlx`/`mlx_lm` through `sys.modules`. They also closed an unbounded read found alongside it — `--resume <a yaml file>` demanded 8.6 quintillion bytes and raised `MemoryError`; all four hostile shapes now give one `ValueError` naming the path, bounded by the file size rather than a magic constant — and found a sibling themselves (`exists()` vs `is_dir()`). Seven mutations, seven killed; zero deleted test lines. Reported and diagnosed by [@imahsanali](https://github.com/imahsanali), whose measurement retracted one of the two findings this was blocked on ([#634](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/634), [#639](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/639)).
  - Closed a gap that a merge commit admitted rather than one an issue advertised: [#738](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/738) wired `training.loraplus_lr_ratio` to a real PEFT LoRA+ optimizer and declared resume unguarded. This proves optimizer momentum **and** scheduler state survive save/resume, by running a real four-step `Trainer.train()` twice over the same seeded data — once straight through, once stopped at the on-disk checkpoint and resumed to the same `max_steps` — and asserting the **full per-group** `get_last_lr()` list, since four groups at four distinct rates *is* the LoRA+ split and a scheduler that rewound while keeping the shape would otherwise pass. **The mutations are what earn it its place**: patching `Trainer._load_optimizer_and_scheduler` to a no-op, and separately rewinding only the scheduler, each leave seven tests green and kill this one alone — nothing else in 20,000+ tests catches either. They also stated its limit unprompted, that a run-versus-run comparison cannot see a defect damaging both runs equally, and gated it below torch 2.6 with a tuple comparison rather than the lexicographic trap that would have skipped it forever ([#724](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/724), [#747](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/747))
  - `soup data validate --format bogus` reported "2/2 rows valid for bogus format" and exited 0, because an unrecognised format silently sets `check_format=False` — so a typo disabled the only check the command performs, and did it in the direction that looks like success. **They reused the single source of truth rather than writing a second allowlist**: the guard imports the same `VALID_FORMATS` the validator itself gates on, so the two cannot drift. Exit 1, not 2, and the PR argues why — this is an input error, not a failed gate, matching `soup data convert`'s `--to` guard in the same file, and a `click.Choice` would have exited 2 and collided with the gate meaning. Six mutations, six killed, including the exit code 1 to 2 ([#866](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/866), [#869](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/869))
  - Added `qwen3.5-0.8b-grpo` and `qwen3.5-2b-grpo`, and the interesting part is the defence rather than the recipes: each carries a hand-written literal repo id pinned on **both** surfaces, and that pin is load-bearing. A repo id mutated on one surface is caught by the catalog-wide cross-checks; mutated **consistently on both**, every cross-field invariant stays silent — the two surfaces still agree with each other — and only the literal pins fire. Since the snapshot fixture is regenerated by the author, an id wrong from the start would be baked into it, so the hand-written literal is the only independent statement of the expected repo. That is the class that shipped `glm-5` pointing at `THUDM`. They also stated the three values [#848](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/848) required be argued rather than inherited — notably `lr: 1e-5`, where copying the SFT siblings' 2e-4 would have been an order of magnitude out on a GRPO objective — and pre-declared five of the six equivalent-by-construction mutation survivors, which they are placed to know exactly, having authored the delta-encoded snapshot guard ([#640](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/640)) and the decision that that direction must not be killed ([#848](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/848), [#864](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/864))
  - Fixed a worker-identity check that could only ever pass by accident. `autodistill`'s MLX teacher worker proved it was the process the parent spawned by comparing its own `os.getpid()` against `Popen.pid` — and on Windows a venv's `Scripts\python.exe` is a **redirector** that launches the real interpreter as a grandchild, so those two legitimately differ and six tests failed on a perfectly healthy worker. **The reason it stayed hidden for a release is worth recording**: nobody had run the suite from a venv on that box, so it surfaced only during a release cut. The replacement is a nonce the parent generates and the worker echoes back into its receipt, which is what the check was trying to establish and does not depend on the process tree at all. Verified here by simulating the defect going away — swapping the launcher for a non-redirecting interpreter makes the test fail rather than silently stop testing anything, so it keeps its own discriminating power ([#898](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/898), [#950](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/950))
  - Widened the failure boundary in `soup train` so an exception raised during setup reaches `fail_run` instead of escaping it, which had left a run that died before training started sitting at `status='running'` forever with no error recorded ([#764](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/764)); they also recorded the run's pid and made `get_run` reconcile an orphaned run on read, so a process that vanished reports as terminated rather than as still alive. **The part I want to credit is what they did not change.** Seven neighbouring fields in `soup runs show` render unescaped text and they routed only `error_message` — arbitrary exception text — through the shared `utils.terminal.for_terminal`, declining to widen into a repo-wide sweep. That was the right call, and the one correction I owed them was in their favour's opposite direction: those fields are *unvalidated* rather than already-validated — `SoupConfig.base`, `output.dir` and the experiment name carry no control-byte validator — so the restraint is still correct but the reason is now written down for whoever reads that hunk next. 339 insertions, **zero removed test lines**. ([#767](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/767))
  - `soup data forge --judge-provider anthropic --judge-base-url <x>` and `soup data recipe --provider anthropic --base-url <x>` blamed a failed Anthropic call on `<x>`, in the terminal and in the recipe's `.checkpoint.json`, although Anthropic ignores that flag and every request goes to `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages`, so an outage pointed the user at a host that was never contacted. They fixed it in the one shared label helper, so a single change covers both commands, and **built the label from the same constant the POST now uses, normalising the provider exactly as the dispatcher does**, so the message and the call cannot disagree on a spelling like `Anthropic`. In review the CLI tests were changed to record the URL actually posted to. Two mutants that had survived (POST sent elsewhere, Anthropic honouring `base_url`) went from 0/8 to 2/8, and they re-ran both themselves before pushing. 8 new test items, 6 of them fail on `main`; eight behaviour-changing mutations, eight killed ([#1340](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1340), [#1370](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1370))
  - Found that the Hub dependency in the test suite was several times larger than the grep estimate in the issue: with the network blocked and an empty `HF_HOME` they ran the real sweep, posted the failure list before touching anything, and showed 76 tests across 10 files downloading tiny models for nothing they check. Replaced each download with a locally trained byte-level tokenizer and a randomly initialised tiny GPT-2/Llama, then consolidated ten copies of the builder into one shared helper that removes its temp directory at exit, keeping file-local only the two models whose shape the tests vary. The 10 files go from 53 failures + 24 errors offline on `main` to 495 passed, no leaked directories, and 9 of 9 mutations of the code under the rewritten tests are killed ([#1356](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1356), [#1544](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1544))

- **MKnaomi2** ([@MKnaomi2](https://github.com/MKnaomi2))
  - Made `soup doctor` recommend a CUDA wheel the driver can actually run, instead of telling everyone to install `cu121`. **The fallback directions are argued, not defaulted**: an unreadable `nvidia-smi` header falls back to `cu121` rather than the newest tag, because a parse failure most likely means an old driver and a too-new wheel is the failure mode that *looks* like success — pip installs happily and CUDA initialisation dies later. They also verified every tag the mapping can emit against the real PyTorch index **with a negative control** to defeat soft-404s. Review found one blocking defect and it is the interesting one: their first tests asserted `"cu121" not in advisory`, which matches the **installed torch version string** rather than the recommendation — so they passed in CI, where torch is CPU-only, and failed on every machine with a CUDA build, the same green-on-the-runner shape that has reddened this repo four times. They fixed it by asserting on `whl/cu121` **and adding a test that pins the trap**, so the naive form cannot return. All four non-blocking items landed in the same push, including the CWE-427 absolute-path resolution of `nvidia-smi` and the stale `cu121` advice in `README.md` ([#612](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/612))

- **Samran Asif** ([@webdevsamran](https://github.com/webdevsamran))
  - First contribution, and it fixed the half of [#650](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/650) that mattered rather than the obvious one. The live training panel built its GPU cell from `memory_allocated()` in `on_log`, which fires *between* steps once activations and gradients are freed, so it reported the trough: `5.8/15.9 GB` against `nvidia-smi`'s 15,893 MiB on the same run — and 5.8 is not an underestimate, it is 1.54 B params x 4 bytes, the fp32 weights at rest and nothing else. Swapping the counter is the easy half; **the reporter's actual complaint was "an unlabeled single number is the problem"**, so the cell is now `GPU peak:` and says what it is. They also wrote down why the lifetime peak is used instead of a per-window one: `reset_peak_memory_stats()` is process-global and would clobber the grad-accum advisor's own reading at `callback.py:566`, which is #650's third criterion — no decision path changes. The mocked-torch test returns 2 GB from `memory_allocated` and 8 GB from `max_memory_allocated` and asserts the panel shows 8.0, so it fails for the reason it exists; both the counter and the label die under mutation ([#650](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/650), [#652](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/652)).
  - Second merge, and it is the one where the *refusals* were the work. LoRA-FA freezes the A matrices and trains only B, so the optimizer state is a fraction of plain LoRA's — but the option collides with half of this repo's PEFT surface, and the first revisions accepted those combinations and discovered them at runtime. The shipped version refuses Spectrum, LISA, `lora.r: 0` full-FT, VeRA (at parse AND at runtime) and non-AdamW optimizers, and **each refusal is killed by its own mutation** — three of them had survived a previous round, which is exactly the finding that made this take four. The detail worth copying is how they pinned the optimizer's own hyperparameters: rather than asserting the optimizer exists, the test supplies `adam_beta1=0.88 / adam_beta2=0.95 / adam_epsilon=1e-7` through a real `TrainingArguments` and checks each value arrives, so dropping either copy line fails separately. 14 mutations, 14 killed, including the attach itself and the `load_state_dict` post-hook that makes resume work. Their regenerated recipe snapshot is exactly one additive line rather than a rewritten file, and `benchmarks/README.md` came back with 43 rows against main's 41 and **zero lost** — I diffed both tables rather than reading them, because a rebase had eaten that row once before. **Process note that is mine, not theirs:** I called an earlier head merge-ready and then their matrix was red on all nine cells for thirteen hours before I said anything ([#725](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/725), [#962](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/962))

- **Yuri P. Baumgartner** ([@YuriPerro](https://github.com/YuriPerro))
  - Two precise reports in one night, then fixed the harder of them. The batch probe decided "fits" purely by the absence of `OutOfMemoryError`, but under WDDM the allocator does not raise when VRAM is exhausted — it spills to host RAM and the step completes — so it approved batches that do not physically fit, **cached the answer**, and did so on the default path (`batch_size: auto`). Their diagnosis is the part I could not have written: *the second probe did not inherit the first one's defence* — layer streaming already reads `max_memory_*` after its step for exactly this reason. I reproduced their bug on unrelated hardware before merging: a batch peaking at **4.87 GB on a 4.29 GB card** was approved on `main` and is refused by their fix, while a genuinely-fitting batch still passes. Both load-bearing choices are argued from measurements already in this repo rather than picked for looking reasonable — `max_memory_allocated` over `reserved` (which runs 1.08-1.41x and is not a constant), and **no safety fraction at all**, because the measured probe is already +12.5-14.3% conservative. They also bumped the cache key so entries poisoned by the old probe cannot survive, and added the `AcceleratorError`-at-synchronize case I had not thought of. 10 of 11 mutations killed; zero deleted test lines. Separately, they held first refusal on [#650](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/650) and **declined it** when another contributor's PR already delivered it ([#649](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/649), [#650](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/650), [#654](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/654)).

- **Abdulwaarith Zakariyya** ([@abdulwaarith0](https://github.com/abdulwaarith0))
  - First contribution, and it closed a crash nobody had reported: `_convert_multimodal` called `msg.get("content")` without checking `msg` is a dict, and `AttributeError` is **not** in the tuple `format_to_messages` catches to route malformed rows to its drop path — so one bad line in a large JSONL file killed the entire dataset load instead of being skipped like every other malformed row. Confirmed at the loader rather than only in the converter: `_format_rows` has no `try/except`, so on `main` a 5-row file with one bad row raises straight out of `load_dataset`, and returns 4 rows with the fix. **The test is the strong form, and that is the part worth copying**: replacing the `raise` with `continue` — which keeps the row and lets the malformed message through verbatim — fails all four parameters, because the assertion is `is None` rather than "no exception". That is exactly the silent-corruption case `_require_str_content`'s docstring warns about, and the easy version of this test would have passed it. Scope verified complete for the class it names across four bad shapes and ten format paths, and the **qualification is theirs to be credited for too** — they did not overclaim the blast radius, and review found `detect_format` never returns `multimodal`, so the path needs an explicit `format: multimodal`. Zero test lines removed. Review of this PR turned up the silent half of the same contract in `chatml`/`audio`/`video`, filed as [#676](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/676) rather than widened into their diff ([#670](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/670))
  - Second merge, and it closed the half their own first PR's review had split off. `soup data validate` answered a different question from the loader: it checked that the keys in `FORMAT_SIGNATURES[fmt]` were present, while `load_dataset` runs the converter and drops whatever raises. The gap ran both ways — a row could carry every required key and still be dropped, and six formats absent from that table were never validated at all — so on `multimodal` the validator reported **5/5 rows valid** on a file the loader cuts to 4. **The part worth copying is what they did to make the refactor reviewable.** Extracting the converter dispatch touched +87/-46 in a file every training run goes through, which is exactly the diff a reviewer has to take on trust; instead they made it checkable, and I verified their claim with a differential harness of **20 formats x 51 row shapes = 1020 cases** comparing output and raised exception type and message between the two trees — byte-identical, zero diffs. They also ran every shipped `examples/data/*.jsonl` through both builds to show no good data starts failing, and confirmed `validate` still exits non-zero only on a missing file or an undetectable format, so the `soup ci init` PR gate cannot start blocking merges. Zero mocks — the tests call the real converters. Four mutations, four killed, including neutering the per-row reason so no row is ever invalid, which fails 16 of 34 tests. Two removed test lines, both audited: a docstring, and an assertion on a message string that no longer exists, replaced by the equivalent check **plus** a new row-index assertion ([#695](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/695), [#712](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/712))

  - Third merge, and it made a documented option true for the first time. `training.loraplus_lr_ratio` was accepted by the schema, printed in the docs as `lr_B = lr x 16`, and had **never run**: the SFT, pretrain and embedding wrappers inserted it into `training_kwargs` and forwarded that to `TrainingArguments`, which has no such field, so enabling LoRA+ raised `TypeError` before the first step. **The choice worth copying is refusing the mock.** Their tests build a real PEFT model and a real `transformers.Trainer`, because a mock auto-creates the LoRA parameter groups this path depends on and would have hidden the defect -- the easy version of this test passes on the broken code. They also found the spelling that actually works rather than the one that looks right: PEFT ignores a plain `weight_decay` here and honours `loraplus_weight_decay`, and swapping the two fails exactly one named test. The second round is what made it hard to delete: a wiring-coverage scan mirroring `test_issue359_deepspeed_guard_coverage`, requiring every wrapper that applies a LoRA adapter to either call the helper or be listed with a reason in `_LORAPLUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`. I verified the premise for it rather than asserting it -- with the attach call replaced by `pass` in all three wrappers, the five pre-existing files still report `303 passed, 1 skipped`, and only the new scan notices. The exemption list is checked in both directions, so an invented module fails it and an exempt module that gains the call fails it too. Seven mutations, seven killed; zero deleted test lines. **They also scoped honestly without being asked**: save/resume of optimizer state is untested and the thirteen preference/RL trainers are exempt rather than wired, both recorded in the exemption set instead of quietly left out ([#724](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/724), [#738](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/738))
  - Fourth, fifth and sixth merges, and together they close the doubled-BOS class on both the training and the serving side. The training half is the one worth reading: the rendered chat template already carries BOS, TRL then tokenised that text with `add_special_tokens=True`, and every row trained on `[BOS, BOS, ...]` — **a token sequence the model never sees at inference**. Pre-tokenising in the SFT path stops TRL's `tokenize_fn` running at all, but that also bypasses TRL's `add_eos`, and **the first revision silently dropped the stop token on truncated rows** — the review caught it, and the repair was to reproduce TRL's rule directly rather than probe the post-processor. Measured afterwards across 12 template x post-processor shapes on real fast tokenizers: **36 of 36 cases keep main's EOS**, including every truncated one. They also made `utils/data_doctor.py` *call* the shared label builder instead of mirroring it, so `soup data doctor --show-mask` cannot drift from the real collator path, and pinned that with a test a mutation kills. The serving half does the same one layer out — the engine now receives token ids rather than a string it would re-tokenise — and the fragment names the **opposite** case too (a template rendering no BOS now yields zero rather than one), which is the half an author is not obliged to volunteer. Scope was held throughout: SGLang and MII were split into [#890](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/890) and [#891](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/891) with measured evidence rather than folded in, the cache-vs-live divergence went to [#876](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/876), and when asked for the minimum vLLM version that accepts a mapping they answered with the release and the signature change and then sent it as its own PR ([#785](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/785), [#788](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/788), [#867](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/867), [#892](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/892))
  - Seventh merge, and it is the last backend in the doubled-BOS class they opened themselves. `soup serve --backend mii` rendered the chat template and then handed the engine the rendered **string**, which MII tokenises itself with the special tokens re-added — 37 ids against the template's 36. The fix sends ids: one render, one encode, a `TokenizedPrompt` to the engine, and `create_mii_pipeline` wrapping the already-loaded serve tokenizer so nothing can re-tokenise behind the seam. **The part worth copying is what happened at the call site.** My own earlier review called this merge-ready and was wrong: one mutation survived — dropping the `tokenizer=` kwarg in `commands/serve.py` left every test green, so the code that makes the seam hold was unpinned. Told that, they did not add an assertion that the kwarg exists; they captured `create_mii_pipeline`'s kwargs with a `side_effect`, asserted the **loaded** tokenizer is the one that arrives, and pinned the ordering (`_load_serve_tokenizer` before the pipeline is built), which also covers the `None`-degrade path — so a fix that merely passed *something* still fails. Re-verified here on the merged tree: 12 mutations, 12 killed, the former survivor now failing by name; the pre-fix render fails 24 tests; both test files on a clean `main` give 28 failed / 27 passed; blast radius 392 passed. Zero test lines deleted, and their "serve.py is byte-identical to the head you verified" claim checked out by blob SHA rather than by reading. The docs conflict with [#995](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/995) was resolved by agreement beforehand, so no page now claims either MII or SGLang sends the engine the rendered string ([#891](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/891), [#996](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/996))
  - `soup data preprocess` tokenised the rendered chat template directly, with no `add_eos` step, while the live SFT path reproduces TRL 0.29.1's rule in `data/loss_mask.py` — so on a template that renders no EOS and a tokenizer whose post-processor appends none (the Qwen shape), the live path trained on one stop token and the cache on **zero**, teaching run-on generation. It was silent in both directions a user can look: the loss curve is normal, and `soup data doctor --show-mask` renders the live path rather than the cache. **The part worth copying is what they did when the first baseline was challenged.** Their original test compared the cache against a faithful reproduction of TRL's `add_eos`, with a fair justification — the real `SFTTrainer._prepare_dataset` hangs on Windows through `PartialState` — but a reproduction of a rule is evidence about one's reading of the rule, not about the rule. They drove TRL's genuine `_prepare_dataset` instead, unbound with a minimal `self`, and made four things agree at one EOS per row: real TRL, their reproduction, the live `build_full_sequence_labels` path, and the cache. The second thing to copy is the truncation control: the obvious fix appends unconditionally, theirs appends only while the row is under the length budget, because the live path appends *then* truncates and keeps no trailing EOS there either — a constraint that comes from following the live path to its end rather than from the bug report, and their `append unconditionally` mutation proves the control is load-bearing. The cache key bumps `v2 -> v3` so a dataset cached under the old encoding is re-tokenised rather than silently reused, and the changelog fragment names the remedy for anyone already affected ([#791](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/791), [#938](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/938))
  - `soup serve --backend sglang` rendered the prompt through the chat template — which already emits the model's BOS — and then handed that string to the engine with special tokens added **again**. Every request began with two BOS tokens: a prompt the model was never trained on, with no error and no warning, so the degradation reads as the model simply being a bit worse and nobody files a bug for it. **The shape of the fix is the point** — it follows #781 in encoding the prompt the way training encoded it, rather than detecting a doubled BOS and stripping it afterwards. A stripper has to know which token to remove and when, and is wrong the moment a tokenizer behaves slightly differently; not adding special tokens to an already-rendered template has no such failure mode. They also diagnosed their own red Windows cell correctly as #382's illegal-instruction crash in a file they do not touch ([#890](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/890), [#995](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/995))
  - `training.loraplus_lr_ratio`, which their own #738 made work on sft, pretrain and embedding, was accepted and silently ignored by the thirteen other trainers that build a LoRA adapter: B trained at the base rate and the loss curve looked normal. Twelve now attach the LoRA+ optimizer after construction, online_dpo included, which hands TRL a `peft_config=` instead of calling `get_peft_model`. **PPO is the part worth reading.** trl's PPO trainer builds its optimizer and scheduler inside `__init__`, so a late attach would leave the scheduler on a discarded optimizer; they build the optimizer first, add the value model's groups (without them the critic belonged to no optimizer and never trained), prune empty groups under DeepSpeed, and pass it to the constructor, detecting that trainer shape rather than the trl version. Tasks with no trainable LoRA B, VeRA included, refuse the ratio at parse. **The part worth copying is how the tests grew**: across four review rounds, each surviving mutation became a test of what the trainer actually receives (group sizes as the constructor sees them, `base_lrs` on the real trl trainer), and a source scan fails when any LoRA-building trainer ships without LoRA+ or an earned exemption. On the final head: 195 tests plus 2 real-trainer smoke tests, 8 of 9 mutations killed (the survivor is error-message wording), and 3653 passed across 49 files on the merged tree ([#745](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/745), [#1080](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1080))
  - `soup data canary insert` appended chat-format canaries to every dataset, so on any other format the loader dropped all of them before training and `canary check` could only answer OK. The issue's alpaca repro kept 3 of 7 rows on `main` and all 7 here. The canaries are now written in the file's own format, and every format whose canaries would be dropped, or that `check` cannot score, is refused before the manifest or the data exists. That includes plaintext, a file whose format cannot be detected, a first row that is not an object, and an explicit `--format` that is not the file's own. The manifest records the format, and legacy manifests read as chatml. **The part worth copying is the sweep:** every format `detect_format` can return must either load every canary or be refused with nothing written, and a guard test fails when a new format arrives without a fixture. It caught #1306's `cross_encoder` before either PR merged. Second round: the one surviving mutation became a test. 20 of 20 mutations killed; 350 passed, 1 skipped with the canary suite ([#1216](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1216), [#1317](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1317))
  - `format: tool-calling` read only top-level `tools` and `tool_calls`. It dropped every row `soup agent synth` writes, and every OpenAI-shape trajectory, and `soup train --dry-run` still said `Ready to train!` on zero rows. The converter now reads calls where they live, in source order, and keeps the old top-level form as a fallback. `format: auto` recognises `{messages, tools}`, synthesised rows carry their tool's schema, and `tools` may be a JSON-encoded string. **The part worth copying is the stop, and what it found.** `soup train` now exits 1 before the model loads when a load yields no training rows, and names the format and the first dropped row's reason. On its first run it caught two fixtures in the existing suite whose only row had gone to validation, runs that had trained on nothing; they fixed the fixtures rather than loosening the check. They also caught that Arrow gives every message every key, so a plain user turn reached Llama-style templates as a call, and they pinned that with a render through a real tokenizer. Second round: a string `tools` regression, null content, a mutable load record and one unpinned line, all closed with tests. 25 of 25 mutations killed; 4066 passed across a 74-file batch ([#1217](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1217), [#1321](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1321))
  - `soup data clean --repair-json` and `--drop-invalid-json` read only a flat `arguments` key on a row's top-level `tool_calls`, so the calls the tool-calling loader actually reads were never touched: calls nested in an assistant turn (what `soup agent synth` writes) were not repaired or checked, and a valid `{"function": {"arguments": ...}}` call read as `""` and got its row dropped. They wrote the issue with four shapes and a repro, then routed the top-level list and every assistant turn through one helper in both the `tool-calling` and `chatml` branches, following exactly what the loader accepts (dict arguments and a missing `arguments` left alone, non-assistant turns untouched). **When review found that the fix turned `"tool_calls": null`, which a schema-unified export writes on every no-call row, into a dropped row under the default flags, they took the guard and the test in one push** and filed the neighbouring empty-turn case as its own issue. The new file gives 45 passed on the branch and 24 failed on `main`, 8 mutations of the fix and 3 of the null guard all fail a test, and a four-row CLI run matches ([#1338](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1338), [#1453](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1453))
  - `soup data clean` silently deleted valid tool-calling training rows: with no flags, the `--min-tokens` check in the `chatml` branch of `clean_row` dropped a trajectory whose call-only assistant turn had `"content": ""` (the shape Soup's own tool-calling format writes) while the same turn with `null` was kept and the loader accepted both. They found it while working on #1338, reported it with a runnable snippet and the CLI output, and fixed it by exempting only a turn with a non-empty `tool_calls` list, leaving `[]`, `null`, a missing key and non-lists on the old rule. 18 tests (6 fail on `main`) cover `""`, whitespace and `null` in both branches; their mutation table (6 / 10 / 2 / 3 / 18 / 1) reproduced exactly and five further mutations were all caught ([#1477](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1477), [#1493](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1493))
  - `soup ab` estimated the variance from the rows it tested, so its two-sided mSPRT needed a 30-40 row burn-in, was calibrated only to 1000 rows and `--alpha 0.01`, and still sat at 0.011 there. They replaced it with the normal-inverse-gamma mixture Bayes factor, which depends on the rows only through the t statistic, so the false-positive bound holds at any number of looks: worst simulated 0.033 at `--alpha 0.05` and 0.0066 at 0.01, from 7 rows per arm. **The part worth copying is that the cost is published next to the gain**: `accept_h0` is slower when there is no difference (281 pairs against 43 at 0.5 sd), and that table sits in the docs, the record and the changelog. Across four review rounds every edge review found became a refusal or a guard with a test a mutation kills: a near-constant held-out column, an overflowing prior at a small row count, a variance that underflows to 0, and a saturated start that had ended 99% of simulated runs in a wrong `accept_h0`, now held at `continue` while the tested rows spread more than 3 times the held-out ones (0 such runs in 2000, 15 of 6000 Gaussian first verdicts changed, no rejection). 208 passed across the `soup ab` suite; 16 of 17 mutations of the final changes killed ([#1265](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1265), [#1419](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1419))
  - `training.freeze_layers` and `training.freeze_ratio` loaded on all 23 tasks but are read in exactly one place, `SFTTrainerWrapper._setup_transformers`. On the other 21 tasks a `freeze_layers: 2` run trained every layer and said nothing. They refused the fields at load on those tasks and kept `tts` on the applying side, because its wrapper inherits the SFT setup. They tied the refused list to the trainers with an AST scan of `trainer/dispatch.py` instead of a hand-kept table, pinned the refusal on every backend, and filed the remaining within-`sft` gap as #1581. 106 tests; every reachable mutation killed ([#1497](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1497), [#1532](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1532))
  - `soup ab` reached `accept_h0` slowly when there was no difference: under their own #1265 statistic the Bayes factor had to fall to `log(beta / (1 - alpha))`, which took 621 pairs per arm on average at 0.3 standard deviations. They read the same mixture the other way, as an always-valid confidence sequence for the difference, and accept once it lies inside +-effect_size: 307 pairs at 0.3 sd and 117 at 0.5, with the reject rule unchanged byte for byte. **The part worth copying is how the decision was made**: both readings of `--beta` were measured on the same replayed runs and put in the PR with their costs (level beta was faster, 220 pairs against 307 at 0.3 sd, but took power at effect_size from 0.997 to 0.964). The maintainer chose level alpha, and the record keeps the option not taken. `--beta` is retired through the existing one-release deprecation staging: it warns once and changes no verdict. 466 passed across the `soup ab`, deprecation, fragment, startup and ANSI-guard tests; 27 of 30 mutations killed, the survivors an exact float tie and an unpinned `--beta 0` warning ([#1418](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1418), [#1575](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1575))
  - Kept their own #1419 release-note entry true after #1575 retired `--beta`: the entry said the `--alpha` + `--beta` < 1 check "stays" although it is in no tagged release and #1575 removes it, and pointed at #1418 instead of quoting the new 117-pair figure. Changelog-only; verified that neither v0.75.1 nor v0.75.2 carries the check, fragment test 21 passed, and every fragment assembled cleanly on main + #1575 + this PR; they flagged the matching #1378 sentence instead of editing another contributor's entry ([#1575](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1575), [#1635](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1635))

- **GitFromRatatouille** ([@swalla02](https://github.com/swalla02))
  - First contribution, and it is the third instance of this repo's most persistent bug class found by someone else ([#363](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/363), [#423](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/423), [#659](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/659)): `soup migrate` refused **any** input named `*.jsonl`, a valid LLaMA-Factory YAML config included, because the guard branched on the filename while the content sniff written to gate it had **zero call sites in `src/`**. The diagnosis is worth more than the one-line fix — the test was named after the command and its body called the helper, so it passed on a codebase where `migrate` never imported it. **Then review found a regression their own fix introduced, and the way they closed it is the part to copy**: the sniff read `utf-8`, so a UTF-8 BOM survived as U+FEFF, which `str.strip()` does not remove, and a real BOM'd JSONL silently lost the friendly error it used to get — on a project with three Windows CI cells where PowerShell's `Out-File` writes that BOM by default. Their BOM test **guards its own fixture** (asserting the file really contains a BOM before asserting behaviour) and their bounded-read test measures the property with `tracemalloc` rather than pinning the spelling of the loop. Six mutations, six killed; zero assertions removed. They also **declined to widen the fix** to files whose name gives no hint, correctly citing the `.ipynb` case the original comment warns about, and left that as an open question rather than guessing ([#672](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/672), [#675](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/675))
  - Fixed the README's very first command. `pip install soup-cli` fails on Debian 12 and Ubuntu 23.04 or later with `error: externally-managed-environment` — PEP 668, the **default** on current Debian and Ubuntu, so the ordinary first-run experience on Linux rather than an edge case — and nothing in Soup can rescue it, because the package is not installed yet when that line runs. The install now leads with `pipx` and `uv tool`, which is right on the merits: Soup declares a `soup` console script, so it is an application, and both tools sit outside PEP 668's scope. Every `pip` line survives, under a sentence saying when it is the right choice. **The best judgement in the PR is the place they refused to recommend pipx**: for the contributor setup in `CONTRIBUTING.md` they wrote out `venv` instead, because an editable checkout has to be importable by the test suite and pipx isolation is precisely what stops that — recommending it there would have manufactured, for every new contributor, the resolve-to-the-wrong-copy failure this repo already fights. They reached that unprompted, and likewise left four other docs pages alone on the reasoning that an extras hint mid-prose describes a package name rather than an install procedure ([#671](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/671), [#673](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/673))

- **Yuzhong Zhang** ([@BetterAndBetterII](https://github.com/BetterAndBetterII))
  - First contribution, and the fastest response to an issue this project has had: [#676](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/676) was filed at 19:17 and their pull request opened at 19:48. `format_to_messages` documents a drop contract — a malformed row returns `None` so one bad JSONL line is skipped rather than corrupting the dataset — and `_convert_chatml`, `_convert_audio` and `_convert_video` passed a non-dict `messages` element through verbatim, so `{"messages": ["hello"]}`, or `42`, or a nested list where a message object belongs, survived into training. `chatml` is what `detect_format` returns for a bare `{"messages": [...]}` row, so this was the **default path for the most common dataset shape**. **The part worth copying is how they handled losing a comparison.** A second pull request ([#679](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/679), @v01dst) landed on the same issue ninety minutes later; an identical battery picked theirs on validator/loader agreement and mutations killed, and review then found the one thing the other PR had that theirs lacked — a `isinstance(messages, list)` guard, since rejecting a non-list by *iterating* it works by accident for `"hello"` and `42` and silently keeps any **empty** non-list iterable such as `""` or `{}`. They folded that guard in with credit, plus the fixtures for both shapes, within two minutes of it being handed over. Verified by mutation on the merged tree: removing @v01dst's list guard fails 6 tests and removing their own element guard fails 14, so the two halves are pinned **independently** rather than one masking the other. Zero test lines removed ([#676](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/676), [#678](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/678))

- **Mahmoud Ahmed** ([@v01dst](https://github.com/v01dst))
  - Found the defect that neither the issue nor the competing fix had seen, and then gave it away. Their [#679](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/679) arrived on [#676](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/676) alongside [#678](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/678); an identical battery run against both picked #678, and **the single most valuable idea in either PR was theirs** — an explicit `isinstance(messages, list)` check before the element loop, because iterating the value directly catches `"hello"` and `42` only by accident and silently keeps any *empty* non-list iterable. Both `{"messages": ""}` and `{"messages": {}}` are JSON-reachable and both detect as `chatml`. **The finding that came out of reviewing their own PR is the one to remember**: deleting that guard passed all 199 of their tests, because only an empty non-list iterable can discriminate it and that was the one case they had not written a fixture for — an implementation that was right and a suite that could not tell. Told the comparison had gone against them, they closed their own PR citing the measurement, then posted the guard into the other thread ready to apply so its author would not have to reverse-engineer it from their branch; it was merged two minutes later. Standing down on someone else's number, and then doing the work to make their win cheaper, is worth more to this project than the PR would have been ([#676](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/676), [#679](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/679), merged in [#678](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/678))


- **Jagadeep Mamidi** ([@jagadeepmamidi](https://github.com/jagadeepmamidi))
  - Arrived with three pull requests in one evening against a batch of freshly filed bugs, and two of them repaired v0.74.0 release regressions. **[#708](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/708)**: `EmbeddingTrainerWrapper.train()` reads `self.trainer.model` and `self.trainer.args`, and `_EmbeddingTrainer` delegated neither, so the whole transformers embedding path died after setup — a 100% crash reproduced here through the real CLI at exit 1. Eight lines. **The tests are what make it,** and they are the direct answer to the trap [#690](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/690) was filed about: a real `LlamaModel` on disk, the real `load_config_from_string`, the real `setup()`, and an explicit `isinstance(wrapper.trainer, _EmbeddingTrainer)` — where `tests/test_embedding.py` holds 79 green tests over that same crashing path because a `MagicMock` auto-created the two missing attributes. Five mutations killed, including `model` returning `None` and `args` returning a bare `object()`, which is coverage of the delegation *target* rather than a presence check. **[#709](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/709)**: `packing: true` was a hard `TypeError` on the only trl range Soup supports, and the diff also retires `packing_cross_doc_attn_mask` — a call I asked them to justify and they did, with evidence I could check: `attention_free` appears in **zero of nine trl wheels** from 0.7.11 to 0.29.1, so the path was never live and nobody had a working cross-document run to lose. Told on review that `trainer/pretrain.py` carried the identical crash one file away, they took the extend-this-PR option rather than leaving a half-fixed defect, and it is pinned by its own mutation. Zero test lines removed across both ([#690](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/690), [#708](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/708), [#691](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/691), [#709](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/709))
  - `soup ship` recorded **no numerics for a judge-backed run**, so evidence produced under one quantization could be replayed against another with nothing to notice. Two design choices make the stamp trustworthy rather than decorative: it is taken from the very string handed to `make_generator`, so recorded and loaded values cannot drift by construction, and `utils/live_eval.py` raises on an unsupported quantization rather than falling back, so the stamp cannot silently lie either. The refactor is better than the request — I had asked for a check, they made the drift impossible. Verified here: sixteen evidence files measured across the exit-code taxonomy (0 SHIP / 2 DON'T / 3 usage / 1 runtime) with no gate refusal exiting 2, and CLI and MCP now refuse the same file for the same reason, which they did not before ([#367](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/367), [#746](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/746))
  - MiniLLM enabled with the default offline blend **ignored the teacher while still loading it** — at mix 0 the offline reverse-KL target is the detached student, so the run paid for a teacher that contributed nothing ([#692](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/692)). Refused at parse, with mix 0 kept legal on the on-policy path where it genuinely means student-only sampling. **The second round is the one worth recording.** Told that `--minillm-on-policy` was applied *after* validation and so was now dead for the exact config it exists to serve, they did not move the assignment — they gave `load_config` a `training_overrides` parameter that merges into the raw `training:` mapping before `SoupConfig` is built, so a CLI flag now passes through the same cross-validators as YAML and the next such flag inherits the fix. Turned around in under an hour, with a test that invokes the CLI rather than grepping `--help`. ([#977](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/977))
  - The distill test harness recompiles `_DistillTrainer.compute_loss` and bound the closure's free variables **by value at compile time**, so a new free variable raised a `NameError` **attributed to `distill.py`** — a file where nothing was wrong. That is worse than a crash with no location, because it costs someone an afternoon before they doubt the traceback. The bindings are now derived from the factory and `TrainingConfig` defaults and the error names the symbol that could not be resolved; nested functions such as `_token_weighted_accumulation` are no longer misreported as missing, which is what keeps the error trustworthy; and the recompiled class carries `<compiled _DistillTrainer>` so the misattribution cannot return even if the derivation is wrong one day. Verified here on the merged tree: 45 passed across the #720 grad-accum file, the changelog-fragment guard and the #887 broken-teacher suite ([#990](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/990), [#1008](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1008))
  - A PR's green marks describe the `main` that existed when the run started, `strict` is off, and a stale-green PR nearly merged a `NameError` onto `main` on [#763](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/763). They built the missing half: a `merge-freshness` check that rebuilds the live merge with `git merge-tree --write-tree`, scans it for undefined names, and re-evaluates every open PR on each push to `main`. **The reason this one is worth reading is the security round.** The re-evaluation job holds a `checks: write` token while materialising fork trees, and the first revision ran `python -m ruff` with the working directory set to that tree — so a planted `ruff/__main__.py` executed with the token, which I demonstrated rather than argued. Their fix is three independent layers (an absolute binary resolved by `shutil.which` before any cwd change, a scrub of `GITHUB_TOKEN` / `GH_*` / `ACTIONS_*TOKEN*`, and `--isolated` so the fork's own `ignore = ["F821"]` cannot suppress a finding), each one pinned by the same test, and the fork tree is extracted from `git archive` with `filter="data"` so it carries no `.git` and no persisted credential. Re-run on the final head: the control still writes the token on the old head and the new head writes nothing on **both** invocation paths. They also moved the re-evaluation logic out of the argv reader into an injectable function so the two mutations that had survived now redden named tests, pinned five workflow-shape properties structurally (the `push` trigger, no `ref:` on the checkout, the `if:` conditions, `cancel-in-progress`, the ruff pin), and **documented the one thing that cannot be fixed in code** — the gate job grades its own PR, so only the check posted from `main`'s copy is trustworthy ([#1017](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1017), [#1071](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1071))
  - ReLoRA **threw away what the adapter had learned at every restart**: the callback magnitude-pruned `lora_A`/`lora_B` in place without merging them into the base, and a tie could zero both factors into a branch with no gradient. They took the paper contract rather than the rename: merge the PEFT-scaled `B @ A` into the base, re-initialise A and B, clear the optimizer moments, re-warm the LR from `1/(W+1)`. They carried it through five review rounds, each of which found something the previous one could not see: a skip that switched ReLoRA off on every real peft model; a merged base that never reached the saved output (95.4% of the training effect missing after three restarts, reloaded the way `soup merge` does); and a dense save that crashed after training on the embedding and PPO wrappers. **Each time they fixed the cause rather than the symptom**: the save now resolves the embedding wrapper's inner trainer and trl's PPO policy, refuses a model it cannot save dense before step 1, keeps the literal non-ReLoRA save so two existing guards stay valid, and is pinned by a ratchet over every trainer that attaches ReLoRA. Verified here on the tree merged with `main`: the output reloads to the trained logits (0.00004% missing), `soup train` finishes dense for sft, dpo and embedding, their three test files pass where `main` fails 33, and all seven mutations re-run on the final head are caught ([#693](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/693), [#1133](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1133))

- **Dipak Chaudhari** ([@dchaudhari7177](https://github.com/dchaudhari7177))
  - Took multipack FFD placement from O(N^2) to O(N log N) by descending a segment tree of per-bin remaining capacity instead of scanning every open bin — 5.5x faster at 1,000 rows, 37x at 10,000, 96x at 30,000, with per-10x-N scaling dropping from 104-130x to 8.9-13.9x. **The property that decides a change like this is not that the packing is valid but that it is IDENTICAL**, since moving one item to a different bin silently changes every multipack training run with no test failure to announce it; verified against the pre-change function over 6,000 randomized cases, 5,384 of them containing repeated lengths, for zero mismatches. They also wrote the one test that a behaviour-preserving revert cannot escape — no equivalence assertion can detect a pure performance regression, so a single timing test is the only guard against silently returning to quadratic, and they wrote it as a **ratio measured inside one process** rather than an absolute threshold, which is the shape that survives a slow CI runner. @Srinivasan8888 stood down on [#716](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/716) in favour of their design on a neighbouring PR, saying it made the pin unnecessary to trust rather than merely pinning it ([#694](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/694), [#726](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/726))
  - `soup data lint` flagged **every** conversational DPO row as `prompt_leak`, both shipped examples included, because a conversational `chosen`/`rejected` carries its prompt as the leading user turn and the linter flattened every turn before looking for it. `length_bias` had the same root cause, measuring prompt plus answer. Both now read only the assistant turns; `identical_pairs` and `near_duplicates` deliberately keep the full text, because a duplicate is a duplicate across the whole row. **The hard half of a false-positive fix is not removing the true positive**, and this one is pinned against exactly that: replacing the new `extract_completion_text` with a constant `""` — disabling the detector rather than narrowing it — fails 14 tests, led by `test_a_prompt_echoed_inside_the_assistant_turn_is_still_flagged`. Measured through the real CLI, `dpo_sample.jsonl` goes MAJOR 3/8 -> OK 0/8 while a genuine echo inside the assistant turn still reports MAJOR 10/10. They also **declined to edit the example datasets** to make the gate green: both still exit 2 on a real 6x length gap, and saying so in the PR body beat papering over it ([#810](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/810), [#916](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/916))
  - The MLX smoke harness pinned `batch_size` and `epochs` but not `gradient_accumulation_steps`, so a config change could move the optimizer-update count under a record claiming a fixed one. **The design point is the part worth naming, and they found it themselves**: the guard checks BOTH counts, because at 48 rows the wrapper runs 48 iterations whether accumulation is 1 or 4 — an iterations-only check would accept a deleted accumulation pin in silence. Verified: weakening the condition to iterations alone fails exactly the test written for it. The arithmetic mirrors the wrapper rather than re-deriving it (`epochs * ceil(rows/batch)`, rounded DOWN by accumulation, never divided) and was cross-checked against the real `TrainingArgs` across six shapes, including four read-backs measured on an M1 8 GB by @Srinivasan8888 — whom they credited in their own changelog fragment. They also filed the fragment under `0.75.0/` rather than the `0.74.0/` the maintainer asked for, because the release had landed in between and the instruction had gone stale ([#716](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/716), [#727](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/727))
  - `data.interleave` with `strategy: over` recycles the shorter stream, so the positional val split could put a row **and its copy** on opposite sides — measured against real `datasets` end to end, 10 of 20 validation rows also appeared in train. The val set is now sized over DISTINCT rows. **The second round is the interesting one**: their first fix took the tail of a deduplicated list and silently lost a genuinely repeated source row, which @AmirF194 caught; the repair prefers unique content, withholds the remaining copies **and prints the count**, and refuses a `val_split` that would leave zero training rows. They also replaced a vacuous assertion rather than adding to it — `len(train) == len(kept)` re-derived the implementation's own filter, and the multiset equality that replaced it is the only assertion that kills the row-loss mutation. A control that survived 65 tests on the previous head now fails three ([#702](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/702), [#729](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/729))
  - `_DistillTrainer` inherited transformers' loss-kwargs path, so the accumulated gradient scaled with `gradient_accumulation_steps` instead of matching the equivalent full batch — measured at exactly 4.0x at GA=4. **What makes this entry worth reading is the test, and the fact that its first version was wrong in a way that looked right**: it built a bare `torch.nn.Linear` and took a `compensate` flag the test supplied itself, so five of six cases passed on unmodified `main` and would have passed with `src/soup_cli/` deleted. Told so, they replaced it with one that compiles the REAL class body out of `distill.py` via AST, binds it to the real `Trainer`, and asserts on a gradient norm ratio. A side effect: nothing in the repo had ever constructed `_DistillTrainer`, so dropping `num_items_in_batch` from its signature was an instant `TypeError` that 761 distill-touching tests did not notice ([#720](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/720), [#742](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/742))
  - Built the package-wide guard behind [#752](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/752), where a typer command called as a plain Python function kept its literal `typer.Option(...)` defaults — truthy `OptionInfo` objects — and `soup eval auto` died with a `TypeError` *after* a clean eval. **The call-site fix landed in someone else's PR and they kept going anyway**, which is why the next occurrence will be caught rather than shipped. Their scanner walks `src/soup_cli` with `ast`, resolves each bare-name call through the calling module's own imports, and matches positional arguments to parameters **by declaration order rather than by count** — the subtlety that stops a leading `ctx` from silently absorbing the check. Two rounds, both closing mutations that had walked past the previous head. I ran it in both directions on live repository code: reintroducing the real defect names the file, the line and only the genuinely unfilled parameters, two correct new call sites behind nested imports produce zero findings, and re-run against a `main` that had moved underneath it, it named *two* missing parameters where it had named one that morning — reading the repository as it changes, not only its own fixture. ([#757](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/757))
  - Stopped `soup profile` presenting an **assumed** GPU as a measured fit ([#830](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/830)). With no device and no `--gpu`, it fell back to 24 GB and still printed `OK Fits in 24 GB VRAM` and `OK Recommended batch_size: 8` — **a confident verdict about hardware nobody had looked at**, which is worse than no verdict because it does not prompt anyone to check. They returned the *provenance* (`flag` / `detected` / `assumed`) rather than special-casing the message, so `--json` and the MCP `profile` tool carry `gpu_memory_source` too and a non-human caller can tell the difference. The quiet half will bite more people: laptop GPUs were sized against the desktop part, and an `rtx5070laptop` is 8 GB against 12. Making `normalize_gpu_key` strip `nvidia`/`geforce`/`tesla`/`gpu` plus spaces and hyphens is what makes it actually work — torch's own `"NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5070 Laptop GPU"` and a hand-typed `--gpu "rtx 5070 laptop"` resolve to the same entry. They hold under both peft 0.20 and 0.21. Dropping the patterns in the streaming or PPO path fails a test that prints the wrong rank, and an injected new caller trips the scan. They merged `main` in rather than force-pushing, so earlier review marks stayed traceable ([#959](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/959), [#963](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/963))

They replaced `skipif(os.name == "nt")` with a capability probe and a `requires_symlink` marker across 91 test files, and the numbers say it removes skips rather than coverage: `skipif` across `tests/` drops 152 -> 60 while the assert count goes UP. **The part that earned the merge is the ratchet** that fails the suite when a new test hand-rolls its own probe - it counts 291 offenders on `main` today, so the convention is enforced rather than announced. Forcing the probe True on an unprivileged account fails 45; forcing it False on a capable one fails exactly the test that compares it against a live `os.symlink`. The 91-file merge resolution was audited by re-running git's own automerge and by an AST decorator diff: zero test objects lost, zero non-symlink decorators lost, 147 gained the marker ([#832](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/832), [#1039](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1039))
  - Twice a streamed LoRA run saved an adapter that reloads as nothing — v0.72.0's `.inner.` keys, then #1005's 0-tensor file under peft 0.21.0 — and training reported success both times. Every streamed save, each `checkpoint-*` and the final one, now reads the written file's keys back and raises, naming the file and both counts, on a missing file, a LoRA tensor count other than the model trained, or an `.inner.` key. **The reference is the design decision**: no streamed save is legitimately empty, so the check compares against the trainable count rather than a bare `> 0` and catches a partial adapter too, and the final save is checked on its own because `save_model` dispatches no `on_save`. Their #1005 mutation test makes the guard fire on peft 0.21.0 and skips, saying so, on a peft where the mutation is inert instead of passing vacuously. The head now takes a private copy of its weight only when the slot is shared and the loss runs a reference forward before its backward, and the VRAM pre-flight charges that copy for exactly those runs, so its never-under-predicts contract holds. **Where the decision lives is the part worth copying**: a trainer flag, because the trainer is the one thing that knows whether a second forward runs before the backward, so `sft`, `orpo`, `simpo` and the embedding keep the view and pay nothing (the SFT half was @VanshSharmaPES's catch in #1174). Following the route-1 ruling, any value other than `expand_layers` is now refused at load, naming both fields and the value to set, and the description, the `block_expansion.py` docstrings and both docs pages now say the value must equal `expand_layers`. **The test worth copying is the one through the real `SFTTrainerWrapper.setup()`**: it compares the `requires_grad` set from `named_parameters()` with the appended blocks, so it fails when the freeze is skipped, leaves one new block frozen or leaves the embeddings trainable, not only when a helper returns a different number. 17 of 18 mutations killed, including all five the ruling listed ([#1409](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1409), [#1586](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1586))

The first revision was written against a pre-#712 copy of `validator.py`, so it silently reverted that fix and `soup data validate` went back to reporting `3/3 rows valid` on a file the loader drops two thirds of; twenty existing tests said so. **They then did more than was asked on the second finding**: told that a test pinning both copies of the join-separator arithmetic would be sufficient, they removed the duplication instead, so one helper now serves both functions and mutating it fails two tests where the second copy had survived +0 failures across 1432. The `_to_hashable` collision class ({"messages": []} and {"messages": {}} both canonicalising to `()` and reporting as duplicates on real chat data) is closed and verified over a 184-case differential harness across all eighteen formats. **And they corrected their own number rather than defending it** — the benchmark now interleaves the two implementations instead of timing them sequentially, reporting 1.34x-1.57x, where the version first shipped printed a *slowdown* for a change that was faster.
