- **Ercan Er** ([@Ercaner1988](https://github.com/Ercaner1988))
  - Replaced the path-containment guard that could not catch the next occurrence. The old one asserted `".relative_to(cwd)" not in src` over seven named files, so it leaked in both directions at once — a new module was never looked at, and inside the seven it matched exactly one literal spelling, letting `.relative_to(base)`, a formatter-split call and `.is_relative_to(...)` sail past. Theirs walks the AST of all 501 modules under `src/soup_cli/` and keys on the **call** rather than the text around it, which is what makes the spelling irrelevant. **The judgement call worth copying is the one they declined to make**: separating a `ValueError` handler that denies from one that cosmetically falls back needs taint analysis, and they reported that the cheap heuristic misclassified `commands/adapters.py:38` *in both directions while they were writing it*, so both surviving sites went into an allowlist keyed by `(file, line)` with a mandatory re-derivable reason — plus `test_the_allowlist_has_no_dead_entries`, so a moved site is re-verified instead of rotting into a silent hole. The scan finds nothing outside that allowlist today, which is exactly why `TestTheScannerCanActuallyFail` is load-bearing, and they had written it before being asked. Verified here against a real offender dropped into `src/soup_cli/` rather than synthetic strings: caught by file and line, as were an intermediate-assignment spelling and an aliased `Path as P` / `PurePath` receiver, and a fresh offender appended to the *allowlisted* `adapters.py` was still reported. One assertion removed, replaced in place by a strictly stronger one ([#777](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/777), [#778](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/778))
  - Translated the README into Turkish and built the gate that keeps a translation honest, after I made that gate the condition for accepting any translation. A sha256 stamp alone proves *when* a file was synced, not *what* it contains, so they added a structural check against `README.md` — heading sequence, code-fence languages and inline-code spans — which, run against their own earlier draft, found 29 dropped and 3 invented inline-code spans that heading counts could not see. Files are discovered by glob so a new language cannot land unstamped, the hash is LF-normalised so it survives `core.autocrlf`, and the controls call the same predicates the real assertions use. **They took the narrow version when it was offered** — one language instead of six — and put the cost of the ratchet into numbers themselves: 54 of the last 60 `README.md` commits were direct pushes that will now turn `main` red until the Turkish file is re-synced. Verified: 18/18 and 3/3 headings in order, 13/13 fences, commands byte-identical, all 37 relative links and localised anchors resolving ([#769](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/769), [#852](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/852))
  - Closed the hole in their **own** previous contribution. The #852 sync stamp proves *when* a translation was synced; it does not prove it still says the same thing, so a command changed inside a fence or a figure changed in prose stayed green behind a freshly recomputed stamp. Each `## ` section is now compared on code-block **contents** (with `#` comments dropped, since comments are the one part a translation may legitimately localise), link targets and prose numbers. **Two design choices are better than the obvious version**: anchors are *resolved* rather than matched, because a translated heading necessarily has a different slug and a matching check would have been permanently red; and the comment-dropping came with its own control test. They probed **false positives** rather than assuming none — nine plausible correct Turkish renderings, seven pass — which matters in a repo that has deleted a guard for firing on correct code. Told their changelog figure count was wrong, they rebased past a release that changed the figure set under them and recounted from scratch rather than patching the number: 10 figures, 18 occurrences, 11 lines, independently confirmed, with nothing but separators altered ([#870](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/870), follow-up to [#852](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/852))
  - The README translation gate forced a Turkish re-translation of `What's New` every release, for text that is replaced every release. That section is now exempt. **The design point worth copying:** the exempt list feeds the stamp hash, so widening it changes every stamp and cannot happen quietly. All four conditions of the issue survived mutation in review ([#1013](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1013), [#1020](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1020))
  - Arabic and Japanese READMEs, both behind the same structural ratchet that gates the Turkish one they shipped in v0.75.0 — so a `README.md` edit now has to re-translate and re-stamp three files. Coverage is structural rather than listed (`translations()` globs `README.*.md`), which I proved by appending a line to `README.md` and watching the stamp test name both new files. All four files agree exactly: 21 headings with an identical level sequence position by position, 12 fenced blocks with an identical language sequence, 20 table rows, and the same 34 URLs and 34 relative paths, every path existing on disk. The translations are real rather than nominal — 89.2% of the Arabic prose letters are Arabic, 83.4% of the Japanese are kana/kanji, and the only long Latin-script line is the preprint citation, which correctly stays in English — with 13 `<span dir="ltr">` wrappers so the DOI and version strings still read left-to-right inside RTL prose. Four mutations, four killed, and the guard names the section and the differing command rather than counting lines ([#1100](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1100))
  - Built the CodSpeed trip-wire for `config/schema.py`'s import cost, the dominant leaf of `soup version`'s cold start that they measured in #780. It is one pytest-codspeed benchmark in its own non-required workflow, off until a repository variable enables it and never run on fork PRs, with no stored secret. **They corrected their own proposal in public**: the CodSpeed App's real permissions were far wider than the "read access" they had first described, so they listed them from the App's registration and left the install decision to the maintainer. The benchmark says in its own docstring what it does not measure, and the isolation test that guards its `sys.modules` restore kills all 4 mutations tried ([#1065](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1065), [#1086](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1086))
  - Took the layer-streaming runtime off `soup version`'s import path, using the third option from #780, which they re-measured before proposing it. `config/schema.py` imported ten bounds from `layer_stream`, `async_disk_source` and `ship_verdict`, so every CLI start loaded six modules (`layer_stream`, `layer_shard`, `async_disk_source`, `safetensors_reader`, `queue` and `_queue`) to read ten numbers and two tuples. A dependency-free `utils/config_bounds.py` now holds them and the runtimes re-export it, so the schema bound and each runtime message stay one object. **They stated the saving at its honest size**: about 1.6% of `import soup_cli.cli`, with a wall-clock A/B inside the noise. They named the 22-25% drop in `import soup_cli.config.schema` on its own for what it is, the #780 trap in miniature rather than a win. **They also corrected my suggestion on #780**: an `is` check per re-export cannot see a copied `MAX_NOISE_FLOOR_RUNS = 10`, because CPython caches small ints. So the re-exports are pinned on the source text instead, counting every name bound in store context, the schema's included. Verified: config schemas, values and runtime messages are byte-identical to `main`, and CLI import goes from 526 to 521 modules, with exactly those six leaving. The 78 related test files pass, and all 12 mutations are killed, five of them only after the review round widened the guards ([#780](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/780), [#1368](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1368))

- **KJO92** ([@Konuktor](https://github.com/Konuktor))
  - Stopped every in-process inference path from prompting a tuned model with a sequence it never trained on. `soup chat`, `soup serve`, `soup infer` / `soup bench`, `soup diff`, the `--mole` decoder, `soup data generate` and the `live_eval` generators behind `soup ship` / `diagnose` / `advise` all rendered the chat template to text and then let the tokenizer add its own special tokens, so a Llama-3 / Gemma / Mistral template produced a **doubled BOS** while Soup's default SFT path trains with one, and Soup's own `data.chat_template` presets (which render none) were prompted with one. **The design choice worth copying is the rule they chose over the obvious one**: not "drop a duplicate BOS" but "text a template rendered gets no tokenizer special tokens" — a startswith-BOS check would have fixed the vendor case and silently missed the presets, which need 1 -> 0, not 2 -> 1. `fallback_on_error` is keyword-only with **no default**, so none of the seven call sites could quietly inherit a different error contract, and four hand-rolled copies of the legacy fallback prompt were deleted rather than fixed a fifth time. 62 offline tests over a real fast tokenizer with a genuine BOS/EOS post-processor (30 of them red on `main`), a train/infer prefix property, and an AST ratchet with controls proving it can fail. They reported running 24 mutations; I re-ran three myself and all three were killed — the encoder reverted to pre-fix behaviour failed 23 tests. A first contribution ([#781](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/781), [#782](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/782))
  - Gave `soup ingest` its first network call — `--pull` for Langfuse — and built it around the credential and SSRF surface rather than bolting that on afterwards. The host goes through the shared `validate_webhook_url` (HTTPS only; loopback, private and numeric-IPv4 spellings refused without an explicit opt-in), redirects are refused so `Authorization` cannot follow to another host, server-echoed text is scrubbed before truncation so no cut can expose part of a key, and every call is bounded by a timeout, a size cap, a loud page cap and a capped `Retry-After`. **The test that matters is the recorded one**: a real, sanitised Observations v2 page runs through the pull adapter and then the existing parser with the resulting records asserted — and recording it surfaced three places where the API differs from its docs. Standard-library only, and without `--pull` the path is byte-identical and opens no socket. Verified with 34 hostile hosts, a seven-scenario secret-leak run against a real loopback transport, and 17 mutations, all killed ([#204](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/204), [#859](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/859))
  - Three findings from the review of their own #859, each **reproduced before it was fixed**. The headline one: `timeout` reached `opener.open` and each `read`, so it bounded a single socket operation rather than the call — a server sending one byte before every timeout expired kept the pull alive indefinitely. Measured against a loopback server dripping 20 bytes 0.1s apart, **a 0.4s timeout allowed a 2.01s call**. The body is now read through `read1`, which returns what has arrived instead of blocking for a full chunk, with the wall-clock deadline checked between chunks; a read already in flight can still overshoot by at most the socket timeout, which is the honest bound and is documented as such rather than claimed away. The second finding is the subtler one: a drip-fed **error** body hit the deadline and lost the status it was reporting, so a 401 surfaced as a timeout — `PullError` now belongs to the body-read fallback, with a comment saying *why* it is in that tuple rather than merely listing it. Verified here: 120 passed on the rebased head, and replacing the deadline check in `_read_capped` with `if False:` fails exactly the two deadline tests and nothing else. At first review **5 of the 8 new tests failed against main's source and the 3 that passed were the controls** ([#865](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/865), [#881](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/881))

- **tristangrech** ([@tristangrech](https://github.com/tristangrech))
  - Measured where the layer-streaming pinned store is actually accounted, on an L4 — the hardware nobody in the #622 thread could supply — and published it in `benchmarks/` as a working record. 4 GiB pinned lands in `RssShmem` in both pinned runs while **a pageable control of the same size lands in `RssAnon`**, which is what makes the attribution to pinning conclusive rather than suggestive. Host-wide `Shmem` drift is kept rather than rounded away, per-process and cgroup counters carry the weight, the probe refuses to run without 2x headroom and is embedded with its SHA-256, and the conclusion stays exactly as narrow as the data: `statvfs` on `/dev/shm` is not a capacity bound on that stack, and nothing is claimed about others. They claimed the issue before measuring. A first contribution ([#655](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/655), [#787](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/787))

  - Seven tests asserted `"\x1b" not in result.output` to prove an untrusted field was sanitised. What they actually proved was that the shell did not force colour: under `FORCE_COLOR=1`, Rich styled the surrounding panel and the assertion failed however good the sanitiser was. Each test now pins `Console(force_terminal=False)`, with no assertion weakened. **They corrected the maintainer twice, and were right both times**: the assertion the review suggested was inert under the swallow mutant while theirs fired, and they built the mutation for a seventh pin the review had declined to claim, warning that only one of the two identical anchors (`:232`, not `:247`) makes it fire. When they reported a PR-body fix as done before it was published, they said so plainly and then fixed all five items ([#987](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/987), [#993](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/993))
  - `soup data preprocess` cached rows with no `labels`, so a `pre_tokenized` SFT run trained on every prompt token. TRL's collator falls back to `labels = input_ids`, so the same `soup.yaml` optimised a different objective depending only on whether it had been preprocessed first, and the loss curve looked normal. **The cached labels come from the live path's own `data/loss_mask.py` builders, aligned back onto the cached `input_ids`, rather than from a second copy of the mask that could drift.** The mask mode, including `data.mask_history` and `training.train_on_eot`, joined the cache key as schema `v6`. An AST test finds every trainer that calls the gate and checks the argument it passes, so a new trainer is covered the day it is added. Over four review rounds they closed every finding: an off-by-one that a per-row count could not see, now pinned by comparing the trained token sequences; a `train_on_eot` cache that `task: pretrain` could never load; and `mask_history`, which had reopened the bug under a shared key. On the final head, all 29 mutations of the fix fail a test ([#1054](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1054), [#1120](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1120))
  - `soup bench train` read the SM clock once, after training had returned. On the gate-836 box the field read mostly 180 MHz and never above 1417, against a busy median of 2370–2557 MHz, so it could not tell apart two runs whose throughput differed 1.83x. **A background sampler now reads the clock during the counted steps only, for torch's own device by PCI bus id, and never touches torch or CUDA itself.** Each sample is stamped at the midpoint of its query and cut to the collector's own post-warm-up window. `timing.step_seconds` keeps every counted step in run order, and the docs now say which failures write a report. They asked whether torch's PCI bus id matched `nvidia-smi` rather than assuming it, and on an RTX 5070 Laptop it did. The gate record gained an addendum that leaves the sampler-on/off throughput question open instead of claiming there is no overhead. Over three rounds they closed every blocking item, including two assertions they had dropped in a rewrite, and said so plainly ([#1166](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1166), [#1177](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1177))
  - Took the first of the six saturated bundled suites in the #1192 series off the 1.000 rail: `mini_arithmetic` replaced 12 single-step rows with 16 multi-step or large-operand ones, bumped `BUNDLED_SCORER_REVISION` to 4 and explained why the fingerprint correctly did not move, and published a record that says it is not the 7B reference and that half the strong model's misses are truncation (Qwen2.5-1.5B 0.800, SmolLM2-135M 0.375). After review the test now recomputes every hard key from its own question, which closed two surviving mutations (a wrong question with its key kept); twelve mutations killed, new file fails 5 of 7 on clean main ([#1192](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1192), [#1462](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1462))
  - `soup data augment` wrote rows with an empty rewritten field and `soup distill-prompt` dropped every failed row and wrote an empty file, both exiting 0 while every provider call failed, because neither built its provider with `raise_on_error`; they fixed the cause in both builders, counted every call, treated an empty reply as a failure, exited 1 without a file when nothing was usable and reported the failure count on a partial run, and replaced the five tests that had encoded the defect with ones asserting the raised error and the exact count. After review they escaped provider error text before Rich prints it (an error containing `[/x]` crashed the command, after the file was written on the partial path), tightened five `!= 0` assertions to `== 1`, and covered translate, style and all three escape sites. 16 of 18 new tests fail on clean main, fifteen mutations killed ([#1274](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1274), [#1463](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1463))

- **Aditya Pratap Singh** ([@akkupratap323](https://github.com/akkupratap323))
  - Closed a silent pass in the PR gate `soup ci init` writes: `soup data validate` exited 0 at "0/5 rows valid", so a dataset the loader would drop entirely sailed through CI. Zero usable rows now fails with exit 2 and a reason, while a partial drop still passes as it did — **the change is exactly as narrow as the issue asked**, and it reuses the pass-0 / failed-gate-2 / input-error-1 shape `soup ship` and `soup data lint` already use rather than adding a sixth convention. An opt-in `--min-valid-fraction` adds a stricter gate, with the equal-to-threshold case pinned by a test. Every test kills its mutation, including over-broadening to "any dropped row", which fails four tests. A first contribution ([#811](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/811), [#858](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/858))
  - `soup data stats` wrote 951 raw ANSI bytes into redirected output with `NO_COLOR=1`, because plotext's `show()` goes straight to stdout and Rich's colour decision was never in the path. Plots now build to a string and print through Rich, so there is **one** decision point rather than a hand-rolled TTY/`NO_COLOR` predicate sitting beside Rich's own. The second half is the one worth naming: Rich's `Console.size` returns `width - legacy_windows`, so on Windows Rich reports 79 columns where plotext has already drawn at 80, and that one-column overhang wrapped **every** row — 42 emitted lines against 23. Asked for it, they did not merely add `soft_wrap=True`; they wrote a test that discriminates, asserting at `Console(width=4)` both the line count and that a long line survives intact, which also kills the near-miss variant (`no_wrap` without `crop=False`) a future refactor would most likely introduce ([#824](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/824), [#860](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/860))
  - Two more rows off the #275 model-family tracker, `minimax-m3-dpo` and `mistral-large-3-dpo`, each the DPO sibling of an existing SFT entry. **Both base repo ids were re-checked with `huggingface_hub.model_info(..., token=False)` rather than with an HTTP status**, which is the exact distinction [#661](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/661) exists for and the step most recipe PRs skip. They also took two bookkeeping corrections without argument — the fragment was named after the issue instead of the PR, and the credit line named the wrong number — and the follow-up diff was the rename plus two lines with nothing else touched, which is what makes a correction round cheap to re-verify ([#849](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/849), [#933](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/933))
  - Added the `qwen3.6-27b-dpo` and `qwen3.6-27b-grpo` recipes, taking the catalog to 176. All three review asks landed, and the cross-reference was fixed the stronger way: the recipe count was dropped from it rather than bumped, so that sentence cannot go stale again. On the tree merged with `main`, the count agrees at all nine sites and the config snapshot is a clean regeneration. The changelog says plainly that the 27B configs were validated against the schema and the CLI but not trained ([#845](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/845), [#1015](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1015))
  - `soup loop canary --traffic 5%` wrote a policy that serving and the watch daemon never read, so there was no traffic split, no live verdict and no rollback. Keyed `/v1/chat/completions` requests now follow the configured split, and every outcome, streamed errors included, is recorded against a per-promotion rollout id. `soup loop watch` rolls back a `MAJOR` canary after at least 30 samples and keeps a healthy one. Across four review rounds each surviving mutation was closed with the discriminating test, and on the merged tree 17 of 17 mutations fail a test ([#815](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/815), [#1104](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1104))

- **Zhengzhuo Wang** ([@wangzhengzhuo05](https://github.com/wangzhengzhuo05))
  - `soup doctor` treated the seven `[train]` packages as required core dependencies, so a supported core-only install reported seven failures **and exited 0** — wrong in both directions at once, alarming about a correct install while staying silent about a broken one. The training stack is now a single optional group, a missing or incompatible core dependency exits 1 while optional packages and the train group never change the exit code, and the "Fix all" suggestion is two commands rather than one, because `--index-url` *replaces* PyPI and the PyTorch index hosts no soup-cli, trl, peft or transformers. **They also fixed the adjacent drift they found**: `soup version --full` derived extras from a hand-kept list, and now reads distribution metadata, so `scikit-learn` and `pillow` resolve and train and mcp appear on a `[dev]` install. Three rounds of maintainer mutations, every one killed — bare per-package floors, a missing core dependency exiting 0, collapsing the two commands into one `--index-url`, an unescaped fix-all payload, and reverting extras detection to `find_spec`. Both suggested commands were checked to resolve under `pip install --dry-run`. A first contribution ([#828](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/828), [#854](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/854))
  - A hostile Langfuse server could paint the terminal of anyone running `soup ingest --pull`. The error body was rendered through bare `rich.markup.escape`, which neutralises `[...]` markup **and nothing else**, so C0 bytes (ESC, NUL, BEL) in a server-supplied message arrived live. Routed through the shared `utils/terminal.py:for_terminal` instead of a tenth private copy, which is the direction the repo is moving anyway. **The restraint is the part worth naming**: the neighbouring `except ValueError` branches were left on `escape`, and that is correct — the host validator refuses a control byte upstream, so widening the patch would have added untested surface for no reachable defect. Verified here: both call sites are individually pinned, reverting them to `escape` fails exactly two tests rather than one shared assertion ([#885](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/885), [#893](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/893))
  - v0.75.0 made an unknown config key a hard error, but `soup plan` and `soup apply` **never ran the check** — they read the YAML as a plain mapping rather than through the loader ([#894](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/894)). So a typo `soup train` refuses was silently accepted by the pre-flight command whose entire job is to tell you what the run will do, and the plan was built from a config the trainer would reject — surfacing later with nothing tying it back to the key. They routed both commands through the same detection, so **the pre-flight and the run now agree about which configs are legal**, which is the property that makes a pre-flight worth running at all. This was a documented v0.75.0 known limitation rather than a regression, so it is one that could have gone on being noted instead of closed. ([#909](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/909))

- **송은우** ([@be-student](https://github.com/be-student))
  - PPO accepted `training.epochs` and `training.ppo_kl_penalty`, documented them, and forwarded neither to trl 0.29, so a run silently used trl's own defaults — this project's most common defect shape, an option validated and read by nothing. Both now reach the trainer under the current names while the legacy spellings still work on older trl, and PPO optimisation epochs stay separate from dataset epochs rather than being conflated, which the rename invites. **Theirs was the only PPO PR that drives a real `PPOTrainerWrapper.setup()` against a config whose values are trl's own defaults**, so a silent fallback cannot pass the test, and the only one implementing the issue's effective-schedule report. Four review items were finished by the maintainer after a publicly announced date passed; their commit is untouched and the merge commit names exactly which commits were whose ([#721](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/721), [#737](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/737))
  - `reverse_kl` returned a finite loss and a NaN gradient, so a distillation run looked healthy while learning nothing from that position. The cause is that `kl_div(log_t, p_s)` recomputes `log(target)` from a probability that has already underflowed to zero. **The repair is a reformulation, not a guard**, and the distinction is the whole review: one clamp was removed and none added. Reverse KL becomes `(p_s * (log_s - log_t)).sum(-1)`, where an underflowed `p_s` multiplies a large finite `log_s` and gives the term's true limit; JS uses `logaddexp(log_s, log_t) - log(2)`, the exact identity for the mixture. The old `m.clamp(min=1e-12).log()` was itself the masking construct — measured on identical distributions with a -40 logit it returns **-5.25e-17, a negative divergence**, where this kernel returns 0.0. Objective preservation was verified rather than asserted: against the previous kernel, value and backward gradient agree to fp32 round-off (rel 1.2e-07 / 1.5e-07 / 3.8e-07), and against a closed-form fp64 reference, 1.1e-08 / 1.2e-07 / 3.5e-08. Where it differs in bf16/fp16, the old one is the wrong one. Two earlier rounds were reworked without argument: fp32 upcasting narrowed to where it is needed, and a `log(2)` -> `log(3)` mutation that had **survived** the first review is now a pure numerical kill ([#719](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/719), [#736](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/736))
  - `soup migrate` chose its parser from the file suffix, so a JSONL file named `.json` went to the YAML parser and the user got a bare `ParserError("expected '<document start>', but found '{'")` — **a traceback about YAML for a file that was never YAML**, with nothing in it naming the real problem ([#699](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/699)). They could have special-cased the suffix and closed the ticket; instead detection became structural, and then *bounded*: a 64 KiB prefix must hold **at least two** complete JSON objects on separate lines. Both halves are load-bearing — unbounded content sniffing is its own problem, and requiring two is what stops a single JSON config being read as a one-line stream. Explicit `.jsonl` unchanged, notebooks and single configs still routed to their own migrator. The regression test is pinned by a mutation whose failure message **is the bug report**: `object_count == 2` to `== 3` reproduces the exact `ParserError` across all four parametrisations. ([#730](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/730))

- **Areen Agrawal** ([@Areen-09](https://github.com/Areen-09))
  - First contribution, and it is a recipe pair that quietly did the hard half right. `moonshotai/Kimi-K2.5` shipped SFT only; these complete the DPO/GRPO trio from the K2.6 templates one release away. Both new recipes are **byte-identical to their K2.6 siblings apart from `base:`** — including the asymmetry the issue flagged and most people would have tidied away: `kimi-k2.6-grpo` drops `moe_aux_loss_coeff` while the DPO recipe keeps it, and each copy mirrors *its own* template rather than being normalised to look consistent. The other half is the count. A recipe PR touches a number that lives in nine places plus a regenerated snapshot fixture, and this one moved **167 -> 169 everywhere it is live** while correctly leaving the historical references alone — a previous release's CHANGELOG section and a docstring recording the prior step. The fixture regeneration is **purely additive**, +29 lines with no existing entry touched, which is what makes it reviewable at all. Verified here: both recipes are covered by the [#621](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/621) ratchet — mutating the `dpo_beta` schema default turns them red alongside the rest, so they are genuinely protected rather than merely present in the file ([#851](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/851), [#872](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/872))
  - Second contribution: `qwen3.6-35b-a3b-dpo` and `qwen3.6-35b-a3b-grpo`, taking the catalog 172 -> 174. **What makes a recipe pair trustworthy is the two-surface pin, and theirs is stronger than the review credited it as** — mutating the recipe YAML's `base:` while leaving `model=` in the `RecipeMeta` intact kills three tests, not the two recorded: the pair in `test_recipes.py` plus the snapshot guard in `test_issue621_recipe_snapshot.py`. Under that same mutation `test_recipe_meta_pins_the_model_id` **passes**, and that asymmetry is the point rather than a gap: the `RecipeMeta` surface is blind to the YAML, so only a test that parses the YAML can catch the two drifting apart. The snapshot is a clean regeneration verified by re-running the generator to an empty `git status`, not a hand-edit; all 13 count-ratchet sites move together; and the eight removed test lines are every one of them the ratchet going 172 -> 174, with `test_issue427_qwen35_text_modality.py` gaining two lines rather than losing any. They also took a rebase without complaint when [#956](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/956) landed 171 -> 172 underneath them, and the ratchet is built so that failing to rebase fails **loudly** at five named sites rather than drifting ([#846](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/846), [#978](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/978))
  - The `AcceleratorError`-as-OOM guard in the layer-streaming probe lived inside `measure_step_peak_bytes`' `except` block, so the only way to reach it was a real probe on a real card — which is why it **skipped in all nine CI cells while shipping to every user**. The verdict moves into `_classify_probe_exception`, which takes the exception and returns the classification, and the call site delegates to it. **The extraction is the fix**; the test that becomes possible is the point of it. The first revision left the call site unpinned and that mutation survived — on this one, unpinning the delegation fails a test that needs no CUDA, which is exactly the coverage the old shape could not have had. Verified here on the tree merged against current `main` under both peft 0.20.0 and 0.21.0: 19 passed on each, and all 17 test files referencing `layer_stream_runtime` 830 passed / 13 skipped on each ([#1002](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1002), [#1047](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1047))
  - The `docs/commands.md` coverage guard matched its regex against the WHOLE line, so a row's description could credit a command the command column never named. They narrowed the match to the command column - and when the first round showed the isolation itself was unpinned (`cmd = _command_column(line)` -> `cmd = line` left 18 tests green), they took the discriminating test as given and added it in the right class rather than arguing the mutation was unrealistic. Seven mutations now: five killed, two proven equivalent. The five doc rows the stricter guard newly refuses were fixed against live `--help` output, not against the diff ([#1046](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1046), [#1094](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1094))
  - A Bearer token containing a non-ASCII character made the Web UI's token check raise `TypeError`, so the request failed with a 500 and a traceback on the server console instead of a 401. **The header is now compared as UTF-8 bytes through one constant-time helper used by both auth paths**, pinned by a spy test that fails if the comparison ever becomes `==`, and a guard that walks the whole package for a `compare_digest` call on a str. They took both requested changes and both optional ones in one push ([#1195](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1195), [#1246](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1246))

Substring hits made it worse: `a3b` read as 3B, `11b` as 1B, `17b` as 7B. They replaced the list with a boundary-safe size parse, handling `8x7B` MoE totals, Llama 4 `17B-16E` active-plus-expert names, `-A22B` active tokens and OLMoE's active-first `1B-7B`. On the 91 catalog bases with a known real count, estimates within 10% went from 34 to 61, and 2x-or-worse under-predictions from 40 to 16. **What made it trustworthy was the yardstick**: the catalog test now checks real parameter totals rather than recipe labels, so a label wrong in the same direction as the estimate (Scout's "17B", DeepSeek-V3-0324's "7B") can no longer hide one. A reverse check keeps both tables in step with the catalog, which is how #1132's repointed ids were caught and fixed in the rebase rather than drifting. - **Harsh Raj Singhania** ([@HarshRajSinghania](https://github.com/HarshRajSinghania))
  - First contribution. With six of seven `[train]` packages installed, `soup doctor` announced `Training stack not installed` and recommended `pip install "soup-cli[train]"` — a command that is a no-op for the six already satisfied. `group_missing` flipped on the first absent member, so **the usual broken install, one missing wheel, was reported as no install at all**. A partial stack now names what is missing. The same patch gates the NVIDIA wheel-tag hint on torch actually being absent: with torch present on a CUDA box that hint explains how to obtain a CUDA build of a package you already have, and it was printing anyway. **What decided this over a competing patch was the test.** The wheel-tag gate had a mutation that survived on the earlier head — dropping `and torch_missing` changed nothing any test could see — and they added `test_doctor_nvidia_partial_stack_with_torch_present`, after which that mutation fails exactly one test and no others. The assertion is `"index-url" not in out` rather than a specific sentence, which catches the hint appearing in either `issues` or `fix_pre`; a sentence match would have missed the duplicate line. They also took a consolidation request seriously when arguing that the original patch already passed its own tests would have been easier ([#875](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/875), [#884](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/884))
  - Two `soup shrink` containment tests downloaded a tokenizer from the Hugging Face Hub before asserting a purely local refusal, so a slow runner turned an arbitrary-write guard into a red cell on unrelated PRs. **The diagnosis was the valuable half**: the Hub call lived in the test helper, not in `soup shrink`, so the command needed no reordering. The `--output-dir` test now stubs `_resolve_trc` to raise, which pins the refusal to run before any model load (moving the refusal after the config load fails it). It and the calib test now assert the refusal text rather than `"cwd"` anywhere in the output, which pytest's own temp-directory name had been satisfying. Verified on the merged tree with the network blocked: 3 passed with zero connection attempts, where `main` fails 2 of 3 after 185 s; three of four containment mutations killed, the fourth uncaught on `main` too ([#1076](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1076), [#1077](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1077))

- **Vansh Sharma** ([@VanshSharmaPES](https://github.com/VanshSharmaPES))
  - First contribution. Six command modules, `utils/ship_verdict.py` and `mcp_server/registry.py` each carried a **byte-identical** private copy of the C0/DEL control-strip table instead of importing v0.75.0's shared `utils/terminal.py:for_terminal` — which is exactly how the config loader's own fix went missing for a whole release. **The find that justifies the ratchet is the ninth copy**, which a grep for `_CONTROL_STRIP_TABLE` would never have located: `commands/serve.py`'s `--auto-spec` pairing message had the same table under a local `_ctrl` name. So the guard walks the whole AST rather than matching an identifier, and catches the name, a renamed module-scope copy and a function-local copy alike. It finds zero offenders today, which makes its own can-it-fail test the load-bearing half — and that test was added after review rather than argued away. Two theoretical spellings and the parse-failure case are recorded as limits rather than chased, which is the right call: a guard that chases every spelling is how guards become unmaintainable ([#896](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/896), [#907](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/907))
  - `proof-4gb.ipynb` pinned an unreleased version, so anyone running the notebook got a different tool than the one the proof was measured with ([#844](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/844)). They repinned it to a released version **and re-ran it on a real T4** rather than repinning and assuming — the distinction that matters for a notebook whose entire purpose is evidence. A correction they made mid-review was itself correct. ([#910](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/910))
  - `benchmarks/run-t4-colab-free-tier.md` forecast 31-46 tok/s for the free-tier T4 from an assumed clock; the card actually runs at a measured **525 MHz**, 33% of its 1590 MHz boost, so the record now carries 20.39 TFLOPS measured and a 288-423 bracket. Every cited figure is traceable into the notebook re-run in [#910](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/910) and the arithmetic reproduces (`20.39/2.20 = 9.27`, `288/31 = 9.29`, `423/46 = 9.20`). **The detail worth copying is one of the three stale markings**: the `31-46` occurrence sitting inside the captured transcript is left **verbatim**, because that is what the panel printed — rewriting a number inside a recorded transcript turns a record into a reconstruction, and they marked it in place instead. The rebase was the real risk, since #910 edited the same file, and `range-diff` shows both commits `=` with the diff against main still exactly +42/-1. They were also explicit that this publishes a **corrected but still unvalidated** forecast rather than letting a fixed number read as a validated one, and said so in the file itself — which is what turned the maintainer's unmet acceptance criterion into its own tracked issue ([#999](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/999)) instead of a quietly half-met box ([#714](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/714), [#967](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/967))
  - Removed the two orphaned BitNet helpers (`utils/bitnet.build_bitnet_trainer`, `utils/bitnet.validate_bitnet_compat`) that nothing imported, per the ruling on #1004, and replaced their tests with absence pins: restoring either as a stub, or re-exporting the live wrapper under the old name, fails a test. **When review showed that an extra change to the async disk reader rested on a race that did not exist, they reverted it within minutes and said so plainly**, so the squashed diff touches no streaming file ([#1004](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1004), [#1034](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1034))
  - On an untied streamed checkpoint, `embed_tokens` and `lm_head` share one device slot, so every step's first lookup waited for a whole head-sized copy that `_prime()` issued with nothing to overlap it. The issue first blamed the disk reader's `_plan_queue`. They showed the cost was the shared slot's ping-pong instead, and retracted a sentence of their own analysis that nobody had challenged. The fix reloads the embedding at the tail of the previous step's backward and keeps `_prime()`'s load as a same-owner fallback: 18.6-22.7 ms of embedding wait became 0.12-0.19 ms per step on an RTX 5070, bit-identical to `main` and to resident (CPU fp32 for SFT, ORPO, SimPO, DPO and KTO; CUDA bf16 for SFT and DPO). **The part worth copying is the guard, not the speedup**: the refill's only GPU-side protection is the `wait_stream` in `LargeLayerBufferPool.load_async`. They committed a pool-level GPU test for it that does not depend on host speed, ran it on their own RTX 3050 (5/5 passing, and 5/5 failing with the guard patched out), and said so when a first mutation attempt had run against unmodified code. A 4-layer test pins that the refill fires once per backward, at layer 0. 10 of 12 mutations fail the tests; the survivors are an equivalent flag check and the GPU-only guard ([#975](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/975), [#1178](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1178))
  - Once #1178 was in, what was left of an untied streamed checkpoint's large-layer wait was the output head's load into the shared slot. It was issued at the last decoder layer, where it could hide behind only that layer's compute. They moved it to right after the embedding lookup (`StreamPrefetcher.head_prefetch_layer`, default `0`; `None` restores the old timing), at no extra VRAM: the cheap alternative to #1174's second permanent slot. On an RTX 5070 Laptop GPU the step's median total wait fell from 11.96 to 4.50 ms on the RAM tier and from 15.83 to 1.42 ms on the disk tier, with every layer-0 sample below every last-layer one. A decoder copy now queues behind the head's and gives back part of the saving; their CPU trace of the copy order had shown that queueing before the card measured it. **The part worth copying is that they built the instrument before the number existed.** The switch is a plain attribute, validated on assignment, so one process runs both timings. The interleaved A/B driver they wrote for a card they do not own has an A/A control, rotating arm order and a record of the tree it measured, and it ran on that card as one command per configuration. When review found that the provenance change dropped the NVIDIA driver version from every record and recorded the driver's checkout instead of the measured tree, they fixed both and pinned them with tests within ten minutes. They then covered the driver's own `main()` with a CPU test, so all 11 mutations of its provenance code fail. Gradients and adapters are bit-identical across SFT, SFT at GA 2, ORPO, SimPO, DPO, KTO and tied SFT; all 8 mutations of the new validation are killed; and the pool-level GPU guard fails 3 of 3 with the `wait_stream` removed ([#1174](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1174), [#1258](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1258))

- **fei** ([@feiiiiii5](https://github.com/feiiiiii5))
  - First contribution, and it fixes the command whose whole job is to report this project's most common defect. `soup doctor --config` printed `Every setting this config writes is read on this backend.` for a `task=sft backend=mlx` config setting `use_liger: true` and `neftune_alpha: 5` — two fields MLX never reads. **The wording was the real defect, not the table.** A seven-entry table produced a sentence about the entire config, so the all-clear was a guarantee built on evidence that did not support it, and the fix scopes it: `None of the N setting(s) known to be unread on task=<task> backend=<backend> is set in this config.` The two repro fields were added to `_MLX_SFT` with matching branches in `trainer/mlx_sft.py::_check_unsupported`, so the trainer reports them too rather than only the doctor. Each entry was grep-checked for zero hits across the three MLX modules **before** being declared unread, which is the step that keeps a declared table honest. Verified here against the issue's exact repro in both directions — on `main` that config prints the all-clear, with this change it prints the two-row table and the scoped count — and confirmed no conflict with the five other open PRs touching `commands/doctor.py`. The narrowing to 2 of the issue's 11 fields was declared rather than hidden, and the follow-up for the remaining nine is filed ([#903](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/903), [#911](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/911))

- **ump45nose** ([@ump45nose](https://github.com/ump45nose))
  - First contribution. `soup doctor` had defined an MLX panel that **nothing ever rendered** — `_get_mlx_info()` and `get_mlx_info()` both existed and `doctor()` called neither — so on Apple Silicon the backend Soup supports for training did not appear in Soup's own diagnostic at all. **The second half is the one that would have been easy to miss and is arguably worse**: `get_mlx_version()` read `mlx.__version__`, which the package does not export on its top-level module (it lives on `mlx.core` and in distribution metadata), so a correctly installed MLX reported `unknown` — a report that reads as a broken install rather than as a broken lookup. All three states are now covered, informational and non-fatal: MLX present with version, chip and unified memory; Apple Silicon without MLX with an install hint; non-Apple. They answered four substantive review rounds and had every item fixed within days. **What kept this open from 2026-09-04 was the maintainer's follow-through, not theirs** — two announced dates passed without the last two items being picked up, and they were eventually finished in-tree as a fast-forward so none of their commits were rewritten ([#659](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/659), [#660](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/660))

- **Rayan** ([@Rayan-and-beyond](https://github.com/Rayan-and-beyond))
  - First contribution. MCP's `tool_data_doctor` accepted a blank or unknown `format` where its sibling `tool_data_validate` refuses one — #882 had fixed the validate side and left this behind, so the **same malformed argument produced a refusal through one tool and silent misbehaviour through the other**. Both now agree: omitted means auto-detect, blank and unknown are refused, and the error names the rejected value beside the accepted set, while staying path-free and free of user input as `McpToolError` requires. **The context deserves recording**: they lost a different issue to a seven-second claim collision I created, waited without complaint while I twice offered them work that turned out to be already taken, and this landed anyway ([#889](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/889), [#952](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/952))

Such a config is now refused at load, with a pointer to the export path that does work. **The choice worth recording:** they deleted the compat call rather than reordering it, and that is what closes the side doors. They retired the recipe that could not run, and kept every recipe-count site and the config snapshot in sync through two moves of `main`. Replacing the refusal with `return self` fails nine tests ([#825](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/825), [#981](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/981))

- **Isaac Emmanuel** ([@1cbyc](https://github.com/1cbyc))
  - First contribution. `soup draft distill` and `soup shrink` each render a `soup.yaml` themselves and hand it to `load_config_from_string`. **The intent to fail fast was already written down** — both call sites carry the comment `# validate before spending a subprocess` — but neither caught anything, so a field-name typo in either template surfaced as a raw `ValueError` traceback seconds before a subprocess that can run for hours. Both now print through the project's error path and exit 1. **The detail that makes this the right patch rather than a passable one** is that it routes the message through the shared `utils/terminal.py:for_terminal` instead of adding a tenth private control-strip copy — the exact duplication [#896](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/896) exists to ratchet against — and one of the two tests per command feeds an ESC byte and live Rich markup to prove it. Mutation-checked here: deleting the `except` in `shrink.py` fails exactly those two tests behaviourally. The declared limit is that the tests raise from a patched loader rather than from a genuinely malformed rendered config, so they pin the handling branch and not the template's ability to produce one ([#895](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/895), [#904](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/904))

- **jarik2014** ([@jarik2014](https://github.com/jarik2014))
  - First contribution. The repo-wide control-strip ratchet walked every tracked file under `src/soup_cli/` and parsed it, and swallowed a `SyntaxError` with `except SyntaxError: continue`. **A file that does not parse is a worse problem than the duplicate the guard exists to find**, and nothing else in the suite reported it as one — it would have surfaced later as an `ImportError` somewhere unrelated. They saw the second-order defect too, and it is the better half: skipping made a clean answer mean *"zero offenders among the files I could read"*, which is indistinguishable from a genuinely clean run. One `except` clause replaced by `pytest.fail` naming the file; walk, name check and shape check untouched. Their probe registers the broken file with `git add -N` rather than only writing it to disk, so it is genuinely part of the `git ls-files` walk the guard performs — the difference between testing the guard and testing a fixture. **Two rounds, and the second was theirs to notice:** told the failure path could leave the broken `.py` behind, they moved the cleanup inside the `try` and wrote a comment explaining *why* both lines sit there, which is the thing the next person would otherwise undo while tidying. Reproduced by holding `index.lock` so `git add -N` fails: before, an untracked `zz_unparseable_guard_probe.py` was left in the tree; after, `git status --porcelain` is empty. ([#951](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/951))

- **Vladimir Babin** ([@chiliec](https://github.com/chiliec))
  - First contribution. `soup doctor` warned when an installed `[train]` package sat beyond the ceiling Soup declares for it — and then exited 0 ([#874](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/874)). **A CI job running it as a pre-flight therefore passed on the exact stack Soup says it does not support**, which makes the check decorative rather than a gate; they made the exit code carry the warning it was already printing. The pairing is what makes it worth having: [#368](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/368) had already taught `soup env check` to audit installed versions against the declared bounds, and this stops `doctor` reporting a green line about the same fact. Verified on the merged tree rather than on the branch — `main` took sixteen merges after the review, two of them into `commands/doctor.py`, and it merges clean over both; the mutation still dies on `assert 0 == 1`, and `soup doctor` stays on the light CLI path (`torch in sys.modules: False` after `doctor --help`). ([#877](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/877))
  - `task: distill` with `backend: unsloth` validated, loaded and trained on plain transformers, because nothing in the distill path reads the backend. `soup train --dry-run` even printed `Backend: unsloth (fast mode)` for a run that never touched it. It is now refused at config load, next to the `mlx` refusal, with a message that names the reason and points at `backend: transformers`. That one check covers `soup train`, `soup doctor --config` and `soup sweep`. **They checked the shipped configs before refusing anything**, which is the step #1186 asked for and the easiest one to skip. On the second round they ran the review's exact mutation before saying it was fixed. All 207 shipped configs still load, and 6 of 7 mutations fail tests ([#1186](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1186), [#1193](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1193))
  - The error handler printed exception text as Rich markup, so install hints lost their `[extra]`, control bytes reached the terminal, and an error containing `[/]` crashed the handler; every print site, including both `--verbose` traceback panels, now goes through `for_terminal` ([#1200](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1200), [#1202](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1202))
  - `training.expand_layers` (LLaMA Pro block expansion) was accepted on a quantized base, where the appended blocks start as `-absmax` instead of zeros under NF4 and a positive `freeze_trainable_layers` crashes on integer weights. They refused it at config load in a `SoupConfig` validator. **When a reviewer showed their first placement ran before the #795 quantization resolver and refused configs that load unquantized on eight tasks, they moved the check behind it and took the test patch as written.** After #1390 and #1399 landed they rebased onto one commit with the scope refusal first, so a config that is out of scope and quantized gets the scope reason. Verified: 247 passed on the three files (1616 across 22 related files), and all 10 mutations of the check fail a test ([#1237](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1237), [#1308](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1308))

- **00200200** ([@00200200](https://github.com/00200200))
  - First contribution. The backend-support guard harvested field names with `re.findall(r"\b(?:training|data)\.[a-z_]+", ...)`, which **stops at the first digit** — so `training.use_fsdp2_compile` was harvested as `training.use_fsdp` and `training.bnb_4bit_use_double_quant` as `training.bnb_` ([#964](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/964)). The guard then compared an **invented** name against the registry, so for any field with a digit in it it could neither confirm a real entry nor catch a missing one. Widening the character class is the obvious half; the half that matters is what they added beside it — every harvested name is cross-checked against the real `TrainingConfig` / `DataConfig` `model_fields`, failing with *warning list names fields that do not exist*. **A widened regex that starts inventing a different wrong name is no better than the narrow one it replaced**, and that check is the whole difference. A competing patch carried the widening without it. Unblocks #961 and #965. ([#966](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/966))

- **Ege** ([@ege-arhan](https://github.com/ege-arhan))
  - First contribution. A polish pass over the Turkish README for the v0.75.0 content ([#924](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/924)). Translations here sit behind a **structural** sync gate — every `README.*.md` carries a sha256 stamp of `README.md` and must mirror each section's fenced-code languages, external URLs and inline code — so the interesting question is never the prose but whether any technical token moved. None did: token sets extracted from the removed and added lines match as multisets on inline code, URLs, links, version strings, fences and digits, and the stamp recomputes to the merged tree's README byte for byte. ([#957](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/957))
  - `soup doctor --config` declared seven CUDA-only fields as ignored by the MLX backend and missed four reachable ones (`use_mod`, `moe_lora`, `quantization_aware`, `use_fsdp2_compile`). **The entry worth reading here is a deletion.** Their first version carried an inline regex widening that duplicated [#964](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/964) in a weaker form — no trailing anchor, no cross-check of harvested names against `model_fields` — and when [#966](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/966) landed that work properly they **dropped their own hunk** rather than keep a second implementation that also happened to pass, so the merged diff inherits both properties instead of re-deriving them: zero extractor change, zero removed lines. They also got the hard field right — `use_fsdp2_compile` is *reachable but refused earlier*, at `soup train` time rather than inside the MLX trainer, so calling it a plain "MLX ignores this" would have been false, and the clause says which. Both directions of the registry guard are mutation-pinned: deleting a registry entry fails their own new test, and deleting the trainer's warn branch while keeping the entry fails two more **by name**, so the table cannot drift from the trainer without a test saying which way it went. They waited out a five-day hold caused by a race the maintainer created — publishing the reachability finding on a third thread minutes before a second contributor implemented it — and confirmed they wanted to continue rather than letting it lapse ([#958](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/958), [#961](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/961))

Worth recording that one of the two reasons the maintainer gave when filing that issue was wrong, and was corrected in review rather than left standing. They read the encoding as a mapping and passed its attention mask to `generate`, kept tensor and list tokenizers working, and tested it with a real `PreTrainedTokenizerFast` and a tiny random-init Llama instead of a fake. In one follow-up push they added the fragment, the CPU `setup()` test with a val split, a content assertion and a stricter fake teacher. - **Rayan-and-beyond** ([@Rayan-and-beyond](https://github.com/Rayan-and-beyond))
  - MCP's `tool_data_doctor` accepted any `format` argument while its sibling `tool_data_validate` had been validating the same field since #882 — so one of two adjacent tools refused a typo and the other acted on it. Brought into line: omitted still means auto-detect, blank and unknown are refused, and the error names both the rejected value and the accepted set ([#952](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/952))

Refused at parse, with the error pointing at the BitNet/TQ1_0 *export* path that does work, and the unusable `falcon-e-bitnet-sft` recipe retired rather than left as a recipe nobody can run — with the recipe-count and snapshot bookkeeping synced in the same change ([#981](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/981))
  - `soup train` printed `Loss: 0.0000` on the MLX SFT and unlearn paths where **no loss had been measured**, so a run that measured nothing looked identical to one whose loss had genuinely reached zero. **A placeholder indistinguishable from a real result is worse than no number**, because zero is a value people act on; both paths now report the value as unavailable rather than substituting a float. All three review points were addressed and the branch rebased without being chased twice.   - First contribution, and it removes a maintenance tax rather than a bug: the recipe count lived as a hand-edited integer in nine places, and four of the assertions that would have caught a stale copy sat deliberately outside the ratchet checking the rest ([#1016](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1016)). `scripts/sync_recipe_count.py` derives it from the catalog and writes every site, so there is one source and eight consumers. **The part worth crediting is how they handled being second in line.** [#1016](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1016) was claimed by another contributor before this PR opened and I gave them the order, not the quality judgement; when [#1033](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1033) shipped the overlapping half, they rebased down to the four files that were genuinely left and rewrote their own description to match, rather than arguing the overlap. They then rebased a second time when the branch went twelve commits behind and **re-derived the count against the new base instead of assuming it still held**. Verified here on the tree merged with current `main`: count still matches the catalog after the day's merges, `ruff --select F821` clean, 47 passed across the touched tests and the fragment ratchet ([#1016](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1016), [#1051](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1051))

- **Jakub Krasuski** ([@Blizzeq](https://github.com/Blizzeq))
  - First contribution, and it closes a silent-wrong-data defect: `data.chat_template` reached NEITHER half of the preprocess cache - the rendering ignored the override and the key did not name it, so two configs differing only in their template shared one cache entry and whichever ran first decided the tokens both of them trained on. Measured on `main` before the fix: a config with an override produced a cache holding the SHIPPED template's markup, and an unregistered template name was accepted with exit 0. **What makes it a good first PR is the second round.** The first head weakened the repo's only positive pin on the cache schema token into a vacuous `!=` - a six-field blob can never equal a five-field one - so forcing the token back to `v2` left 18 tests green; they restored a real `==` pin, and v2, v3 and v5 now each fail. Nine mutations, nine kills, one per half: key, render, training gate, metadata provenance, and both directions of the new refusal message. The fixture for "a cache written before the fix" is a real main-written key rather than a hand-rolled stand-in - I reproduced it byte for byte ([#1067](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1067), [#1082](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1082))
  - Took the follow-up named in the merge note of their [#1082](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1082): an unregistered `data.chat_template` name escaped as a bare `KeyError` traceback. In `soup train` it only escaped after the model had downloaded and loaded, and `--dry-run` and `--cloud` passed it with exit 0. **They measured where it escaped instead of counting call sites.** That put the `train` check right after the config loads, which covers every `soup train` run, rather than in the two trainer paths where it surfaced. Review then found a terminal-safety gap: the name came from `soup.yaml`, so ESC/BEL bytes and Rich markup in it reached the terminal. They routed the message through the shared `for_terminal`, and then pinned both of its halves. Control bytes and a `x[/]` name that used to raise `MarkupError` each have their own test, and each fails if its half is removed. The final head has 13 mutations and 13 kills, with and without forced colour ([#1175](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1175))
  - `soup data preprocess` keyed its cache on the tokenizer, template and mask mode but not on what decides which rows exist, so configs differing only in `val_split` or the replay settings shared one cache and a second run overwrote the first. **The key now comes from one helper and a declared table covering every `DataConfig` field, with a test that fails on an unlisted one**, and the `pre_tokenized` gate refuses a changed row-set input. They audited past the issue and keyed four more fields the loader really reads, and their hand-written one-field-apart cases caught moves the table walk alone would miss; 15 of 15 mutations fail a test ([#1127](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1127), [#1253](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1253))
  - Took all four #1272 follow-ups from the #1253 review within an hour of the issue being filed: a refused `pre_tokenized` cache now says why. `metadata.json` records the key generation and the row-set inputs, so a cache from before row-set keying says it predates #1127, and a current cache names the `data.*` fields that changed instead of showing two bare hashes. The seven exclusion reasons now describe the code and are tied to the staged-field and consumer allowlists, and the gate's `task` lost its default. **Review found the oldest-first order unpinned**, because the writer change left the older-cache fixtures carrying the new fields. They added the supplied test and took all three optional points in the same commit, so an unreadable `dataset_key` now names no field. All 15 mutations fail a test at the final head ([#1272](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1272), [#1281](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1281))

- **Rishabh Jangid** ([@rishcx](https://github.com/rishcx))
  - `lookup_gpu_rate` matched GPU names as SUBSTRINGS, so `A100X`, `A1000` and `RTX A1000 Laptop GPU` were all priced as an A100 and a general row could shadow a specific one. Every pattern is now word-anchored at both ends, the table is ordered most-specific-first, and current hardware (L4, H200, B200, RTX 40/50, RTX PRO 6000) has rates - which also turns the `soup loop` budget gate on for those cards. Two rounds, and the second closed both surviving mutations: the trailing-`\b` case is now pinned twice, by a hygiene test AND by a behavioural fixture, so a variant that keeps a textual `\b` while defeating it is still caught. The four names that become unknown are named in the changelog, because rendering an em dash is the visible change for whoever relied on the wrong number ([#831](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/831), [#1081](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1081))

- **Sharvin** ([@Guts1005](https://github.com/Guts1005))
  - First contribution, and it fixes a command that did not work at all on Windows: `soup infer` wrapped a Hub repo id in `pathlib.Path`, whose Windows string form turns `org/model` into `org\model`, so every Hub id was rejected with "Repo id must use alphanumeric chars". The id now stays the exact string the user typed and only a local directory becomes a path; `soup bench` went down the same route and is fixed with it. Reproduced both ways on Windows, and 8/8 mutations killed — including the `is_local` guard that survived the first round and is now pinned ([#1097](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1097), [#1118](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1118))
  - Pinned `trainer/pretrain.py`'s LoRA target resolver, whose deletion had left the whole suite green, by driving pretrain and the remaining MoE text trainers through their real setup ([#1157](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1157), [#1160](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1160))
  - The `gpu` test marker asked whether torch was *built* with CUDA, so under `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=""` on a machine with a card every gpu-marked test ran and failed in CPU-only bitsandbytes instead of skipping. The fix is subtler than the issue first proposed: a test that initialises CUDA flips `device_count()` from 0 to 1 mid-session, so the count is now pinned once at conftest import, before any test can touch CUDA, and used only by the marker, leaving `cuda_available()` to the test bodies that pick a device with it. **Both directions are checkable on a CI runner with no GPU**: child processes fake `torch.cuda` before the conftest is imported, and the hook tests turn a skip into a failure, so reverting the fix line fails two tests on every cell instead of passing as two skips. Nine of nine mutations killed ([#1128](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1128), [#1134](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1134))
  - Gave the seventeen config fields #808 found accepted and read by nothing a voice at load: a non-default value now prints `<field> is accepted but read by nothing; v0.77 will refuse it`. **The comparison uses the validated value, so `'false'`, `'off'` and `''` stay silent**, and the loader cannot fall back to the weaker raw comparison. The deadline sits in one constant, and a `TestTheDeadline` test fails if the v0.77 refusal is forgotten or done early: #627's warn-then-refuse mechanism, applied to fields instead of keys ([#808](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/808), [#1106](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1106))
  - Brought the nine training-intelligence and convergence knobs that #808 had left in `soup train`'s undated "not enforced" note (`forgetting_eval_steps` / `_benchmark` / `_stop`, `checkpoint_eval_steps` / `_metric` / `_tasks` / `_keep_top`, `convergence_window`, `convergence_rel_tol`) under the dated warn-then-refuse rule of the first seventeen. Each is now reported once, with the v0.77 date, and the note keeps only what the loader does not cover. **The second review round closed every item raised, including two non-blocking tests that would have broken silently at the v0.77 switch.** 339 passed on the merged tree, and 203 shipped configs load under the error severity. All 10 mutations fail a test, including the two that survived the first round ([#808](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/808), [#1379](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1379))

On CUDA, fp32, bf16 and NF4 agree exactly with peft ([#839](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/839), [#1087](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1087))

  - First contribution: the MCP run log at `.soup/mcp-runs/<run_id>.log` was the one unguarded open left in `mcp_server/execution.py`, so a file symlink planted there redirected everything the spawned child wrote. It now opens through the existing `open_no_follow` helper (#820) rather than a new inline guard, and a refusal surfaces as the existing path-free `ExecutionError` before the child spawns. **They declared a deviation from their own plan, with the reason**: `0o666` rather than the `0o644` they had proposed, because that is what plain `open()` passes, so a new log keeps today's permissions. Both mutants that survived the first review round, dropping `O_APPEND` and narrowing the mode, now fail tests, and the test skips cleanly where symlinks cannot be created ([#1138](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1138), [#1150](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1150))

  - First contribution: The Web UI kept its bearer token in `sessionStorage`, readable by any script in the page. The sweep now shrinks to the rows it has over the full LR range, refuses below four points before the model loads, and blames the output path only for real path errors; the refusal is re-raised past the synthetic-curve fallback instead of being turned into a report; 13 of 14 mutations fail a test ([#1189](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1189), [#1244](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1244))

A `TrainingConfig` validator now refuses both at load, naming both fields, with the runtime GaLore check kept as the backstop. They parametrized the 36-test file over `sft`, `dpo` and `ppo`, then proved it by moving the refusal into an `sft`-only validator (10 failures), and rebased across two docs conflicts with the code unchanged. Re-run by the maintainer: 15 of 36 fail on `main`, 10 of 10 mutations killed, 1333 related tests pass ([#1210](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1210), [#1255](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1255))

On a loopback bind, `/?token=` now swaps it for an `HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict` session cookie holding a random id that the server keeps in memory only, then redirects to the same URL without the token. Every port of `127.0.0.1` is one site, so a cookie-authenticated state change must also name this exact server in `Origin`; 64 of 64 foreign-Origin requests to the 8 state-changing routes are refused. The constant-time comparisons and the redirect target are pinned by tests, and 19 of 21 mutations fail a test ([#1191](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1191), [#1474](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1474))

- **anying** ([@anyingiit](https://github.com/anyingiit))
  - First contribution: `--baseline registry://` kept each benchmark's oldest eval score while its provenance warning came from the newest row, so re-measuring a baseline silenced the warning and left the stale score in the gate: a candidate at 0.38 passed against 0.30 instead of regressing against the re-measured 0.45. The score and the provenance now come from the same newest row. Provenance is checked per benchmark, which caught a scorer-revision mismatch `main` missed entirely. Ties on `created_at` resolve by insertion order, and `soup eval gate` escapes the warning now that it carries benchmark names. All 8 mutations tried across two review rounds fail a test ([#1224](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1224), [#1260](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1260))
  - `soup data forge --judge-provider` turned every failed judge call into an empty-answer row that no `--uncertainty-threshold` could prune, then exited 0 under a green "synth complete" panel. Failed, malformed and empty replies are now counted and never become rows. A run with no usable row exits 1 and writes nothing, and a partial outage keeps the good rows under a yellow panel that names the provider, the endpoint and the first error with its cause. The failure modes were tested against a real local HTTP server for ollama and vllm. 25 of the 28 new tests fail on `main`, and every mutation of the core fix fails a test ([#1221](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1221), [#1261](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1261))

  - First contribution: `soup autopilot` turned on FlashAttention and Liger on every Ampere or newer GPU without checking that the packages were installed, so on a standard install `soup train` refused the config autopilot had just written, and the Liger refusal's install hint lost its `[liger]` extra to Rich markup.
