Verified: 16 passed in the new test file (14 failed on `main`), and all 10 mutations of the fix fail a test ([#1251](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1251), [#1259](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1259))
  - `src/soup_cli/utils/auto_quant.py` had outlived its purpose. `soup serve --auto-quant` has refused with exit 2 since #816, so the module survived only as a dormant import target for historical tests, with no end date. They deleted it on the release #1131 fixed, together with the six test classes that exercised only its helpers (41 tests), and left the refusal's regression test untouched. They also corrected the CONTRIBUTING.md module list and test-table rows that still described the deleted coverage. `import soup_cli.utils.auto_quant` now raises `ModuleNotFoundError`, and the refusal still exits 2 on every backend. Verified: 441 passed on the merged tree (482 on `main`, the difference exactly the removed tests), and all 5 mutations of the refusal and its imports fail a test ([#1131](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1131), [#1262](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1262))
  - `training.loraplus_lr_ratio` loaded on `backend: mlx` and trained plain LoRA at one learning rate with no warning anywhere, because the MLX optimizer takes no parameter groups. They refused it at config load in its own validator placed after the MLX task gate, so tasks MLX cannot run keep their own messages, and pinned that order with five tests that fail in all three wrong positions; 10 of 10 mutations killed, 133 configs re-loaded with exactly the 11 `task: sft` MLX configs changing ([#1324](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1324), [#1383](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1383))
  - `training.expand_layers` (LLaMA Pro) was accepted on every task, backend and modality although only the transformers text `sft` and `pretrain` trainers apply it, so a `task: dpo` or `backend: mlx` config trained the unexpanded model with no message. They refused it at config load in its own validator placed right after the #795 quantization resolver, where the existing order pins protect it, and took the review's test file, which reads the task list from the schema so a new task is covered without editing a list; 33 tests (30 fail on `main`), 12 of 12 mutations killed ([#1325](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1325), [#1390](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1390))
  - `training.freeze_trainable_layers` was accepted on its own while nothing read it, so a config written to train only the top layers trained the whole model (or every LoRA target) and printed no warning. They refused it at config load when `expand_layers` is unset, in the validator that already holds the reverse refusal, testing `is not None` so `0` is refused and the `null` of a dumped config still reloads; 5 new tests fail on `main`, 8 of 8 mutations killed ([#1396](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1396), [#1399](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1399))
  - `soup tokenizer train` saved a byte-level BPE with no `ByteLevel` decoder, no 256-byte initial alphabet and a hard-coded `unk_token`, so `decode(encode(x))` returned `Ġ` for spaces, unseen accents, emoji and CJK became `<unk>`, and `--special-token` without `<unk>` made encoding raise. They fixed all three, and measured that the vocabulary floor is `256 + special tokens + 1` (anything lower silently yields a vocabulary with zero merges) instead of trusting the number in the ticket. In review they pinned the two survivors, the saved `unk_token` and the exact boundary, with a parametrised test on both sides of the floor; 9 tests, 7 failing on `main`, and 8 of 8 mutations killed ([#1429](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1429), [#1455](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1455))
  - `soup expect` passed datasets it had not checked: a UTF-8 BOM hid the first row from the PII check, malformed and non-object JSONL lines were skipped or dropped silently, and an empty file passed with `Rows 0`. They read the file as `utf-8-sig` and refused unreadable lines and zero-row runs as an input error (exit 3, count plus the first ten line numbers), kept distinct from a real suite failure (exit 2), and updated the two docs sentences that described the old behaviour. In review they tightened two `in (2, 3)` asserts to `== 3` and pinned the count and the ten-line cap; 9 tests, all failing on `main`, and 12 of 12 mutations killed ([#1434](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1434), [#1457](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1457))
  - `soup compile`, quantized GGUF export and `task: unlearn` all checked their destination only after the expensive step and then discarded the result: the compile optimizer ran its LLM calls before refusing `-o ../out.py` (exit 1, no clean refusal), a quantized export into a missing directory crashed in `mkdtemp` after the adapter merge and the cleanup deleted the merged model, and unlearn trained to the end before refusing an output outside the working directory or a symlink. They moved each check in front of the work (plan-time compile check with the write-time one kept as the TOCTOU guard and its `ValueError` turned into exit 2, the export parent created before the merge, `setup()` refusing first), pinned the unlearn ordering with an import sentinel that fails when the call is removed, and in review made both export tests hermetic with the missing-parent precondition asserted after two stray `model.gguf` files had been committed and had hidden the fix from CI; 9 tests (6 fail on `main`, 3 are in-cwd controls), 4 of 4 mutations killed ([#1445](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1445), [#1469](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1469))
  - KTO reached TRL with a per-device batch of 1 by three routes, and TRL refuses that outright ("Actual (not effective) batch size must be > 1"): a resident `task: kto` config at `batch_size: 1` loaded and loaded its model before dying (the parse-time refusal covered only `stream_layers`), `batch_size: auto` floored its halved estimate at 1, and `soup local-rl train --train-method kto` hard-wrote `batch_size: 1`. They refused it at config load for every KTO config, floored `auto` at 2, and changed the local-rl default; the `auto` test runs a real `KTOTrainerWrapper.setup()` on a tiny CPU model with the estimator patched to 1, 2 and 3, and the local-rl test builds a real `KTOTrainer` from the rendered YAML. Over four review rounds they rewrote the refusal message to name the KL term and the fix (it had told resident users about "a streaming run"), put the MLX refusal back in front of the batch message, documented the rule in two pages, and removed two comments that named a release the change had not shipped in; 5 new tests fail on `main`, 9 of 10 mutations killed ([#1420](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1420), [#1476](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1476))

A config written out in full by `soup autopilot` also reported 15 rows where the same minimal config reported none.   - First contribution: `soup registry diff`, `soup eval compare` and `soup eval leaderboard` kept the oldest eval score per benchmark. They overwrote rows in the tracker's newest-first order, so a re-measurement was ignored: on the issue's example both commands reported +0.20 and no regression, where comparing the newest scores gives a -0.05 regression. **They found that the leaderboard had the same bug, which the issue did not name**, and routed all three commands and the eval gate's `registry://` baseline through one helper that picks the newest `created_at` and then the latest insert. Their tests list rows newest first, as the tracker returns them, so restoring the old code at any call site fails a test. The second round added a two-model leaderboard test for the one mutation that had survived, and skips empty benchmark names as #1260 did. 13 of 15 mutations fail a test, and the other two would need a NULL value that the schema forbids ([#1270](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1270), [#1291](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1291))
  - The test-count badge step in `ci.yml` ran on a fork's own `main`, where the upstream `GIST_TOKEN` secret does not exist, so the Gist API answered `401 Bad credentials` and an otherwise green test job failed. They reported the failing run, and when the maintainer pointed out that it ran on their own fork and that their first idea (`continue-on-error: true`) would have hidden a genuinely expired upstream token, **they shipped the fix as a single AND-ed `github.repository == 'MakazhanAlpamys/Soup'` term on one line, with the reasoning written into the PR**. When review showed their first test only checked that the guard's text appeared somewhere in the step, so an `||` or a comment would still pass, they replaced it in one push with a test that parses the workflow YAML and checks the guard is an AND-ed term of the step's `if:`, and dropped the changelog fragment a CI-only change does not need. All 8 mutations of the step fail a test, and it fails on `main` ([#1414](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1414), [#1458](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1458))
  - Second merge, and the same overwrite-in-the-wrong-order mistake as their first, one command over. `soup card` built its scorecard by walking the tracker's newest-first eval rows and overwriting each benchmark's entry, so the last row it saw, the oldest, won: two rows for one benchmark (0.30, then 0.45) rendered 0.300. They reused the helper their own #1291 added instead of writing a second one, rewrote the old "last wins" test against the tracker's real order, and stated in the PR body that the row cap now applies after the newest row per benchmark is picked. **When review showed that neither the cap order nor the tie-break was pinned, they added the fragment and five tests in one push, including one through a real `ExperimentTracker`.** The tests fail 4 on `main`, and 6 of 7 mutations of the change and the helper fail a test, the seventh being a helper-level gap outside this PR ([#1315](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1315), [#1459](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1459))

  - First contribution: A local `.parquet` dataset with list columns loaded 0 training rows for eight formats, because pandas handed every list cell to the converters as a numpy array, and `soup data validate` / `inspect` crashed on the same file. The loader now reads parquet with pyarrow, so rows match the same data in JSONL, and it drops pandas' hidden `__index_level_N__` index column while keeping a named index. The new tests fail 15 of 24 on `main`, and all 5 mutations of the fix fail a test ([#1218](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1218), [#1292](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1292))

They moved it to `soup train` itself, after the save, so it evaluates the adapter that run produced, on the main process only, with `trust_remote_code` off, and a failure does not fail the run. They also routed the 16 trainers through one callback builder, guarded by an AST scan. Verified with a real CPU `soup train`, 348 tests across 10 related files, and 6 of 8 mutations killed ([#1487](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1487), [#1546](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1546))

They routed both checks through the file's own `_plain(...)`, which collapses whitespace as well as stripping ANSI. They added one shared resolver that pins the model to the requested device on both the full-model and the adapter branch of all three commands, and, after review, made an unknown or mis-cased `--device` fail with a clear message, stated the CPU float32 RAM cost in the changelog and docs, and, when the maintainer decided that GPUs must keep float16, removed the bfloat16 pick and pinned the dtype with an exact test that forces `cuda_supports_bf16()` to True. They routed all three loads through #1460's `resolve_inference_device_map_and_dtype`, validated `--device` early with exit 2 on every backend, kept an explicit `--kv-cache-type` winning over the helper's dtype, and updated the help and the fragment, including that a CPU-only host now loads float32 (twice the RAM). - **sup3dev** ([@sup3dev](https://github.com/sup3dev))
  - First contribution: `soup` reported any error whose message contained the digits `401` or `403` as "Authentication failed." / "Access denied." and hid the real message. That covered a too-long SFT row 401, a tensor size of 4013, a `checkpoint-4010` path and a hash in a Hub URL. The status is now read from the exception object along its `__cause__`/`__context__` chain, with a text fallback limited to explicit HTTP phrasing, so genuine requests, urllib and huggingface_hub 401/403 errors get the specific hint instead of "HTTP request failed.". **The detail that decided a three-way race for the issue**: `GatedRepoError` and `RepositoryNotFoundError` are declined anywhere in the chain. A missing local adapter directory that peft first looks up on the Hub, which answers an unauthenticated user with 401, therefore keeps its real message; both competing fixes reported "Authentication failed." there. 16 of the 31 new tests fail on `main`, and 21 of 22 mutations fail a test ([#1267](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1267), [#1268](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1268))

  - First contribution: `soup eval behavior --base-model` scored each live generation by whether it contained the probe's oracle label word, so an unchanged healthy model read MAJOR and a real refusal regression read delta 0.00. Generations are now classified as refusals or answers against a frozen table, `--evidence` keeps word matching, and `elephant` / `syceval`, whose `disagree` oracle a refusal classifier cannot judge, are refused before any model loads with exit 3. **They also taught the refusal classifier the contracted `I'm unable` / `I'm not able`, and when review showed that this moves the `mini_safety` and `mini_over_refusal` gate suites too, they bumped the scorer revision with contracted forms in the fingerprint corpus, so an older `--baseline` warns and a revert is caught.** On `main` 24 of the new file's 25 tests fail; 5 of 6 mutations in the last round fail a test, and the sixth (the revision number set back while the new fingerprint stays) is a property of the repository's revision test, not of this change ([#1228](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1228), [#1335](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1335))
  - Second merge. Four places read model output with a pattern that did not match the format they asked for: the LLM judge's one-level-nesting regex scored every criterion at the minimum whenever the reasoning quoted a nested object (and a missing or differently cased criterion became the minimum too), `\b...\b` could never match a keyword such as `-5`, `(B)` or `C++` so `soup eval checklist` reported MAJOR on a correct answer, and the recipe judge kept `NOT OK` because it contains the letters `OK`. They parse the structure instead (`raw_decode` over each `{`, one shared `(?<!\w)...(?!\w)` matcher for both keyword readers, a first-word verdict) and added two refusals nobody asked for, a 65,536-character reply cap and an ambiguous-spelling check. **The second round is the one to read**: each review finding became a test that fails under its mutation, and the five mutations that survived round 1 now all fail (right-hand edge 4 cases, `scores` preference 1, rubric-name folding 1, non-object `scores` 4, one-word default prompt 1); the deep-nesting test was rebuilt so it stays under their own size cap. 63 tests, 48 of them fail on clean `main`; 37 mutations, 34 killed ([#1467](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1467), [#1472](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1472))
  - Third merge. Aligned ULD (`uld_strategy: wasserstein_aligned`, which `soup draft distill` selects for every cross-tokenizer pair) gave each student position with no aligned teacher token (a byte-fallback piece, an empty piece, a student-only special token) an all-zero teacher row, which softmaxes to uniform, and kept it in the loss: each such position scored about V/2 (7999.4 at a 32k vocabulary) against near 0 for aligned positions, and the gradient pushed the student toward maximum entropy there. They counted a position only when its alignment names an existing teacher position, in the sum and in the denominator, under the label mask, the attention mask and no mask alike (a sequence with nothing aligned gives a finite zero), and made the trainer decode student special tokens to an empty string as the teacher text already does. 15 tests (11 fail on main; the 4 that pass are controls), including one that runs the real `_DistillTrainer.compute_loss`, and a six-row mutation table that reproduces; ten mutations in review all died, and old-vs-new on fully aligned input was bit-identical in 800 comparisons ([#1426](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1426), [#1541](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1541))

After an echoed option list, `\boxed{B.}` and `\boxed{**B**}` scored as C in the eval gate and in `soup ship`, while the reward parser read B. The letter tier now reads boxes through one shared, linear scan in `utils/final_answer.py`, which yields only innermost boxes, so nested boxes stay linear and a double-boxed answer still reads its inner value. Both commands now go through one `build_trainer`, which refuses an unknown task instead of falling back to SFT, and the parity test reads its task list off the schema's `task` Literal, so a new task cannot skip it. **The debugging worth copying**: three unrelated-looking failures turned out to be a recorder leaking into `TTSTrainerWrapper`'s base class at first import, and they fixed the fixture rather than the test order. The review round came back complete: tests that pin the kwargs each command forwards, and a failed arm's error printed without Rich eating the `[mlx]` in its install hint. The fallback now reads every exception in the chain, outermost first, and they wrote down why pattern-major order would be wrong: the 401 patterns come first in the table and would beat an outer 403. **Review found two things the chain walk changed, and both came back with tests**: the wrapped-urllib test from #1268 no longer pinned the `.code` probe, because the walk matches urllib's own `HTTP Error 403` text, so a urllib error whose message states no status now pins it again, and an exception whose `__str__` raises would crash the formatter, so a `_safe_str` guard skips it. - **qwist1233-cpu** ([@qwist1233-cpu](https://github.com/qwist1233-cpu))
  - First contribution: `soup ab` accepted `--alpha` + `--beta` at or above 1. There the reject and accept boundaries cross and every LLR between them rejects, so two identical arms were reported as a significant regression. A huge `--effect-size`, or a near-constant column at the default, ended on a bare `OverflowError` that named no flag. They refuse the pair in `MsprtConfig`, naming both values, and bound the standardised effect at sqrt(float max) before it is squared. The bound is exact: sqrt(max) squared is finite, and the next float up overflows. It also closes a silent-NaN `continue` at `--effect-size 1e308` that the issue never listed. Their 15 tests include 5 deliberate controls that pass on main, 10 fail on main, and six mutations of the fix were each caught ([#1339](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1339), [#1378](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1378))

They added two tests, one checking that an unpacked run carries the mask on both splits with the right content, and one driving trl's real `_prepare_dataset` and the trainer's own collator. **Review found that an all-zero mask still passed both, because the expected count came from the dataloader, which applies `assistant_masks` itself, so 0 == 0; they took the expected positions from the `labels` column instead and re-ran the row the next morning.** 13 of 13 mutations (10 in `trainer/sft.py`, 3 in a scratch copy of trl) now fail the new file, against 7 of 13 for the #1236 file alone ([#1351](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1351), [#1376](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1376))

They claimed that one item on the issue and touched nothing else, changed the gate to `run_probe` (one line), and wrote the tests with the probe helpers monkeypatched so they count the calls as well as checking the table. **Review found that nothing pinned plain `--probe`: rewriting the gate to `probe_model is not None` kept all 71 tests green and would have hidden the table on the path the old gate served. They added the test the next morning, and that mutation now fails it.** The new file is 1 failed / 3 passed on `main`, 4 of 4 mutations of the gate are caught, and 228 related tests pass with `main` merged ([#1448](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1448), [#1521](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1521))
  - Fourth merge. `net_guard.parse_ip_literal` fell back to the C library's `inet_aton`, so the same host spelling was read differently per platform: Windows refused `0xffffffff`, `4294967295` and `0377.0377.0377.0377` where glibc reads `255.255.255.255`, and IPv6 forms that embed an IPv4 address were classified only by the interpreter's `ipaddress` tables. They wrote a pure-Python reader of the BSD grammar, unwrapped 6to4, NAT64, Teredo and IPv4-mapped addresses by hand so that the inner address is classified as well, and made `refuse_private_ip_literal` parse once. **The fixture that makes the interpreter's tables say "public" comes with a guard test proving that it does, so the embedded-address tests cannot pass by accident. In the second round they put a per-base digit check in front of `int()`, so a radix prefix is read once, restored the C rule that the text ends at the first whitespace character, and moved `"1 "` to the table it then belonged in instead of deleting the row.** 108 tests (80 fail on `main`), 2758 passed across the 38 related test files, 11 of 13 mutations fail the new file and the two that pass are accounted for ([#1550](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1550), [#1562](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1562))

The prompt now asks for `#### <number>` on its own line for `math` and a final `Answer: <answer>` line for `logic` and `code`, and the new tests pin the two fixture copies as byte-identical and every row as a numeric gold. **They re-scoped the PR the moment #1371 made their fixture edit unnecessary and said so in the description, and when review found that nothing read a gold written the way the new prompt asks, they added that test and ran the marker mutation themselves, reporting 11 failed / 4 passed, which reproduces exactly.** On `main` 3 of the 15 tests fail (the template-marker ones), and 9 of 9 mutations of the template text, both fixture copies, the variable-prefix rule, the `####` marker and the answer-phrase matcher fail a test in the file ([#1349](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1349), [#1471](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1471))
  - Third merge. Custom-eval scoring was wrong in both directions: `contains` was a raw substring test, so an expected `4` was paid by `14` and `42` by `#### 420`, and `exact` scored `The answer is 4.` as a miss. They made `contains` token-bounded and added `scoring: "answer"`, which compares the answer a model states through the shared GRPO parser and refuses, at load time and in the eval gate, an `expected` that states no single answer, naming its line. **The second round is the part worth copying**: my review found that removing `re.escape` left all 24 tests green and that a stamped baseline recorded under the old `contains` (1.0) read back at 0.5 with a failed gate and no warning, because the baseline stamp covers only the bundled suites. They took the test verbatim (3 of 6 cases fail without the escape), put the score-shift warning in the changelog and the docs, and documented the limits that remained (the `.` boundary, ASCII-only bounds, `answer` needing a stated answer). 30 new tests (15 fail on `main`); 17 of 18 mutations caught; 433 related tests passed ([#1342](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1342), [#1490](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1490))
  - Fourth merge. `soup reward synth`'s numeric verifier carried its own regex copy of the answer parser, so `1,000`, `#### 1,000`, `\boxed {42}` and `#### −42` scored 0 under the generated file and a good reference counted as rejected with no name. Both sides now go through `parse_reference` / `parse_completion` (the built-ins' parser), the compare at tolerance 0 is `Decimal` equality so large integers stay exact, and the report names every rejected reference while a refused run prints the first ten and `+N more`. **Worth copying is how three rounds were taken**: review found three mutations that survived (the refusal panel's naming, and reading the gold or the completion with the wrong parser) and they put the three tests in verbatim; the next push also took the unrequested follow-ups (`variables_conflict` in the emitted body, the capped listing) and then tripped the repo's ANSI ratchet, which they fixed with `strip_ansi` and a precise "non-dollar currency" wording. 27 new tests (7 failed, 6 passed, 14 errors on `main`); 22 of 22 mutations killed; 4,000 generated pairs agree with `math_verify_reward` ([#1350](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1350), [#1533](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1533))

- **Maxi** ([@maximilianfeix](https://github.com/maximilianfeix))
  - AutoDistill's vocabulary guards and the weight shards' `retain_bytes=False` were pinned by no test. Disabling both guards and turning full-RAM hashing back on left all 41 autodistill tests passing. Tests now cover the capture guard for both bounds, the worker's own guard before the teacher loads (on a dataset that still matches its fingerprint), and a shard hash that retains no bytes. **They noticed the `-1` cases still passed with the capture guard off, because `CaptureToken` raises the same phrase, and said so in the PR instead of shipping them as coverage. Anchoring the matches on the full message closed the gap on the next push.** Every mutation fails the new tests: whole guard 4, lower bound 2, context and prompt bypasses 2 each, worker guard 1, teacher loaded first 4, each retention mutation 1 ([#1341](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1341), [#1400](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1400))
  - First contribution: three pieces of stale text from #1353. The streaming docs said DPO's streamed reference "costs no extra weights", but since #1061 an untied checkpoint under `dpo` or `kto` keeps a private copy of the output head for its backward every step. That is about 1.05 GB for Llama-3.1-8B, and the pre-flight already charged it. The docs now state that cost in four places and say the untied bit-exact check covers `dpo` only. The `citation_faithful` refusal said its runtime "ships in v0.62.1" when it had been live since v0.71.10, and the `unlearn`-on-MLX refusal said "deferred to v0.61.1". Eleven comments and docstrings in `schema.py` and `ra_dit.py` also still pointed at past releases. All of them now describe current behaviour, each checked against the code. **They noticed that the stale refusal was an f-string concatenation, which a scan of plain string constants would miss. They extended the #823 guard to every `raise` message in `schema.py`, with f-strings read, and pinned that reading with its own test.** On `main` the guard fails on exactly that refusal, and both mutations fail it (1 and 2 failures) ([#1353](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1353), [#1402](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1402))
  - First contribution: three benchmark harnesses each carried a copy of the helper that records which tree a row measured. Every copy credited a `soup-cli` wheel installed into a gitignored `.venv/` inside the checkout with the checkout's clean commit. One shared `source_sha()` now refuses a `soup_cli/__init__.py` the checkout does not track. `head_prefetch_ab` keeps its narrower `src/` dirty scope through a parameter, so the other harnesses do not inherit it. **They tested it with real `git` against a throwaway checkout rather than a faked `subprocess.run`, which is the only way a swallowed `ls-files` failure gets caught.** The tests skip cleanly without git, ignore a global `commit.gpgsign`, and catch all 8 mutations tried (82 passed) ([#1387](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1387), [#1403](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1403))
  - `training.moe_aux_loss_coeff` is applied only by SFT's text setup, yet a non-default value loaded on `task: sft` with `modality: vision` or `audio` and was never read. They refuse it at config load, beside #798's check for the same field, naming the modality and pointing to `modality: text` as #1179 does for `moe_lora`. The default `0.01` still loads everywhere, and `audio_out`, which SFT sends to the text setup, is pinned as allowed. 20 related test files passed with main merged (2675 passed, 0 failed), and 8 of 9 mutations of the fix fail a test ([#1394](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1394), [#1404](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1404))
  - `task: ppo` could not complete a single step on trl 0.29. The dataset handed to trl's `PPOTrainer` still carried the `prompt_text` strings, and trl's padding collator raised on them at the first batch, after three models had loaded. A train set smaller than one rollout batch spun forever at 100% CPU instead. trl now gets only `input_ids` / `attention_mask` at all three hand-offs, and a train set with fewer than `batch_size` x `gradient_accumulation_steps` x the number of processes rows is refused before any model loads, naming the numbers. **They wrote the suite's first test that runs a real trl PPO update, on CPU with nothing mocked, and it fails on `main` with the issue's exact error. When review asked for the refusal to move ahead of the model loads, they applied it and also pinned the two trl hand-offs no test covered.** 191 tests in the six PPO files pass with `main` merged, and 11 of 11 mutations of the fix fail a test ([#1391](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1391), [#1410](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1410))
  - `--fsdp` was accepted and silently dropped on `task: prm` and `task: moe_lora_routing`, and `--fsdp` and `--deepspeed` on `task: online_dpo`: the wrappers stored `fsdp_config` / `deepspeed_config` and never handed them to their trainer arguments. The fix spreads `**(self.fsdp_config or {})` into the `TrainingArguments` of `prm.py` and `mole_routing.py` and forwards both to `OnlineDPOConfig`, exactly as `dpo.py` does. The four new tests build each real wrapper over a tiny Llama and record the arguments-class kwargs, so no FSDP or DeepSpeed runtime is needed; 3 of 4 fail on `main`, the "neither set" control passes on both, and all 6 mutations of the fix fail a test ([#1204](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1204), [#1417](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1417))

- **Urooj Fatima Mukhtar** ([@UroojFatima-052](https://github.com/UroojFatima-052))
  - First contribution: ten real-`train()` tests were still skipped on every windows-latest cell under #382, although #1314 had traced the crash to CPU bf16/fp16 matmuls and the skip helper's docstring still blamed bitsandbytes for all of them. Nine now request `aten_half_matmuls` and the tenth, an fp32 run, just loses its skip. **The part worth copying is the guard that keeps the list honest**: a `FIXTURE_TESTS` check fails if any of the nine stops requesting the fixture, and, after review, if one both requests it and carries the skip again. `GUARDED_TESTS` shrinks to the two NF4 tests that really are the bitsandbytes crash, with the reason written next to the list.   - Second merge, and it is the one that claimed the work and then did what it said. #1490's whole-token `contains` scorer had become quadratic in `len(output) * len(expected)` on repetitive token-separated text (a 400,000-character output against a 200,000-character `expected` took 68 s; v0.75.1's substring test took 0 s) because its pattern began with a lookbehind and ran under `re.IGNORECASE`, which keeps `re` off its literal-prefix search. They posted the plan before writing code (literal first, a fixed-width lookbehind after it, both sides lowercased once, `İstanbul` and `ſ` staying `False` as in v0.75.1) and the pull request is that plan. The tests mirror the acceptance list: a growth test on four shapes in the `test_issue1346_rewrites_are_linear.py` style, a 20,000-case differential against a copy of #1490's regex over the issue's own alphabet, the two Unicode pins documented in the `contains` row, and a gold token at character 60,000 of a 60,016-character output so a reintroduced slice fails. Review ran 16 mutations of the scorer (either boundary, the lowercasing, `re.IGNORECASE` back in, the lookbehind width off by one both ways, a 50,000-character slice, the literal last again, no `re.escape`, no `strip`) and all 16 fail the new tests; 4 of the 8 new tests fail on `main`, and a 320,000-case differential against an explicit overlapping scan agrees with 0 mismatches ([#1528](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1528), [#1540](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1540))

  - First contribution: `http://[::1]:<port>` was accepted by the judge API-base and vLLM checks but refused by eval-gate suites, `soup ship --judge-model` and the online-DPO trainer, because three gates kept their own `("localhost", "127.0.0.1")` literal and two more kept a third copy. They made all five import `net_guard.LOOPBACK_HOSTS` and added an AST ratchet that fails on any rebuilt loopback literal, including single-quoted and multi-line spellings. Their 27 tests: 11 fail on main, 27 pass here; 7 mutations, all killed ([#1548](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1548), [#1554](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1554))

- **Pranav Deshpande** ([@PranavD2905](https://github.com/PranavD2905))
  - First contribution: the refusal for a non-public IP literal in an outbound endpoint named no remedy, and `http://0.0.0.0` in `soup data generate --api-base` and the Web UI chat proxy got only the generic HTTPS text while three other validators already said "0.0.0.0 is ambiguous; use 127.0.0.1 or localhost". They claimed the issue first, appended "address the server by its hostname" to the one shared message, moved the 0.0.0.0 wording into a single `net_guard` constant that all five checks now use, and kept every existing test case, changing only the four message assertions. **Their description said exactly what does not change, and it held**: 185 host spellings through 21 checks gave 0 differences in what is accepted or refused against main. 601 tests pass (40 fail on main); 14 mutations, all killed ([#1549](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1549), [#1582](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1582))

- **Yang Bai** ([@ybai08](https://github.com/ybai08))
  - First contribution: a judge that was down still cost 3 s of backoff for every row, because each row retried on its own: 1,000 rows slept 3,000 s against a host that never answered, and `soup data from-traces --judge` then wrote an empty file with exit 0. They added a per-evaluator count of requests whose retries were spent, a `JudgeDownError` at the third, and a stop in each caller that names the judge URL and how much was left; a 4xx or an unusable reply counts as an answer and resets it. **The part worth copying is how they handled what they could not prove**: they measured that `ship` and `best-of-n` already stopped on `main` and said a counter mutation cannot fail those tests, flagged their own 60-second cooldown as the reviewer's to overrule, and when review showed it turning a 9-second outage into 48 unranked pairs in a row, removed it and rewrote the tests the way the real callers behave. 19 tests (8 fail with the counter reverted), 2,584 passing across 40 judge-related files, 27 of 29 mutations killed ([#1522](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1522), [#1563](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1563))
  - First contribution: a hand-written `use_dora: true` on a GPTQ, AWQ, AQLM or EETQ base loaded and then failed inside peft when the adapter was attached, after the checkpoint download and the model load. They claimed the item on the issue first, then added a `SoupConfig` validator that refuses the pairing at config load with a message naming both settings and the fix, and moved the four formats into one set that Autopilot's `decide_peft` now reads too, so the schema and Autopilot cannot drift apart. **The part worth copying is the controls**: every test uses the same GPTQ-named base and flips only `quantization`, so a refusal keyed on the model name would have failed their own tests. 17 tests (5 fail on `main`), 11 of 12 mutations killed, and across 14,904 config loads nothing is newly accepted and the 404 new refusals are all `use_dora: true` with one of the four formats ([#1466](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1466), [#1555](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1555))
  - `training.quantization: mxfp4` passed config validation and then failed at the model load, because Soup built a bitsandbytes config for a format that belongs to transformers' own `Mxfp4Config`; the MXFP4 base Autopilot passes through failed the same way. They reproduced both on `main` and posted what transformers' quantizer says before writing code, then mapped `mxfp4` to `Mxfp4Config(dequantize=True)`, took it out of the k-bit prep path of all 15 trainer sites (it would cast the dequantized model to fp32), refused a local base that is not MXFP4 and the bitsandbytes-only `bnb_4bit_quant_storage`, and priced the base as the bf16 model it loads. **The part worth copying is the "not run" list**: no CUDA, no real MXFP4 weights, no training run, each with what it leaves unproven. 13 new tests (all fail on `main`, 26 across their test files), 26 of 26 mutations killed ([#1466](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1466), [#1558](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1558))
  - First contribution: disk-tier layer streaming turned direct I/O off for the whole store when torch's caching host allocator handed back a pinned staging arena off a 4096-byte boundary, and said so only in a log line. They aligned the placements inside the arena or took the next power of two, measured the shift again on the replacement, made `pinned_bytes` count the arenas actually held, and put the remaining page-cache fallback on the console. 12 CPU tests with a forced 512-byte offset (4 fail and 5 error on main), 12 of 12 mutations killed ([#1531](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1531), [#1621](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1621))

- **Nariman Abdikarim** ([@Nari-Ab](https://github.com/Nari-Ab))
  - First contribution: `soup data generate --template preference` appended every preference row before the dedup check, so rows repeating a `--dedup-with` file and repeats within the same run were all written. They claimed that one item of the #1448 audit, routed preference rows through the shared dedup check, and built an order-independent key from the DPO and KTO fields. **Review found that nothing pinned which fields make up the key: a prompt-only key passed every test and would have dropped every later row for the same prompt. They added the proposed test and the exact duplicate count within minutes.** The new file is 4 failed on `main`, 11 of 11 mutations are caught, and 507 related tests pass ([#1448](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1448), [#1605](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1605))
  - First contribution, one slice of the #1448 audit: `soup serve` dropped `--structured-output` / `--json-schema` / `--regex-pattern` and `--reasoning-parser` on streaming chat completions, so `stream: true` returned unconstrained text with the `<think>` block still in it while the same request without streaming was constrained and stripped. They claimed that one item, wired the processors, the n-gram config and the reasoning parser into the streaming path exactly as the non-streaming handler does, and stripped the text before both the SSE chunks and the trace log. **Review found that the first constraint test mocked the builder, so building for `None` (the reported bug) still passed; they added the parity test the review supplied, which drives the same app with and without `stream` and compares what the client sees and what generation receives.** 5 tests fail on `main`, 11 of 11 mutations of the constraint, n-gram and parser hand-offs are caught, and 1231 serve-related tests pass with `main` merged ([#1448](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1448), [#1607](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1607))
  - Fourth merge: a bad `SOUP_LAYER_STREAM_STRIPE_DIRS` entry was refused only when the layer-stream setup ran, after the dataset had loaded and a run row existed, and `soup train --dry-run` and `soup doctor` never checked the variable. They split the checks that need no disk probe into one helper shared by the setup path and doctor, so the setup stays the authority and the NVMe probe stays where it was; `soup train` and `--dry-run` now refuse before the prompt and the dataset load, and `soup doctor` lists each entry, sanitised, records an advisory issue for a refused one and applies the NVMe rule under `--disk`. Every gap four review rounds found became a test: 20 pass, the file cannot import on `main`, and 15 of 15 mutations of the new code fail one ([#1613](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1613), [#1628](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1628))

- **John Coplin** ([@johncoplin](https://github.com/johncoplin))
  - First contribution: the friendly-error formatter's `HTTP 401`/`HTTP 403` text patterns let two whitespace groups compete for the same run, so an exception message with `HTTP` followed by a long whitespace run and no status took about 13 s to print at 50,000 spaces. Since #1366, that check runs for every exception in the chain. They claimed the issue, rewrote both patterns so the optional whitespace is consumed only after a `:`/`=` separator, and added a timing test that fails on `main`. **The part worth copying is the equivalence test**: all 15 phrasings from the issue run against the old and the new pattern for both 401 and 403, so the speed-up provably changes nothing about what gets classified. 31 tests (1 fails on main), 7 of 7 mutations killed ([#1367](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/issues/1367), [#1594](https://github.com/MakazhanAlpamys/Soup/pull/1594))
